## XVI A Disputed Authorship / 十六、著作权之争

===Original===
“One moment,” said Mr. Lawrie, “might I ask what is the subject of the poem?”

“Death of Dido,” said the Headmaster. “Good, hackneyed, evergreen subject, mellow with years. Go on, Wells.”

Mr. Wells began.

> Queen of Tyre, ancient Tyre,

 

 Whilom mistress of the wave.

===Chinese===
「等一下，」劳里（Lawrie）先生说，「我能问问这首诗的主题是什么吗？」

「狄多之死，」校长说，「好题目，老掉牙却四季常青，岁月使它愈发醇厚。念吧，威尔斯。」

威尔斯先生念了起来。

> 推罗女王，古老的推罗，
> 昔年雄霸波涛的女主。

===Original===
Mr. Lawrie, who had sunk back into the recesses of his chair in an attitude of attentive repose, sat up suddenly with a start.

“What!” he cried.

“Hullo,” said Mr. Wells, “has the beauty of the work come home to you already?”

“You notice,” he said, as he repeated the couplet, “that flaws begin to appear in the gem right from the start. It was rash of Master Lorimer to attempt such a difficult metre. Plucky, but rash. He should have stuck to blank verse. Tyre, you notice, two syllables to rhyme with ‘deny her’ in line three. ‘What did fortune e’er deny her? Were not all her warriors brave?’ That last line seems to me distinctly weak. I don’t know how it strikes you.”

===Chinese===
劳里先生原本带着专注而安详的神态深深陷进椅子里，此刻猛地一惊，坐直了身子。

「什么！」他叫道。

「哟，」威尔斯先生说，「作品的妙处这就打进您心里去了？」

「您注意到没有，」他把那两行重念了一遍，说道，「这颗宝石打一开头就现出了裂纹。洛里默（Lorimer）少爷要驾驭这么难的格律，未免太莽撞。勇气可嘉，但确属莽撞。他还是老老实实写素体诗的好。您看，『推罗』（Tyre）两个音节，正好和第三行的『deny her（亏待她）』押韵。『命运何曾亏待过她？她的战士岂非个个英勇？』依我看，最后这行实在软弱无力。不知您觉得如何。」

===Original===
“You’re hypercritical, Wells,” said the Head. “Now, for a boy I consider that a very good beginning. What do you say, Lawrie?”

“I—er. Oh, I think I am hardly a judge.”

“To resume,” said Mr. Mortimer Wells. He resumed, and ran through the remaining verses of the poem with comments. When he had finished, he remarked that, in his opinion a whiff of fresh air would not hurt him. If the Headmaster would excuse him, he would select another of those excellent cigars, and smoke it out of doors.

“By all means,” said the Head; “I think I won’t join you myself, but perhaps Lawrie will.”

“No, thank you. I think I will remain. Yes, I think I will remain.”

Mr. Wells walked jauntily out of the room. When the door had shut, Mr. Lawrie coughed nervously.

===Chinese===
「你也太苛刻了，威尔斯，」校长说，「就一个孩子而言，我认为这个开头相当不错。你怎么看，劳里？」

「我——呃。哦，我想我算不上内行。」

「言归正传，」莫蒂默·威尔斯（Mortimer Wells）先生说。他言归正传，一边点评一边把余下的诗节从头到尾过了一遍。念完之后他表示，依个人浅见，出去透透气于他无损。若是校长不见怪，他想再取一支那种上好的雪茄，到门外去抽。

「尽管请便，」校长说，「我就不奉陪了，不过劳里没准儿愿意同去。」

「不，谢谢。我想我还是留下。对，我还是留下。」

威尔斯先生步履轻快地出了房门。门一关上，劳里先生便不安地咳了一声。

===Original===
“Another cigar, Lawrie?”

“I—er—no, thank you. I want to ask you a question. What is your candid opinion of those verses Mr. Wells was reading just now?”

The Headmaster laughed.

“I don’t think Wells treated them quite fairly. In my opinion they were distinctly promising. For a boy in the Upper Fifth, you understand. Yes, on the whole they showed distinct promise.”

“They were mine,” said Mr. Lawrie.

“Yours! I don’t understand. How were they yours?”

===Chinese===
「再来一支雪茄吗，劳里？」

「我——呃——不用了，谢谢。我想请教您一个问题。方才威尔斯先生念的那些诗行，您真正的看法如何？」

校长笑了。

「我看威尔斯待它们有失公允。依我之见，它们显出相当的潜力。当然是就一个高五班（Upper Fifth）的学生而言，你明白的。是啊，总体上确实显出可造之才。」

「那是我写的，」劳里先生说。

「你写的！我不明白了。怎么会是你写的？」

===Original===
“I wrote them. Every word of them.”

“You wrote them! But, my dear Lawrie—”

“I don’t wonder that you are surprised. For my own part I am amazed, simply amazed. How the boy—I don’t even remember his name—contrived to get hold of them, I have not the slightest conception. But that he did so contrive is certain. The poem is word for word, literally word for word, the same as one which I wrote when I was at Cambridge.”

“You don’t say so!”

“Yes. It can hardly be a coincidence.”

“Hardly,” said the Head. “Are you certain of this?”

===Chinese===
「是我写的。每个字都是我写的。」

「你写的！可是，亲爱的劳里——」

「你不吃惊才怪呢。连我自己都惊呆了，简直是惊呆了。那孩子——我连他名字都记不得——究竟是怎么弄到手的，我毫无头绪。但他确实弄到了手，这一点确凿无疑。那首诗一字不差——真的，一字不差——与我当年在剑桥写的一首完全相同。」

「竟有这种事！」

「是啊。这总不会是巧合。」

「恐怕不是，」校长说，「你对此有把握吗？」

===Original===
“Perfectly certain. I am not eager to claim the authorship, I can assure you, especially after Mr. Wells’s very outspoken criticisms, but there is nothing else to be done. The poem appeared more than a dozen years ago, in a small book called *The Dark Horse*.”

“Ah! Something in the Whyte Melville style, I suppose?”

“No,” said Mr. Lawrie sharply. “No. Certainly not. They were serious poems, tragical most of them. I had them collected, and published them at my own expense. Very much at my own expense. I used a pseudonym, I am thankful to say. As far as I could ascertain, the total sale amounted to eight copies. I have never felt the very slightest inclination to repeat the performance. But how this boy managed to see the book is more than I can explain. He can hardly have bought it. The price was half-a-guinea. And there is certainly no copy in the School library. The thing is a mystery.”

===Chinese===
「十拿九稳。老实说，我并不急于认领这份著作权，尤其是在威尔斯先生那番直言不讳的批评之后，可事已至此，别无他法。那首诗十多年前发表过，收在一本叫《黑马》（The Dark Horse）的小书里。」

「啊！我猜是怀特·梅尔维尔（Whyte Melville）那一派的东西？」

「不，」劳里先生厉声道，「不是。绝对不是。那些是严肃的诗作，多数还是悲剧性的。我把它们结成集子，自费出版了。自费得非常彻底。所幸我用了笔名。据我所能查证的，总共卖出八本。打那以后，我再没起过丝毫重操旧业的念头。可这孩子究竟从哪儿见到这本书的，我就无从解释了。他总不见得是自己买的吧，定价可是半几尼。学校图书馆里也肯定没有藏本。这件事是个谜。」

===Original===
“A mystery that must be solved,” said the Headmaster. “The fact remains that he did see the book, and it is very serious. Wholesale plagiarism of this description should be kept for the School magazine. It should not be allowed to spread to poetry prizes. I must see Lorimer about this tomorrow. Perhaps he can throw some light upon the matter.”

When, in the course of morning school next day, the School porter entered the Upper Fifth form-room and informed Mr. Sims, who was engaged in trying to drive the beauties of Plautus’ colloquial style into the Upper Fifth brain, that the Headmaster wished to see Lorimer, Lorimer’s conscience was so abnormally good that for the life of him he could not think why he had been sent for. As far as he could remember, there was no possible way in which the authorities could get at him. If he had been in the habit of smoking out of bounds in lonely fields and deserted barns, he might have felt uneasy. But whatever his failings, that was not one of them. It could not be anything about bounds, because he had been so busy with cricket that he had had no time to break them this term. He walked into the presence, glowing with conscious rectitude. And no sooner was he inside than the Headmaster, with three simple words, took every particle of starch out of his anatomy.

“Sit down, Lorimer,” he said.

There are many ways of inviting a person to seat himself. The genial “take a pew” of one’s equal inspires confidence. The raucous “sit down in front” of the frenzied pit, when you stand up to get a better view of the stage, is not so pleasant. But worst of all is the icy “sit down” of the annoyed headmaster. In his mouth the words take to themselves new and sinister meanings. They seem to accuse you of nameless crimes, and to warn you that anything you may say will be used against you as evidence.

“Why have I sent for you, Lorimer?”

===Chinese===
「一个必须解开的谜，」校长说，「事实摆在那儿：他的确见过这本书，这就非常严重了。这等整篇的抄袭，本该留给校刊去用，可不能任其蔓延到诗歌奖上来。明天我得找洛里默谈谈。兴许他能对这事给出些解释。」

第二天上午课进行到一半，学校的门房走进高五班教室，通知正埋头把普劳图斯（Plautus）口语风格的妙处往高五班脑袋里灌的西姆斯（Sims）先生，说校长要见洛里默。洛里默的良心干净得异乎寻常，打死他也想不出自己为何被召。就记忆所及，校方没有任何途径能抓到他的把柄。他若惯于在越界区的僻静田野和废弃谷仓里抽烟，兴许还会心里发毛。但他纵有千百种毛病，这一条却不在其列。也不可能是越界的事，因为这个学期他忙于板球，根本无暇越界。他走进校长室，浑身散发着问心无愧的光彩。可他人刚进门，校长只用了简简单单三个词，就把他全身上下每一丝挺括抽了个干净。

「坐下，洛里默。」他说。

请人落座的方式有很多种。平辈之间亲热的一声「随便坐」令人心安；戏院后排观众怒气冲冲地朝你嚷「前排的坐下」（你站起来想看清舞台时），就不那么愉快了；而最糟的，莫过于被惹恼的校长冷冰冰的一声「坐下」。这话到了他嘴里，便平添出种种不祥的新意味，仿佛指控你犯下了说不出口的罪行，又警告你接下来无论说什么，都将成为呈堂证供。

「我为什么叫你来，洛里默？」

===Original===
A nasty question that, and a very favourite one of the Rev. Mr. Beckett, Headmaster of Beckford. In nine cases out of ten, the person addressed, paralysed with nervousness, would give himself away upon the instant, and confess everything. Lorimer, however, was saved by the fact that he had nothing to confess. He stifled an inclination to reply “because the woodpecker would peck her,” or words to that effect, and maintained a pallid silence.

“Have you ever heard of a book called *The Dark Horse*, Lorimer?”

Lorimer began to feel that the conversation was too deep for him. After opening in the conventional “judge-then-placed-the-black-cap-on-his-head” manner, his assailant had suddenly begun to babble lightly of sporting literature. He began to entertain doubts of the Headmaster’s sanity. It would not have added greatly to his mystification if the Head had gone on to insist that he was the Emperor of Peru, and worked solely by electricity.

The Headmaster, for his part, was also surprised. He had worked for dismay, conscious guilt, confessions, and the like, instead of blank amazement. He, too, began to have his doubts. Had Mr. Lawrie been mistaken? It was not likely, but it was barely possible. In which case the interview had better be brought to an abrupt stop until he had made inquiries. The situation was at a deadlock.

Fortunately at this point half-past twelve struck, and the bell rang for the end of morning school. The situation was saved, and the tension relaxed.

===Chinese===
这问题够刁钻的，而且深得贝克福德学院（Beckford）校长贝克特牧师（the Rev. Mr. Beckett）的偏爱。十回里有九回，被问的人会紧张得瘫软，当场不打自招，把一切和盘托出。洛里默却因实在无可招供而得救。他强压住想答一句「因为啄木鸟要啄她呀」之类蠢话的冲动，脸色发白地保持着沉默。

「你听说过一本叫《黑马》的书吗，洛里默？」

洛里默开始觉得这场谈话深不可测。对方开场还是那套「法官宣判、戴上黑色小帽」的架势，忽然却轻飘飘地聊起赛马小说来了。他开始怀疑校长的神智是否正常。就算校长接着坚称自己是秘鲁皇帝、全靠电力开动，他的迷惑也不会再加深多少。

校长这边也颇感意外。他预期的是惊慌、心虚、招供之类，等来的却是一片茫然的错愕。他心里也犯起了嘀咕：莫非劳里先生记错了？不太可能，但并非全无可能。倘若如此，这场问话最好戛然而止，等他先查证一番再说。局面就此僵住。

幸而此时十二点半的钟声敲响，上午课结束的铃声大作。危机解除，紧张的气氛松弛了下来。

===Original===
“You may go, Lorimer,” said the Head, “I will send for you later.”

He swept out of the room, and Lorimer raced over to the House to inform Pringle that the Headmaster had run suddenly mad, and should by rights be equipped with a strait-waistcoat.

“You never saw such a man,” he said, “hauled me out of school in the middle of a Plautus lesson, dumps me down in a chair, and then asks me if I’ve read some weird sporting novel or other.”

“Sporting novel! My dear man!”

“Well, it sounded like it from the title.”

===Chinese===
「你可以走了，洛里默，」校长说，「回头我再叫你。」

他大步流星地出了房间。洛里默则一路飞奔回宿舍，向普林格尔（Pringle）报告：校长突然疯了，照理该给他套上拘束衣。

「你从没见过这样的人，」他说，「普劳图斯的课上到一半把我拎出教室，把我往椅子上一摁，然后问我读过没读过什么古怪的赛马小说。」

「赛马小说！我的老兄！」

「唔，听那书名像是那么回事。」

===Original===
“The title. Oh!”

“What’s up?”

Pringle had leaped to his feet as if he had suddenly discovered that he was sitting on something red-hot. His normal air of superior calm had vanished. He was breathless with excitement. A sudden idea had struck him with the force of a bullet.

“What was the title he asked you if you’d read the book of?” he demanded incoherently.

“The Derby Winner.”

Pringle sat down again, relieved.

===Chinese===
「书名。哦！」

「怎么啦？」

普林格尔霍地跳了起来，仿佛猛然发现自己坐在了烧红的烙铁上。他平日那种高人一等的从容荡然无存，兴奋得上气不接下气。一个念头猝然击中他，力道赛过子弹。

「他问你读没读过的那本书，书名是什么？」他语无伦次地质问道。

「《德比赢家》（The Derby Winner）。」

普林格尔重新坐下，松了口气。

===Original===
“Oh. Are you certain?”

“No, of course it wasn’t that. I was only ragging. The real title was *The Dark Horse*. Hullo, what’s up now? Have you got ’em too?”

“What’s up? I’ll tell you. We’re done for. Absolutely pipped. That’s what’s the matter.”

“Hang it, man, do give us a chance. Why can’t you explain, instead of sitting there talking like that? Why are we done? What have we done, anyway?”

“The poem, of course, the prize poem. I forgot, I never told you. I hadn’t time to write anything of my own, so I cribbed it straight out of a book called *The Dark Horse*. Now do you see?”

===Chinese===
「哦。你确定？」

「不，当然不是那本。我不过逗逗你。真名是《黑马》。喂，这又是怎么了？你也犯病了？」

「怎么了？我告诉你。咱们完了。彻底栽了。就是这么回事。」

「见鬼，哥们儿，也给人一个开口的机会行不行。你倒是解释啊，别坐在那儿净说这些。咱们怎么就完了？咱们到底干了什么？」

「当然是那首诗，参赛的那首诗。我忘了，一直没跟你说。我来不及自己写，就径直从一本叫《黑马》的书里抄了一首。现在明白了吧？」

===Original===
Lorimer saw. He grasped the whole unpainted beauty of the situation in a flash, and for some moments it rendered him totally unfit for intellectual conversation. When he did speak his observation was brief, but it teemed with condensed meaning. It was the conversational parallel to the ox in the teacup.

“My aunt!” he said.

“There’ll be a row about this,” said Pringle.

“What am I to say when he has me in this afternoon? He said he would.”

“Let the whole thing out. No good trying to hush it up. He may let us down easy if you’re honest about it.”

It relieved Lorimer to hear Pringle talk about “us.” It meant that he was not to be left to bear the assault alone. Which, considering that the whole trouble was, strictly speaking, Pringle’s fault, was only just.

===Chinese===
洛里默明白了。他一瞬间就看透了这局面赤裸裸的全部妙处，有好一阵子完全丧失了进行高级对话的能力。等到终于开口，他的评语极短，内涵却浓缩得满溢出来，堪称「茶杯里的公牛」的对话版。

「我的老天！」他说。

「这事要闹起来了，」普林格尔说。

「今天下午他叫我去的时候，我该说什么？他说了要叫我的。」

「全盘托出吧。捂是捂不住的。你要是老实交代，他兴许会从轻发落。」

听到普林格尔口口声声说「咱们」，洛里默松了口气。这意味着不至于让他独自挨那一顿雷霆。严格说来，整件祸事本是普林格尔捅出来的，如此分担才算公道。

===Original===
“But how am I to explain? I can’t reel off a long yarn all about how you did it all, and so on. It would be too low.”

“I know,” said Pringle, “I’ve got it. Look here, on your way to the Old Man’s room you pass the Remove door. Well, when you pass, drop some money. I’ll be certain to hear it, as I sit next the door. And then I’ll ask to leave the room, and we’ll go up together.”

“Good man, Pringle, you’re a genius. Thanks, awfully.”

But as it happened, this crafty scheme was not found necessary. The blow did not fall till after lockup.

===Chinese===
「可我怎么解释？我总不能滔滔不绝地讲一大篇你是如何一手操办之类的话。那也太下作了。」

「我知道，」普林格尔说，「我有主意了。听着，你去老头子房间的路上要经过低年级班（the Remove）的门口。等你经过时，掉几个钱在地上。我坐在门边，保准听得见。然后我就请假出去，咱们一块儿上去。」

「好样的，普林格尔，你是个天才。多谢，多谢。」

然而事有凑巧，这条锦囊妙计并未派上用场。那一击直到锁门熄灯之后才落下来。

===Original===
Lorimer being in the Headmaster’s House, it was possible to interview him without the fuss and advertisement inseparable from a “sending for during school.” Just as he was beginning his night-work, the butler came with a message that he was wanted in the Headmaster’s part of the House.

“It was only Mr. Lorimer as the master wished to see,” said the butler, as Pringle rose to accompany his companion in crime.

“That’s all right,” said Pringle, “the Headmaster’s always glad to see me. I’ve got a standing invitation. He’ll understand.”

At first, when he saw two where he had only sent for one, the Headmaster did not understand at all, and said so. He had prepared to annihilate Lorimer hip and thigh, for he was now convinced that his blank astonishment at the mention of *The Dark Horse* during their previous interview had been, in the words of the bard, a mere veneer, a wile of guile. Since the morning he had seen Mr. Lawrie again, and had with his own eyes compared the two poems, the printed and the written, the author by special request having hunted up a copy of that valuable work, *The Dark Horse*, from the depths of a cupboard in his rooms.

His astonishment melted before Pringle’s explanation, which was brief and clear, and gave way to righteous wrath. In well-chosen terms he harangued the two criminals. Finally he perorated.

===Chinese===
洛里默住的是校长直辖的学校宿舍，找他问话可以不必兴师动众，免去「上课期间被传唤」必然招来的动静与围观。他刚开始做晚自习功课，管家便来传话，说校长在宿舍里他那一侧等他。

「先生要见的只有洛里默先生一位，」管家说。这时普林格尔已起身，要陪他的同案犯一道前往。

「没关系，」普林格尔说，「校长见了我向来高兴。我有长期邀请。他会明白的。」

起初，看到来了两个而自己只传了一个，校长非但不明白，还把这一点说了出来。他本已备好火力，要把洛里默打个落花流水，因为他此时已经认定：上次问话中，这家伙听到《黑马》二字时装出的茫然错愕，用诗人的话说，纯属一层薄薄的饰面，一种欺瞒的诡计。自上午起他又见过劳里先生一面，并亲眼比对了那两首诗——印的与手写的；应校长的特别请求，作者已从寓所壁橱的最深处翻出一册珍贵著作《黑马》。

他的惊愕在普林格尔简明扼要的解释面前消融了，随即让位于义愤。他用字斟句酌的言辞把两名罪犯训了一通，最后以结语收场。

===Original===
“There is only one point which tells in your favour. You have not attempted concealment.” (Pringle nudged Lorimer surreptitiously at this.) “And I may add that I believe that, as you say, you did not desire actually to win the prize by underhand means. But I cannot overlook such an offence. It is serious. Most serious. You will, both of you, go into extra lesson for the remaining Saturdays of the term.”

Extra lesson meant that instead of taking a half-holiday on Saturday like an ordinary law-abiding individual, you treated the day as if it were a full-school day, and worked from two till four under the eye of the Headmaster. Taking into consideration everything, the punishment was not an extraordinarily severe one, for there were only two more Saturdays to the end of term, and the sentence made no mention of the Wednesday half-holidays.

But in effect it was serious indeed. It meant that neither Pringle nor Lorimer would be able to play in the final House match against Leicester’s, which was fixed to begin on the next Saturday at two o’clock. Among the rules governing the House matches was one to the effect that no House might start a match with less than eleven men, nor might the Eleven be changed during the progress of the match—a rule framed by the Headmaster, not wholly without an eye to emergencies like the present.

===Chinese===
「只有一点对你们有利：你们没有试图隐瞒。」（听到这里，普林格尔悄悄捅了捅洛里默。）「另外我愿意相信，正如你们所说，你们并非当真想用不正当手段夺奖。但这样的过错我不能视而不见。它是严重的，极其严重。本学期剩下的每个星期六，你们两个都去留校补习。」

留校补习的意思是：星期六不再像奉公守法的良民那样休半天假，而是把这天当成正式上课日，在校长眼皮底下从两点做到四点。通盘考虑，这惩罚算不得格外严厉，因为离学期结束只剩两个星期六，且判决并未提及星期三的半天假。

但实际后果着实严重。这意味着普林格尔和洛里默都无法参加对莱斯特宿舍的宿舍决赛——比赛定于下个星期六两点开打。宿舍赛章程中有一条：任何宿舍开赛时人数不得少于十一人，且比赛进行期间不得更换阵容——这条章程正是校长手订的，未尝不是防着眼下这类紧急情况。

===Original===
“Thank goodness,” said Pringle, “that there aren’t any more First matches. It’s bad enough, though, by Jove, as it is. I suppose it’s occurred to you that this cuts us out of playing in the final?”

Lorimer said the point had not escaped his notice.

“I wish,” he observed, with simple pathos, “that I’d got the Rajah of Seltzerpore here now. I’d strangle him. I wonder if the Old Man realizes that he’s done his own House out of the cup?”

“Wouldn’t care if he did. Still, it’s a sickening nuisance. Leicester’s are a cert now.”

===Chinese===
「谢天谢地，一队的赛事没有别的了。」普林格尔说，「不过，天哪，眼下这已经够糟的了。我想你也想到了，这一下咱们俩都上不了决赛了？」

洛里默表示，这一层他并非没有注意到。

「我真希望，」他语带朴素的悲怆，「塞尔泽普尔王公（the Rajah of Seltzerpore）这会儿就在眼前，我非掐死他不可。也不知老头子想到没有，这一下他自家宿舍的奖杯也黄了？」

「他想没想到我才不管呢。可这事实在恶心透了。莱斯特宿舍这下稳赢了。」

===Original===
“Absolute cert,” said Lorimer; “Baynes can’t do all the bowling, especially on a hard wicket, and there’s nobody else. As for our batting and fielding—”

“Don’t,” said Pringle gloomily, “it’s too awful.”

On the following Saturday, Leicester’s ran up a total in their first innings which put the issue out of doubt, and finished off the game on the Monday by beating the School House by six wickets.

===Chinese===
「稳得不能再稳，」洛里默说，「贝恩斯（Baynes）一个人包揽不了全部投球，尤其在这种硬质球道上，别人又都拿不出手。至于咱们的击球和守场——」

「别说了，」普林格尔阴郁地说，「太惨了。」

到了下个星期六，莱斯特宿舍在第一局便砍下高分，胜负再无悬念；星期一，他们以六个三柱门的优势击败学校宿舍，了结了这场比赛。

## VI —and Stays There / 六、——并且待在那儿

===Original===
“I say,” said Baker of Jephson’s excitedly some days later, reeling into the study which he shared with Norris, “*have* you seen the team the M.C.C.’s bringing down?”

At nearly every school there is a type of youth who asks this question on the morning of the M.C.C. match. Norris was engaged in putting the finishing touches to a snow-white pair of cricket boots.

“No. Hullo, where did you raise that Sporter? Let’s have a look.”

But Baker proposed to conduct this business in person. It is ten times more pleasant to administer a series of shocks to a friend than to sit by and watch him administering them to himself. He retained *The Sportsman*, and began to read out the team.

===Chinese===
「我说，」几天后，杰夫森宿舍的贝克（Baker）激动地嚷着，一阵风似的冲进他与诺里斯（Norris）合用的书房，「你到底看没看见 M.C.C. 要带来的那支队伍？」

几乎每所学校里，都有那么一种青年，专在 M.C.C. 比赛当天的早晨问这个问题。诺里斯此刻正忙着给一双雪白的板球靴做最后的修整。

「没看。哟，你从哪儿弄来的那份《体育人报》（The Sportsman）？给我瞧瞧。」

可贝克打算亲自操办这桩业务。连番震撼由自己亲手施加给朋友，总比坐在一旁看他自己震撼自己痛快十倍。他攥住那份《体育人报》，开始念队伍名单。

===Original===
“Thought Middlesex had a match,” said Norris, as Baker paused dramatically to let the name of a world-famed professional sink in.

“No. They don’t play Surrey till Monday.”

“Well, if they’ve got an important match like Surrey on on Monday,” said Norris disgustedly, “what on earth do they let their best man come down here today for, and fag himself out?”

Baker suggested gently that if anybody was going to be fagged out at the end of the day, it would in all probability be the Beckford bowlers, and not a man who, as he was careful to point out, had run up a century a mere three days ago against Yorkshire, and who was apparently at that moment at the very top of his form.

===Chinese===
「我还以为米德尔塞克斯（Middlesex）有比赛呢，」诺里斯说。贝克戏剧性地顿了顿，好让那位举世闻名的职业球手的名字渗进对方脑子里。

「没有。他们星期一才跟萨里（Surrey）打。」

「咳，」诺里斯厌恶地说，「他们星期一既然有跟萨里这样的大赛，今天干吗还放他们最好的球手到这儿来，把力气耗光？」

贝克委婉地指出：真要说今天打完谁会累趴下，十有八九是贝克福德的投球手们，而不是那位——他特意点明——三天前刚对约克郡（Yorkshire）砍下一百分、眼下显然正处在巅峰状态的人物。

===Original===
“Well,” said Norris, “he might crock himself or anything. Rank bad policy, I call it. Anybody else?”

Baker resumed his reading. A string of unknowns ended in another celebrity.

“Blackwell?” said Norris. “Not O. T. Blackwell?”

“It says A. T. But,” went on Baker, brightening up again, “they always get the initials wrong in the papers. Certain to be O. T. By the way, I suppose you saw that he made eighty-three against Notts the other day?”

Norris tried to comfort himself by observing that Notts couldn’t bowl for toffee.

“Last week, too,” said Baker, “he made a hundred and forty-six not out against Malvern for the Gentlemen of Warwickshire. They couldn’t get him out,” he concluded with unction. In spite of the fact that he himself was playing in the match today, and might under the circumstances reasonably look forward to a considerable dose of leather-hunting, the task of announcing the bad news to Norris appeared to have a most elevating effect on his spirits.

===Chinese===
「哼，」诺里斯说，「他说不定会把自己弄伤呢。依我看，这政策糟糕透顶。还有谁？」

贝克接着往下念。一串无名之辈之后，又冒出一位大名人。

「布莱克韦尔？」诺里斯说，「不会是 O. T. 布莱克韦尔（Blackwell）吧？」

「报上写的是 A. T.。不过，」贝克接着说，又来了精神，「报纸总能把首字母弄错。准是 O. T.。对了，我想你看见了吧，他前几天对诺茨（Notts）拿了八十三分？」

诺里斯试图自我安慰，说诺茨队的投球根本不入流。

「还有上星期，」贝克说，「他代表沃里克郡（Warwickshire）绅士队对马尔文（Malvern）打了个一百四十六分未出局。他们根本没办法让他出局。」他心满意足地总结道。尽管自己今天也要上场，照这形势满可以预料要在场上大追一阵球皮，可是向诺里斯宣布坏消息这件事，似乎对他的情绪产生了一种极为提神的效果。

===Original===
“That’s nothing extra special,” said Norris, in answer to the last item of information, “the Malvern wicket’s like a billiard-table.”

“Our wickets aren’t bad either at this time of year,” said Baker, “and I heard rumours that they had got a record one ready for this match.”

“It seems to me,” said Norris, “that what I’d better do if we want to bat at all today is to win the toss. Though Sammy and the Bishop and Baynes ought to be able to get any ordinary side out all right.”

“Only this isn’t an ordinary side. It’s a sort of improved county team.”

“They’ve got about four men who might come off, but the M.C.C. sometimes have a bit of a tail. We ought to have a look in if we win the toss.”

“Hope so,” said Baker. “I doubt it, though.”

===Chinese===
「那没什么了不起，」诺里斯回答最后那条消息说，「马尔文的球道跟台球桌一样平。」

「我们这个季节的球道也不差，」贝克说，「而且我听说谣言，他们为这场比赛备好了一条破纪录的球道。」

「照我看，」诺里斯说，「今天咱们要想还有击球的份儿，我最好还是赢得掷币。不过萨米（Sammy）、主教和贝恩斯合力，把随便哪支普通队伍收拾出局应该不成问题。」

「问题是这可不是普通队伍。这是一支升级版的郡队。」

「他们大概有四个人可能发威，不过 M.C.C. 的尾部有时也软。只要咱们赢了掷币，就该有机会。」

「但愿如此，」贝克说，「我可不乐观。」

===Original===
At a quarter to eleven the School always went out in a body to inspect the pitch. After the wicket had been described by experts in hushed whispers as looking pretty good, the bell rang, and all who were not playing for the team, with the exception of the lucky individual who had obtained for himself the post of scorer, strolled back towards the blocks. Monk had come out with Waterford, but seeing Farnie ahead and walking alone he quitted Waterford, and attached himself to the genial Reginald. He wanted to talk business. He had not found the speculation of the two pounds a very profitable one. He had advanced the money under the impression that Farnie, by accepting it, was practically selling his independence. And there were certain matters in which Monk was largely interested, connected with the breaking of bounds and the purchase of contraband goods, which he would have been exceedingly glad to have performed by deputy. He had fancied that Farnie would have taken over these jobs as part of his debt. But he had mistaken his man. On the very first occasion when he had attempted to put on the screw, Farnie had flatly refused to have anything to do with what he proposed. He said that he was not Monk’s fag—a remark which had the merit of being absolutely true.

All this, combined with a slight sinking of his own funds, induced Monk to take steps towards recovering the loan.

“I say, Farnie, old chap.”

“Hullo!”

===Chinese===
十点三刻，全校照例成群结队地出去察看球场。行家们压低嗓音交头接耳，盛赞球道看上去相当不错；随后钟声一响，所有不参加比赛的人——除了那位给自己谋到记分员美差的幸运儿——都遛达着回教学楼去了。蒙克（Monk）是和沃特福德（Waterford）一起出来的，但看见法尼（Farnie）独自走在前面，便撇下沃特福德，凑到那位和气的雷金纳德（Reginald）身边。他要想谈一笔生意。他发现那两镑投资并不怎么有利可图。他当初垫出这笔钱，是以为法尼收下它，就等于实际上出卖了自己的独立性。而蒙克对某些事大有兴趣——诸如越界跑出去、采买违禁品之类——这些事他巴不得有人代理。他曾设想法尼会把这差事当作债务的一部分接过去。可他看错了人。他头一回试图施压，法尼就一口回绝，说跟他提议的事毫无干系。他说自己又不是蒙克的小跑腿——这话的好处是绝对属实。

凡此种种，加上自己手头略见拮据，促使蒙克采取措施收回那笔借款。

「我说，法尼，老伙计。」

「哟！」

===Original===
“I say, do you remember my lending you two quid some time ago?”

“You don’t give me much chance of forgetting it,” said Farnie.

Monk smiled. He could afford to be generous towards such witticisms.

“I want it back,” he said.

“All right. You’ll get it at the end of term.”

“I want it now.”

“Why?”

“Awfully hard up, old chap.”

===Chinese===
「我说，你还记得前阵子我借给你两镑钱吗？」

「你可没怎么给我忘掉它的机会。」法尼说。

蒙克微微一笑。对这类俏皮话，他尽可以宽宏大量。

「我要收回来。」他说。

「行啊。学期结束你就拿到。」

「我现在就要。」

「为什么？」

「手头紧得厉害，老伙计。」

===Original===
“You aren’t,” said Farnie. “You’ve got three pounds twelve and sixpence halfpenny. If you will keep counting your money in public, you can’t blame a chap for knowing how much you’ve got.”

Monk, slightly disconcerted, changed his plan of action. He abandoned skirmishing tactics.

“Never mind that,” he said, “the point is that I want that four pounds. I’m going to have it, too.”

“I know. At the end of term.”

“I’m going to have it now.”

“You can have a pound of it now.”

===Chinese===
「你才不紧呢，」法尼说，「你有三镑十二先令六便士半。你既然老爱当众数钱，就怪不得人家知道你有多少家底。」

蒙克略显狼狈，随即改变行动计划。他放弃了迂回战术。

「那个不用管，」他说，「要点是那四镑我要定了。我一定拿得到。」

「我知道。学期结束拿到。」

「我现在就要拿到。」

「现在可以先给你一镑。」

===Original===
“Not enough.”

“I don’t see how you expect me to raise any more. If I could, do you think I should have borrowed it? You might chuck rotting for a change.”

“Now, look here, old chap,” said Monk, “I should think you’d rather raise that tin somehow than have it get about that you’d been playing pills at some pub out of bounds. What?”

Farnie, for one of the few occasions on record, was shaken out of his usual sangfroid. Even in his easy code of morality there had always been one crime which was an anathema, the sort of thing no fellow could think of doing. But it was obviously at this that Monk was hinting.

===Chinese===
「不够。」

「我不明白你怎么指望我再筹出更多的来。我要是筹得出，你以为我还会去借吗？你也换换花样，别再缠人了。」

「这样，你听我说，老伙计，」蒙克道，「我想你总宁愿想方设法筹出那笔钱来，也不愿传出去你在越界区的哪家酒馆里打台球赌钱。对不对？」

有记载可查的少数几回里，这是一回：法尼被撼动了一贯的沉着。即便照他那套宽松的道德守则，也始终有一桩罪是绝对的大忌，是任何体面人都想不到去干的事。而蒙克暗指的，明摆着正是这个。

===Original===
“Good Lord, man,” he cried, “you don’t mean to say you’re thinking of sneaking? Why, the fellows would boot you round the field. You couldn’t stay in the place a week.”

“There are heaps of ways,” said Monk, “in which a thing can get about without anyone actually telling the beaks. At present I’ve not told a soul. But, you know, if I let it out to anyone they might tell someone else, and so on. And if everybody knows a thing, the beaks generally get hold of it sooner or later. You’d much better let me have that four quid, old chap.”

Farnie capitulated.

“All right,” he said, “I’ll get it somehow.”

“Thanks awfully, old chap,” said Monk, “so long!”

===Chinese===
「老天爷，你这家伙，」他叫道，「你该不会是想去告密吧？同学们会把你绕着球场踢一圈的。你在这地方连一个星期都待不下去。」

「事情传开的法子多得很，」蒙克说，「用不着谁当真去告诉先生们（beaks）。眼下我一个字都没泄露。可是你知道，我要是说给任何人听，他们没准又说给别人听，如此这般。一件事要是人人皆知，先生们迟早总会打听到。你最好还是把那四镑给我，老伙计。」

法尼投降了。

「好吧，」他说，「我总归想办法弄来。」

「多谢多谢，老伙计，」蒙克说，「回见！」

===Original===
In all Beckford there was only one person who was in the least degree likely to combine the two qualities necessary for the extraction of Farnie from his difficulties. These qualities were—in the first place ability, in the second place willingness to advance him, free of security, the four pounds he required. The person whom he had in his mind was Gethryn. He had reasoned the matter out step by step during the second half of morning school. Gethryn, though he had, as Farnie knew, no overwhelming amount of affection for his uncle, might in a case of great need prove blood to be thicker (as per advertisement) than water. But, he reflected, he must represent himself as in danger of expulsion rather than flogging. He had an uneasy idea that if the Bishop were to discover that all he stood to get was a flogging, he would remark with enthusiasm that, as far as he was concerned, the good work might go on. Expulsion was different. To save a member of his family from expulsion, he might think it worth while to pass round the hat amongst his wealthy acquaintances. If four plutocrats with four sovereigns were to combine, Farnie, by their united efforts, would be saved. And he rather liked the notion of being turned into a sort of limited liability company, like the Duke of Plaza Toro, at a pound a share. It seemed to add a certain dignity to his position.

To Gethryn’s study, therefore, he went directly school was over. If he had reflected, he might have known that he would not have been there while the match was going on. But his brain, fatigued with his recent calculations, had not noted this point.

The study was empty.

===Chinese===
在整个贝克福德，只有一个人多少有可能兼备那两种把法尼从困境里捞出来的必要品质。这两种品质，第一是有本事，第二是愿意无担保地垫出他所需要的那四镑。他心里想定的这个人，是盖斯林（Gethryn）。上午课后半段，他已把这事一步一步盘算停当。法尼知道，盖斯林对自己这位叔叔谈不上有多少深情，可到了十万火急的关头，没准能验证血浓于水（正如广告里说的）。不过他转念一想，必须把自己描绘成面临开除而非挨鞭子的险境。他隐隐觉得，倘若主教发现等待自己的不过是一顿鞭子，他会满腔热忱地表示：就他而论，这桩好事大可继续进行。开除就不同了。为了使自家人免遭开除，他也许会觉得值得在有钱的熟人圈里挨个募捐。只要四位富翁各出一枚金镑联起手来，法尼便可凭他们的合力获救。而他相当喜欢把自己变成一家股份有限公司的设想——就像普拉扎托罗公爵（Duke of Plaza Toro）那样，每股一镑。这似乎给他处境平添了几分体面。

于是下午一放学，他便直奔盖斯林的书房。他若细想想，本该知道比赛期间那人绝不会在屋里。但他那颗被刚才一连串计算累坏了的脑袋，没有留意到这一点。

书房空无一人。

===Original===
Most people, on finding themselves in a strange and empty room, are seized with a desire to explore the same, and observe from internal evidence what manner of man is the owner. Nowhere does character come out so clearly as in the decoration of one’s private den. Many a man, at present respected by his associates, would stand forth unmasked at his true worth, could the world but look into his room. For there they would see that he was so lost to every sense of shame as to cover his books with brown paper, or deck his walls with oleographs presented with the Christmas numbers, both of which habits argue a frame of mind fit for murders, stratagems, and spoils. Let no such man be trusted.

The Bishop’s study, which Farnie now proceeded to inspect, was not of this kind. It was a neat study, arranged with not a little taste. There were photographs of teams with the College arms on their plain oak frames, and photographs of relations in frames which tried to look, and for the most part succeeded in looking, as if they had not cost fourpence three farthings at a Christmas bargain sale. There were snapshots of various moving incidents in the careers of the Bishop and his friends: Marriott, for example, as he appeared when carried to the Pavilion after that sensational century against the Authentics: Robertson of Blaker’s winning the quarter mile: John Brown, Norris’s predecessor in the captaincy, and one of the four best batsmen Beckford had ever had, batting at the nets: Norris taking a skier on the boundary in last year’s M.C.C. match: the Bishop himself going out to bat in the Charchester match, and many more of the same sort.

===Chinese===
大多数人一旦置身于一间陌生而空寂的房间，便按捺不住想要探个究竟，从内部证据推断主人是何等样人。而人的品性，从没有比私室布置暴露得更清楚的地方了。多少至今受同事敬重的人，只要世人能看上一眼他的房间，便会现出原形、露出真实斤两。因为在那儿他们会看到：此人寡廉鲜耻到了给书包牛皮纸封皮、或用圣诞节增刊附赠的石印画装点墙壁的地步——这两种习惯都表明一种适宜于凶杀、奸计与掠夺的心智。此等人切不可信任。

主教这间此刻由法尼着手考察的书房，不属于此类。这是一间整洁的书房，布置得颇有品位。墙上有球队合影，朴素的橡木相框上刻着学院纹章；还有亲戚的照片，相框努力装出——而且大体成功地装出——绝非圣诞节大减价上四便士三个法寻一件买来的样子。另有若干快照，记录着主教和他的朋友们生涯中种种感人的瞬间：比如马里奥特（Marriott）对「正宗队」（the Authentics）打出轰动一百分后被抬进休息亭时的模样；布莱克宿舍（Blaker’s）的罗伯逊（Robertson）赢下四分之一英里赛跑；约翰·布朗（John Brown）——诺里斯的前任队长，贝克福德史上最优秀的四名击球手之一——在网球场挥棒击球；诺里斯在去年 M.C.C. 比赛中在边界接住高飞球；主教本人在查彻斯特（Charchester）比赛中出场击球；还有许多同类的照片。

===Original===
All these Farnie observed with considerable interest, but as he moved towards the bookshelf his eye was caught by an object more interesting still. It was a cashbox, simple and unornamental, but undoubtedly a cashbox, and as he took it up it rattled.

The key was in the lock. In a boarding House at a public school it is not, as a general rule, absolutely necessary to keep one’s valuables always hermetically sealed. The difference between *meum* and *tuum* is so very rarely confused by the occupants of such an establishment, that one is apt to grow careless, and every now and then accidents happen. An accident was about to happen now.

It was at first without any motive except curiosity that Farnie opened the cashbox. He merely wished to see how much there was inside, with a view to ascertaining what his prospects of negotiating a loan with his relative were likely to be. When, however, he did see, other feelings began to take the place of curiosity. He counted the money. There were ten sovereigns, one half-sovereign, and a good deal of silver. One of the institutions at Beckford was a mission. The School by (more or less) voluntary contributions supported a species of home somewhere in the wilds of Kennington. No one knew exactly what or where this home was, but all paid their subscriptions as soon as possible in the term, and tried to forget about it. Gethryn collected not only for Leicester’s House, but also for the Sixth Form, and was consequently, if only by proxy, a man of large means. *Too* large, Farnie thought. Surely four pounds, to be paid back (probably) almost at once, would not be missed. Why shouldn’t he—

===Chinese===
这一切法尼都饶有兴味地看在眼里，可当他朝书架走去时，一件更有趣的东西攫住了他的目光。那是一只钱箱，朴素无华，但确凿无疑是只钱箱，他一拿起来，里面便哗啦作响。

钥匙就插在锁眼里。在公学的寄宿宿舍里，照例并不绝对需要把贵重物品时时密封上锁。「你的」与「我的」之间的界限，在这样一种机构的住户中间极少被弄混，于是人就容易大意，时不时便会出点意外。此刻，一桩意外就要发生了。

法尼起先打开钱箱，除好奇之外并无别的动机。他只想看看里面有多少，借以判断向亲戚洽谈借款的前景大概如何。然而等他真看见了，别的情绪便开始取代好奇。他把钱数了一遍。十枚金镑，一枚半金镑，还有不少银币。贝克福德的常设机构之一是一个布道团。全校以（多少算是）自愿捐助的方式，在肯宁顿（Kennington）的蛮荒深处供养着某种「之家」。没人确切知道这个之家是什么、在哪儿，但人人都在学期初尽早缴清捐款，然后努力把它忘掉。盖斯林不但替莱斯特宿舍收款，还替六年级收，因此，哪怕只是代理，也算得上一位资财雄厚的人士了。未免太雄厚了，法尼想。区区四镑，（多半）几乎立刻就能归还，肯定不会被人惦记。他为什么不——

===Original===
“Hullo!”

Farnie spun round. Wilson was standing in the doorway.

“Hullo, Farnie,” said he, “what are you playing at in here?”

“What are you?” retorted Farnie politely.

“Come to fetch a book. Marriott said I might. What are you up to?”

“Oh, shut up!” said Farnie. “Why shouldn’t I come here if I like? Matter of fact, I came to see Gethryn.”

“He isn’t here,” said Wilson luminously.

===Chinese===
「哟！」

法尼猛地转过身。威尔逊（Wilson）正站在门口。

「哟，法尼，」他说，「你在这儿搞什么名堂？」

「你呢？」法尼客气地回敬。

「来取本书。马里奥特说我可以来。你想干什么？」

「哦，闭嘴！」法尼说，「我高兴来就来，凭什么不行？老实说，我是来找盖斯林的。」

「他不在。」威尔逊明白晓畅地指出。

===Original===
“You don’t mean to say you’ve noticed that already? You’ve got an eye like a hawk, Wilson. I was just taking a look round, if you really want to know.”

“Well, I shouldn’t advise you to let Marriott catch you mucking his study up. Seen a book called *Round the Red Lamp*? Oh, here it is. Coming over to the field?”

“Not just yet. I want to have another look round. Don’t you wait, though.”

“Oh, all right.” And Wilson retired with his book.

Now, though Wilson at present suspected nothing, not knowing of the existence of the cashbox, Farnie felt that when the money came to be missed, and inquiries were made as to who had been in the study, and when, he would recall the interview. Two courses, therefore, remained open to him. He could leave the money altogether, or he could take it and leave himself. In other words, run away.

In the first case there would, of course, remain the chance that he might induce Gethryn to lend him the four pounds, but this had never been more than a forlorn hope; and in the light of the possibilities opened out by the cashbox, he thought no more of it. The real problem was, should he or should he not take the money from the cashbox?

As he hesitated, the recollection of Monk’s veiled threats came back to him, and he wavered no longer. He opened the box again, took out the contents, and dropped them into his pocket. While he was about it, he thought he might as well take all as only a part.

Then he wrote two notes. One—to the Bishop—he placed on top of the cashbox; the other he placed with four sovereigns on the table in Monk’s study. Finally he left the room, shut the door carefully behind him, and went to the yard at the back of the House, where he kept his bicycle.

The workings of the human mind, and especially of the young human mind, are peculiar. It never occurred to Farnie that a result equally profitable to himself, and decidedly more convenient for all concerned—with the possible exception of Monk—might have been arrived at if he had simply left the money in the box, and run away without it.

However, as the poet says, you can’t think of everything.

===Chinese===
「你该不会是已经注意到这一点了吧？你眼睛跟鹰似的，威尔逊。你要真想知道，我不过随便转转。」

「哼，我可劝你别让马里奥特撞见你乱翻他的书房。见过一本叫《红灯周围》（Round the Red Lamp）的书吗？哦，在这儿呢。去球场吗？」

「先不去。我还想再转转。你不用等我。」

「哦，行。」威尔逊拿着书退了出去。

此刻威尔逊固然毫无怀疑，因为他并不知道钱箱的存在，可法尼觉得，等钱一旦被发现少了、追查谁来过书房、何时来过的时候，他会记起这场会面。于是他面前只剩两条路：要么把钱整个儿留下，要么把钱带走、把自己撇下。换句话说，跑路。

走前一条路，当然还剩下一线希望，说不定能说动盖斯林借他那四镑；可这从来不过是渺茫的奢望，如今在钱箱展开的种种可能性的光辉面前，他更是想都不再想。真正的问题是：钱箱里的钱，他拿，还是不拿？

他正犹豫，蒙克那番含沙射影的威胁又浮上心头，他便不再动摇了。他重新打开箱子，把里面的东西统统取出，塞进口袋。既然动手了，他想，索性全拿走，也比只拿一部分强。

接着他写了两张字条。一张给主教，放在钱箱顶上；另一张连同四枚金镑一起放在蒙克书房的桌上。最后他离开房间，小心翼翼地带上门，走到宿舍后院——他那辆自行车存放在那儿。

人类心智的运作，尤其是年轻人类心智的运作，是很奇特的。法尼从来没有想到：倘若他干脆把钱留在箱子里、空手跑路，结果对自己同样有利，而且对所有相关人等——可能除蒙克之外——都省事得多。

不过，正如诗人所说，人不能事事周全。

## V Farnie Gets Into Trouble— / 五、法尼惹上了麻烦——

===Original===
It was perhaps only natural that Farnie, having been warned so strongly of the inadvisability of having anything to do with Monk, should for that very reason be attracted to him. Nobody ever wants to do anything except what they are not allowed to do. Otherwise there is no explaining the friendship that arose between them. Jack Monk was not an attractive individual. He had a slack mouth and a shifty eye, and his complexion was the sort which friends would have described as olive, enemies (with more truth) as dirty green. These defects would have mattered little, of course, in themselves. There’s many a bilious countenance, so to speak, covers a warm heart. With Monk, however, appearances were not deceptive. He looked a bad lot, and he was one.

It was on the second morning of term that the acquaintanceship began. Monk was coming downstairs from his study with Danvers, and Farnie was leaving the fags’ day-room.

===Chinese===
法尼被如此郑重地警告过，说与蒙克（Monk）交往殊为不智，结果偏偏因此被此人吸引过去——这或许本是人之常情。人不做被禁止的事，就什么事都不想做。否则，他们之间产生的友谊便无从解释。杰克·蒙克（Jack Monk）不是个讨人喜欢的角色。他嘴皮松垮，眼神游移，那张脸的肤色嘛，朋友们会形容为橄榄色，仇人们（说得更准）则形容为脏绿色。这些缺陷本身当然无关紧要。可以说，多少张黄疸病的面孔底下，都包着一颗热心肠。然而在蒙克身上，外表并不骗人。他看上去是个坏坯子，他也确实是个坏坯子。

相识始于学期的第二个早晨。蒙克和丹弗斯（Danvers）从楼上书房下来，法尼正从低年级仆役的日间活动室里出来。

===Original===
“See that kid?” said Danvers. “That’s the chap I was telling you about. Gethryn’s uncle, you know.”

“Not really? Let’s cultivate him. I say, old chap, don’t walk so fast.” Farnie, rightly concluding that the remark was addressed to him, turned and waited, and the three strolled over to the School buildings together.

They would have made an interesting study for the observer of human nature, the two seniors fancying that they had to deal with a small boy just arrived at his first school, and in the grip of that strange, lost feeling which attacks the best of new boys for a day or so after their arrival; and Farnie, on the other hand, watching every move, as perfectly composed and at home as a youth should be with the experience of three public schools to back him up.

===Chinese===
「瞧见那小子没？」丹弗斯说，「就是我跟你说的那个。盖斯林的叔叔，知道吧。」

「当真？那咱们去亲近亲近。喂，老伙计，别走那么快。」法尼断定这话是对自己说的，而且断得没错，便转身等着；三个人于是一起朝教学楼溜达过去。

在人性观察者眼里，这一幕本可构成一项有趣的研究：两个高年级生自以为对付的是一个初来乍到第一所学校的小男孩，正被那种连最优秀的新生入学后一两天里也难免的奇异失落感攫住；而法尼呢，一面冷眼看着每一步棋，从容自若、如鱼得水——一个有三所公学阅历撑腰的青年，理当如此。

===Original===
When they arrived at the School gates, Monk and Danvers turned to go in the direction of their form-room, the Remove, leaving Farnie at the door of the Upper Fourth. At this point a small comedy took place. Monk, after feeling hastily in his pockets, requested Danvers to lend him five shillings until next Saturday. Danvers knew this request of old, and he knew the answer that was expected of him. By replying that he was sorry, but he had not got the money, he gave Farnie, who was still standing at the door, his cue to offer to supply the deficiency. Most new boys—they had grasped this fact from experience—would have felt it an honour to oblige a senior with a small loan. As Farnie made no signs of doing what was expected of him, Monk was obliged to resort to the somewhat cruder course of applying for the loan in person. He applied. Farnie with the utmost willingness brought to light a handful of money, mostly gold. Monk’s eye gleamed approval, and he stretched forth an itching palm. Danvers began to think that it would be rash to let a chance like this slip. Ordinarily the tacit agreement between the pair was that only one should borrow at a time, lest confidence should be destroyed in the victim. But here was surely an exception, a special case. With a young gentleman so obviously a man of coin as Farnie, the rule might well be broken for once.

===Chinese===
到了校门口，蒙克和丹弗斯拐向他们的教室——低年级班（the Remove）——把法尼留在高四班（Upper Fourth）门口。此时上演了一出小小的喜剧。蒙克慌忙摸了摸口袋，然后请丹弗斯借他五先令，撑到下星期六。丹弗斯对这套请求熟稔于心，也知道人家期待他作何回答。他答道：很抱歉，手头没钱——这就给仍站在门口的法尼递上了暗号，让他主动提出填补缺口。大多数新生——这是他们从经验里摸清的——都会觉得能借点小钱为高年级学长效劳是件体面事。可法尼毫无照办的迹象，蒙克只得诉诸较为生硬的一途：亲自开口借钱。他开了口。法尼以最大的乐意掏出一把钱，多半是金币。蒙克的眼睛赞许地放光，伸出一只发痒的手掌。丹弗斯开始觉得，放过这样一个机会未免鲁莽。按说两人之间有默契：一次只许一个开口借，免得猎物对二人一并失去信任。但这回显然是个例外，特例。面对法尼这样一位明摆着的金币少爷，规矩不妨破上一回。

===Original===
“While you’re about it, Farnie, old man,” he said carelessly, “you might let me have a bob or two if you don’t mind. Five bob’ll see me through to Saturday all right.”

“Do you mean tomorrow?” enquired Farnie, looking up from his heap of gold.

“No, Saturday week. Let you have it back by then at the latest. Make a point of it.”

“How would a quid do?”

“Ripping,” said Danvers ecstatically.

“Same here,” assented Monk.

===Chinese===
「既然你也在这儿，法尼老弟，」他漫不经心地说，「你要不介意，不妨也借我一两先令。五先令就够我撑到星期六了。」

「你是说明天吗？」法尼从他那堆金币上抬起头，问道。

「不，是下下个星期六。最迟那时候准还你。我说话算话。」

「一镑怎么样？」

「妙极了。」丹弗斯心醉神迷地说。

「我也一样。」蒙克附和道。

===Original===
“Then that’s all right,” said Farnie briskly; “I thought perhaps you mightn’t have had enough. You’ve got a quid, I know, Monk, because I saw you haul one out at breakfast. And Danvers has got one too, because he offered to toss you for it in the study afterwards. And besides, I couldn’t lend you anything in any case, because I’ve only got about fourteen quid myself.”

With which parting shot he retired, wrapped in gentle thought, into his form-room; and from the noise which ensued immediately upon his arrival, the shrewd listener would have deduced, quite correctly, that he had organized and taken the leading part in a general rag.

Monk and Danvers proceeded upon their way.

===Chinese===
「那就好办了，」法尼爽快地说，「我还怕你们不够花呢。我知道你有一镑，蒙克，因为早饭时我看见你掏出来过一张。丹弗斯也有一镑，因为随后在书房里他要跟你掷币猜正反赢它来着。再说了，反正我也什么都不能借你们，因为我自己统共不过十四镑上下。」

丢下这句临别妙语，他便沉浸在恬适的思索里退进了教室；从紧随他到来而起的那阵喧闹声判断，耳朵尖的人准能推断出——而且推断得完全正确——他已组织并亲自领衔了一场全班大闹。

蒙克和丹弗斯继续赶路。

===Original===
“You got rather left there, old chap,” said Monk at length.

“I like that,” replied the outraged Danvers. “How about you, then? It seemed to me you got rather left, too.”

Monk compromised.

“Well, anyhow,” he said, “we shan’t get much out of that kid.”

“Little beast,” said Danvers complainingly. And they went on into their form-room in silence.

===Chinese===
「你刚才可有点下不来台，老伙计。」蒙克终于开口。

「亏你说得出口，」蒙受奇耻大辱的丹弗斯答道，「那你自己呢？依我看，你也有点下不来台吧。」

蒙克折中了一下。

「咳，不管怎么说，」他说，「从那小子身上咱们榨不出多少油水。」

「小畜生。」丹弗斯抱怨道。两人默默走进教室。

===Original===
“I saw your young—er—relative in earnest conversation with friend Monk this morning,” said Marriott, later on in the day, to Gethryn; “I thought you were going to give him the tip in that direction?”

“So I did,” said the Bishop wearily; “but I can’t always be looking after the little brute. He only does it out of sheer cussedness, because I’ve told him not to. It stands to reason that he can’t *like* Monk.”

“You remind me of the psalmist and the wicked man, surname unknown,” said Marriott. “You *can’t* see the good side of Monk.”

“There isn’t one.”

“No. He’s only got two sides, a bad side and a worse side, which he sticks on on the strength of being fairly good at games. I wonder if he’s going to get his First this season. He’s not a bad bat.”

===Chinese===
「今天早上我看见你那位年轻的——呃——亲戚，正跟蒙克朋友热切交谈呢。」当天晚些时候，马里奥特（Marriott）对盖斯林（Gethryn）说，「我还以为你要朝那个方向给他递话来着？」

「递了。」主教倦怠地说，「可我不能整天看着那小畜生。他纯粹是存心作对，就因为我吩咐过他别这么干。按理说他不可能喜欢蒙克这个人。」

「你让我想起诗篇作者和那位恶人——姓氏不详，」马里奥特说，「你就是看不见蒙克好的一面。」

「根本没有好的一面。」

「是啊。他统共只有两面：坏的一面，和更坏的一面；仗着球技还算过得去，他把更坏那面牢牢贴在身上。不知道他这个赛季能不能进一队。他击球倒不算坏。」

===Original===
“I don’t think he will. He is a good bat, but there are heaps better in the place. I say, I think I shall give young Farnie the tip once more, and let him take it or leave it. What do you think?”

“He’ll leave it,” said Marriott, with conviction.

Nor was he mistaken. Farnie listened with enthusiasm to his nephew’s second excursus on the Monk topic, and, though he said nothing, was apparently convinced. On the following afternoon Monk, Danvers, Waterford, and he hired a boat and went up the river together. Gethryn and Marriott, steered by Wilson, who was rapidly developing into a useful coxswain, got an excellent view of them moored under the shade of a willow, drinking ginger-beer, and apparently on the best of terms with one another and the world in general. In a brief but moving speech the Bishop finally excommunicated his erring relative. “For all I care,” he concluded, “he can do what he likes in future. I shan’t stop him.”

“No,” said Marriott, “I don’t think you will.”

===Chinese===
「我看进不了。他击球不错，可校内比他强的一大把。我说，我想再给小法尼递一回话，爱听不听随他。你觉得呢？」

「他不会听的。」马里奥特深信不疑地说。

他也没有看错。法尼满怀热忱地听完了侄子关于蒙克问题的第二次专题讲演，虽一言未发，显然已被说服。第二天下午，蒙克、丹弗斯、沃特福德（Waterford）和他雇了一条船，一起溯河而上。盖斯林和马里奥特的船由威尔逊（Wilson）掌舵——这小子正迅速成长为一名得用的舵手——正好将他们尽收眼底：船泊在柳荫下，喝着姜汁啤酒，彼此以及同整个世界显然都相处得再融洽不过。主教发表了一篇简短而动人的演说，终于将这位误入歧途的亲戚逐出教门。「往后他爱干什么干什么，」他总结道，「我才懒得管。」

「是啊，」马里奥特说，「我也觉得您管不了。」

===Original===
For the first month of his school life Farnie behaved, except in his choice of companions, much like an ordinary junior. He played cricket moderately well, did his share of compulsory fielding at the First Eleven net, and went for frequent river excursions with Monk, Danvers, and the rest of the Mob.

At first the other juniors of the House were inclined to resent this extending of the right hand of fellowship to owners of studies and Second Eleven men, and attempted to make Farnie see the sin and folly of his ways. But Nature had endowed that youth with a fund of vitriolic repartee. When Millett, one of Leicester’s juniors, evolved some laborious sarcasm on the subject of Farnie’s swell friends, Farnie, in a series of three remarks, reduced him, figuratively speaking, to a small and palpitating spot of grease. After that his actions came in for no further, or at any rate no outspoken comment.

Given sixpence a week and no more, Farnie might have survived an entire term without breaking any serious School rule. But when, after buying a bicycle from Smith of Markham’s, he found himself with eight pounds to his name in solid cash, and the means of getting far enough away from the neighbourhood of the School to be able to spend it much as he liked, he began to do strange and risky things in his spare time.

===Chinese===
入学后的第一个月，除了挑伙伴这一桩，法尼的做派与寻常低年级生并无二致。他板球打得还算过得去，在一队击球网场上尽了自己那份强制守场的义务，还经常同蒙克、丹弗斯以及那伙「乌合之众」泛舟河上。

起初，宿舍里其他低年级生对他向书房主人们和二队球手们伸手结交颇有愤愤之意，还试图让法尼认识到此中罪愆与愚蠢。但造物主赋予这位青年一肚子刻薄的妙语。当莱斯特宿舍的低年级生米利特（Millett）就法尼那些阔气朋友费劲地编排出几句讥讽时，法尼连出三句话，打个比方说，把他熬成了一小滩瑟瑟发抖的油渍。此后他的行径便不再招致评论——至少不再招致公开的评论。

倘若每周零花钱只有六便士、一个子儿不多，法尼兴许能撑过整整一个学期而不触犯任何像样的校规。可自从从马克姆宿舍（Markham’s）的史密斯（Smith）手里买了辆自行车之后，他发现自己名下有了八镑实打实的现金，也有了足以远离学校周边、随心挥霍的手段，课余便开始干起种种离奇而冒险的事来。

===Original===
The great obstacle to illicit enjoyment at Beckford was the four o’clock roll-call on half-holidays. There were other obstacles, such as half-holiday games and so forth, but these could be avoided by the exercise of a little judgement. The penalty for nonappearance at a half-holiday game was a fine of sixpence. Constant absence was likely in time to lead to a more or less thrilling interview with the captain of cricket, but a very occasional attendance was enough to stave off this disaster; and as for the sixpence, to a man of means like Farnie it was a mere nothing. It was a bad system, and it was a wonder, under the circumstances, how Beckford produced the elevens it did. But it was the system, and Farnie availed himself of it to the full.

===Chinese===
在贝克福德，非法取乐的头号障碍是半天假当天的四点点名。其余障碍还有一些，比如半天假的球赛之类，但略施判断即可绕开。缺席半天假球赛的罚金是六便士。长期缺勤迟早会招来一场或惊或险的板球队长谈话，可偶尔露一次面就足以躲开这场灾难；至于那六便士，对法尼这样的有钱人而言不过九牛一毛。这是个糟糕的制度，而在这种情形下，贝克福德居然还能培养出它确实培养出的那些强队，也算一桩奇事。可制度就是制度，法尼把它利用了个十足。

===Original===
The obstacle of roll-call he managed also to surmount. Some reckless and penniless friend was generally willing, for a consideration, to answer his name for him. And so most Saturday afternoons would find Farnie leaving behind him the flannelled fools at their various wickets, and speeding out into the country on his bicycle in the direction of the village of Biddlehampton, where mine host of the “Cow and Cornflower,” in addition to other refreshment for man and beast, advertised that ping-pong and billiards might be played on the premises. It was not the former of these games that attracted Farnie. He was no pinger. Nor was he a pongster. But for billiards he had a decided taste, a genuine taste, not the pumped-up affectation sometimes displayed by boys of his age. Considering his age he was a remarkable player. Later on in life it appeared likely that he would have the choice of three professions open to him, namely, professional billiard player, billiard marker, and billiard sharp. At each of the three he showed distinct promise. He was not “lured to the green cloth” by Monk or Danvers. Indeed, if there had been any luring to be done, it is probable that he would have done it, and not they. Neither Monk nor Danvers was in his confidence in the matter. Billiards is not a cheap amusement. By the end of his sixth week Farnie was reduced to a single pound, a sum which, for one of his tastes, was practically poverty. And just at the moment when he was least able to bear up against it, Fate dealt him one of its nastiest blows. He was playing a fifty up against a friendly but unskilful farmer at the “Cow and Cornflower.” “Better look out,” he said, as his opponent effected a somewhat rustic stroke, “you’ll be cutting the cloth in a second.” The farmer grunted, missed by inches, and retired, leaving the red ball in the jaws of the pocket, and Farnie with three to make to win.

===Chinese===
点名的障碍他也有办法翻越。总有个把胆大妄为又身无分文的朋友，收点好处便肯替他应名。于是大多数星期六下午，法尼把各守各的三柱门、穿法兰绒的傻瓜们抛在身后，骑上自行车一路飞驰出乡间，直奔比德尔汉普顿（Biddlehampton）村——「母牛与矢车菊」酒馆的老板，除了供应人畜各自的其他消遣之外，还贴出告示说本店可打乒乓球与台球。吸引法尼的不是前者。他既不「乒」，也不「乓」。可对台球他有一种斩钉截铁的爱好，一种真正的爱好，并非他这年纪的男孩有时摆出来的那种硬挤出来的附庸风雅。就年龄而论，他是个了不起的球手。往后的人生里，他多半可以在三种职业中任选其一：职业台球手、台球记分员、台球老千。三行他行行都显出可观的前途。他并不是被蒙克或丹弗斯「诱上绿呢台」的。说实在的，若真有诱拐的活儿要干，多半也是他干，轮不到他们。这件事上蒙克和丹弗斯都不在他心腹之列。台球可不是便宜的消遣。到第六个星期结束时，法尼只剩一镑，以他这口味，这点钱实际上就是赤贫。而就在他最扛不住的当口，命运朝他发出了最恶毒的一击。他正在「母牛与矢车菊」同一位和气却不高明的农场主打五十分一局。「留点神，」对手打出一记颇为乡土的杆法时他说，「再这样你就要把台呢划破了。」农场主咕哝一声，差之毫厘地未进，退了下去，把红球留在袋口，让法尼再得三分即可获胜。

===Original===
It was an absurdly easy stroke, and the Bishop’s uncle took it with an absurd amount of conceit and carelessness. Hardly troubling to aim, he struck his ball. The cue slid off in one direction, the ball rolled sluggishly in another. And when the cue had finished its run, the smooth green surface of the table was marred by a jagged and unsightly cut. There was another young man gone wrong!

To say that the farmer laughed would be to express the matter feebly. That his young opponent, who had been irritating him unspeakably since the beginning of the game with advice and criticism, should have done exactly what he had cautioned him, the farmer, against a moment before, struck him as being the finest example of poetic justice he had ever heard of, and he signalized his appreciation of the same by nearly dying of apoplexy.

The marker expressed an opinion that Farnie had been and gone and done it.

===Chinese===
这一杆容易得近乎荒唐，而主教的叔叔打它时那股自负与大意也荒唐得可以。他几乎懒得瞄准，便出了杆。杆头朝一个方向滑偏，球朝另一个方向有气无力地滚去。等球杆跑完它的行程，台面平滑的绿呢上已添了一道锯齿状的破口，大煞风景。又一位青年堕落了！

说农场主笑了，那还说得太平淡。他那年轻的对手从开局起就拿指点与批评对他进行难以言喻的刺激，结果偏偏在一分钟前刚告诫过他（农场主本人）别犯的错上原样犯了一遍——这在他看来，是他平生所闻天道报应最精彩的范例，他险些中风而死，以示嘉许。

记分员表示了如下意见：法尼这回可是干下好事了。

===Original===
“’Ere,” he said, inserting a finger in the cut to display its dimensions. “Look ’ere. This’ll mean a noo cloth, young feller me lad. That’s wot this’ll mean. That’ll be three pound we will trouble you for, if *you* please.”

Farnie produced his sole remaining sovereign.

“All I’ve got,” he said. “I’ll leave my name and address.”

“Don’t you trouble, young feller me lad,” said the marker, who appeared to be a very aggressive and unpleasant sort of character altogether, with meaning, “I know yer name and I knows yer address. Today fortnight at the very latest, if *you* please. You don’t want me to ’ave to go to your master about it, now, do yer? What say? No. Ve’well then. Today fortnight is the time, and you remember it.”

===Chinese===
「瞧这儿，」他说着，把一根手指戳进破口里以展示其尺寸，「你自个儿瞧瞧。这可得换块新台呢喽，小伙子俺的哥。就是这么回事儿。那就得麻烦您掏三镑喽，您呐。」

法尼掏出他仅剩的那枚金镑。

「就这些了。」他说，「我可以留下姓名地址。」

「您就不用操那份心喽，小伙子俺的哥，」记分员意味深长地说。此人浑身上下透着一股咄咄逼人的讨厌劲儿。「您的大名俺知道，府上的地址俺也知道。最迟两星期后的今天，您呐。您总不想俺为这个去找你们的先生们吧，啊？怎么样？不想吧。那敢情好。两星期后的今天就是期限，您给俺记着。」

===Original===
What was left of Farnie then rode slowly back to Beckford. Why he went to Monk on his return probably he could not have explained himself. But he did go, and, having told his story in full, wound up by asking for a loan of two pounds. Monk’s first impulse was to refer him back to a previous interview, when matters had been the other way about, that small affair of the pound on the second morning of the term. Then there flashed across his mind certain reasons against this move. At present Farnie’s attitude towards him was unpleasantly independent. He made him understand that he went about with him from choice, and that there was to be nothing of the patron and dependant about their alliance. If he were to lend him the two pounds now, things would alter. And to have got a complete hold over Master Reginald Farnie, Monk would have paid more than two pounds. Farnie had the intelligence to carry through anything, however risky, and there were many things which Monk would have liked to do, but, owing to the risks involved, shirked doing for himself. Besides, he happened to be in funds just now.

“Well, look here, old chap,” he said, “let’s have strict business between friends. If you’ll pay me back four quid at the end of term, you shall have the two pounds. How does that strike you?”

===Chinese===
随后，法尼剩下的那点儿元气慢吞吞地骑车回了贝克福德。回来后他为什么去找蒙克，恐怕连他自己也解释不清。但他确实去了，而且把事情原原本本讲完之后，末了开口要借两镑。蒙克的第一个冲动，是请他回顾先前那场谈话——那时情形正好倒过来，即学期第二天早晨那桩一镑的小事。随即他脑子里闪过几条反对这么做的理由。眼下法尼对他的态度独立得叫人不痛快。法尼让他明白：同他来往是出于自愿，两人的结盟容不得半点恩主与依附的意味。倘若现在借给他这两镑，情形就会改观。而为了把雷金纳德·法尼少爷彻底攥在手心，蒙克出比两镑更高的价钱都愿意。法尼有的是把任何事——多险都行——办到底的脑子，而有许多事蒙克很乐意做，却因风险太大，不愿亲自动手。何况他这会儿手头恰好宽裕。

「这样，听我说，老伙计，」他说，「咱们朋友归朋友，账要算得清清楚楚。学期结束时你还我四镑，这两镑就归你。这条件你觉得如何？」

===Original===
It struck Farnie, as it would have struck most people, that if this was Monk’s idea of strict business, there were the makings of no ordinary financier in him. But to get his two pounds he would have agreed to anything. And the end of term seemed a long way off.

The awkward part of the billiard-playing episode was that the punishment for it, if detected, was not expulsion, but flogging. And Farnie resembled the lady in *The Ingoldsby Legends* who “didn’t mind death, but who couldn’t stand pinching.” He didn’t mind expulsion—he was used to it, but he could *not* stand flogging.

“That’ll be all right,” he said. And the money changed hands.

===Chinese===
这条件给法尼的印象——正如会给大多数人的印象一样——是：倘若这就是蒙克所谓的账目分明，那此人身上着实具备一位非同寻常的金融家的素质。不过为了拿到那两镑，什么条件他都肯答应。何况学期结束看起来还遥远得很。

打台球这桩事不好办的地方在于：一旦败露，惩罚不是开除，而是鞭笞。而法尼很像《英戈尔兹比传奇》（The Ingoldsby Legends）里那位「不怕死、就怕掐」的女士。开除他不在乎——惯了；鞭笞他可爱不了。

「就这么办。」他说。于是钱易了主。

## X In Which a Case Is Fully Discussed / 十、本案得到充分讨论

===Original===
Gethryn was right in thinking that the interviews would be unpleasant. They increased in unpleasantness in arithmetical progression, until they culminated finally in a terrific encounter with the justly outraged Norris.

Reece was the first person to institute inquiries, and if everybody had resembled him, matters would not have been so bad for Gethryn. Reece possessed a perfect genius for minding his own business. The dialogue when they met was brief.

“Hullo,” said Reece.

“Hullo,” said the Bishop.

===Chinese===
盖斯林（Gethryn）认为那些问话将不会愉快，他没想错。问话的糟糕程度按等差级数递增，最后在一场与义愤填膺（且义愤得理直气壮）的诺里斯（Norris）的可怕交锋中达到顶点。

里斯（Reece）是第一个发起查问的人。倘若人人都像他，盖斯林的处境还不至于那么糟。里斯在「各人自扫门前雪」这件事上天赋异禀。两人碰面时的对话十分简短。

「哟。」里斯说。

「哟。」主教说。

===Original===
“Where did you get to yesterday?” said Reece.

“Oh, I had to go somewhere,” said the Bishop vaguely.

“Oh? Pity. Wasn’t a bad match.” And that was all the comment Reece made on the situation.

Gethryn went over to the chapel that morning with an empty sinking feeling inside him. He was quite determined to offer no single word of explanation, and he felt that that made the prospect all the worse. There was a vast uncertainty in his mind as to what was going to happen. Nobody could actually do anything to him, of course. It would have been a decided relief to him if anybody had tried that line of action, for moments occur when the only thing that can adequately soothe the wounded spirit, is to hit straight from the shoulder at someone. The punching-ball is often found useful under these circumstances. As he was passing Jephson’s House he nearly ran into somebody who was coming out.

“Be firm, my moral pecker,” thought Gethryn, and braced himself up for conflict.

===Chinese===
「你昨天跑哪儿去了？」里斯问。

「哦，我有点事要去个地方。」主教含含糊糊地说。

「哦？可惜。比赛不坏。」这就是里斯对整个事态的全部评论。

那天早晨去教堂的路上，盖斯林心里空落落地直往下沉。他打定了主意，一个字的解释也不给，而他又觉得这只会让前景愈发不妙。将要发生什么，他心里全无把握。当然，谁也不能真把他怎么样。倒不如真有人朝他动手，反而能让他痛快些——人有时候受了内伤，唯一足以抚平创伤的，就是照准某人结结实实来上一拳。这种时候，拳击吊球往往能派上用场。他路过杰夫森宿舍（Jephson’s）时，差点儿跟一个正往外走的人撞个满怀。

「沉住气，我的胆气。」盖斯林想着，绷紧全身准备应战。

===Original===
“Well, Gethryn?” said Mr. Jephson.

The question “well?” especially when addressed by a master to a boy, is one of the few questions to which there is literally no answer. You can look sheepish, you can look defiant, or you can look surprised according to the state of your conscience. But anything in the way of verbal response is impossible.

Gethryn attempted no verbal response.

“Well, Gethryn,” went on Mr. Jephson, “was it pleasant up the river yesterday?”

Mr. Jephson always preferred the rapier of sarcasm to the bludgeon of abuse.

===Chinese===
「怎么样，盖斯林？」杰夫森先生（Mr. Jephson）说。

「怎么样？」这个问题，尤其是老师对学生发出时，属于屈指可数的几种字面上无从作答的问题。你可以摆出讪讪的脸，可以摆出桀骜的脸，也可以视良心的状况摆出惊讶的脸，但任何言语上的回应都绝无可能。

盖斯林未作任何言语回应。

「怎么样，盖斯林，」杰夫森先生接着说，「昨天在河上愉快吗？」

杰夫森先生向来宁用讥讽的细剑，不用辱骂的大棒。

===Original===
“Yes, sir,” said Gethryn, “very pleasant.” He did not mean to be massacred without a struggle.

“What!” cried Mr. Jephson. “You actually mean to say that you did go up the river?”

“No, sir.”

“Then what do you mean?”

“It is always pleasant up the river on a fine day,” said Gethryn.

===Chinese===
「是的，先生，」盖斯林说，「非常愉快。」他可不打算束手就屠。

「什么！」杰夫森先生叫道，「你的意思是说，你当真去了河上？」

「没有，先生。」

「那你是什么意思？」

「晴天的时候，河上向来是很愉快的。」盖斯林说。

===Original===
His opponent, to use a metaphor suitable to a cricket master, changed his action. He abandoned sarcasm and condescended to direct inquiry.

“Where were you yesterday afternoon?” he said.

The Bishop, like Mr. Hurry Bungsho Jabberjee, B.A., became at once the silent tomb.

“Did you hear what I said, Gethryn?” (icily). “Where were you yesterday afternoon?”

“I can’t say, sir.”

===Chinese===
用一句适合板球教师的比喻来说，他的对手换了投球姿势。他抛开讥讽，屈尊降贵地改用直截了当的盘问。

「你昨天下午在哪儿？」他说。

主教立刻像那位文学士赫里·邦格舒·贾伯吉先生（Mr. Hurry Bungsho Jabberjee, B.A.）一样，化作一座缄默的坟墓。

「你听见我的话了吗，盖斯林？」（语气冷冰冰的）「你昨天下午在哪儿？」

「我说不出，先生。」

===Original===
These words may convey two meanings. They may imply ignorance, in which case the speaker should be led gently off to the nearest asylum. Or they may imply obstinacy. Mr. Jephson decided that in the present case obstinacy lay at the root of the matter. He became icier than ever.

“Very well, Gethryn,” he said, “I shall report this to the Headmaster.”

And Gethryn, feeling that the conference was at an end, proceeded on his way.

===Chinese===
这句话可以有两种含义。可能意味着不知情——倘若如此，说话人就该被轻手轻脚送去最近的精神病院了；也可能意味着顽固。杰夫森先生断定，眼下这桩案子的病根在于顽固。他变得比先前更加冷若冰霜。

「很好，盖斯林，」他说，「我要把这件事报告校长。」

盖斯林觉得会议已经结束，便自顾上路了。

===Original===
After chapel there was Norris to be handled. Norris had been rather late for chapel that morning, and had no opportunity of speaking to the Bishop. But after the service was over, and the School streamed out of the building towards their respective houses, he waylaid him at the door, and demanded an explanation. The Bishop refused to give one. Norris, whose temper never had a chance of reaching its accustomed tranquillity until he had consumed some breakfast—he hated early morning chapel—raved. The Bishop was worried, but firm.

“Then you mean to say—you don’t mean to say—I mean, you don’t intend to explain?” said Norris finally, working round for the twentieth time to his original text.

“I can’t explain.”

“You won’t, you mean.”

“Yes. I’ll apologize if you like, but I won’t explain.”

Norris felt the strain was becoming too much for him.

===Chinese===
礼拜之后，还有诺里斯要应付。诺里斯那天早晨上礼拜堂迟到了，没逮着机会跟主教说话。但仪式一结束、全校人潮涌出教堂奔向各自宿舍时，他在门口截住了主教，要求解释。主教拒绝解释。诺里斯的脾气，在吃下早餐之前从来得不到恢复惯常平静的机会——他恨透了清早的礼拜——于是大发雷霆。主教心里烦恼，立场却很坚定。

「那你的意思是——你不是想说——我是说，你不打算解释了？」诺里斯终于说道，第二十次绕回他的正题。

「我解释不了。」

「你是说，你不愿解释。」

「对。你若要道歉，我可以道歉，但解释我不给。」

诺里斯觉得这压力实在越来越超负荷了。

===Original===
“Apologize!” he moaned, addressing circumambient space. “Apologize! A man cuts off in the middle of the M.C.C. match, loses us the game, and then comes back and offers to apologize.”

“The offer’s withdrawn,” put in Gethryn. “Apologies and explanations are both off.” It was hopeless to try and be conciliatory under the circumstances. They did not admit of it.

Norris glared.

“I suppose,” he said, “you don’t expect to go on playing for the First after this? We can’t keep a place open for you in the team on the off chance of your not having a previous engagement, you know.”

“That’s your affair,” said the Bishop, “you’re captain. Have you finished your address? Is there anything else you’d like to say?”

Norris considered, and, as he went in at Jephson’s gate, wound up with this Parthian shaft—

===Chinese===
「道歉！」他对着周遭的虚空哀叹道，「道歉！一个人在 M.C.C. 比赛中途开溜，断送了我们整场球，回来居然还提出要道歉。」

「这个提议撤销了。」盖斯林插话说，「道歉与解释一并不给了。」在这种形势下还想息事宁人是无望的，形势不许可。

诺里斯怒目而视。

「我猜，」他说，「你别指望这以后还能进一队打球吧？我们总不能在队里给你留着位子，指望你碰巧没有先前的约会吧。」

「那是你的事，」主教说，「你是队长。你的训话完了没有？还有什么要说的吗？」

诺里斯思量了一下，走进杰夫森宿舍大门时，甩出这支帕提亚式的回马箭——

===Original===
“All I can say is that you’re not fit to be at a public school. They ought to sack a chap for doing that sort of thing. If you’ll take my advice, you’ll leave.”

About two hours afterwards Gethryn discovered a suitable retort, but, coming to the conclusion that better late than never does not apply to repartees, refrained from speaking it.

It was Mr. Jephson’s usual custom to sally out after supper on Sunday evenings to smoke a pipe (or several pipes) with one of the other Housemasters. On this particular evening he made for Robertson’s, which was one of the four Houses on the opposite side of the School grounds. He could hardly have selected a better man to take his grievance to. Mr. Robertson was a long, silent man with grizzled hair, and an eye that pierced like a gimlet. He had the enviable reputation of keeping the best order of any master in the School. He was also one of the most popular of the staff. This was all the more remarkable from the fact that he played no games.

===Chinese===
「我只能说，你不配待在公学里。干出这种事的人就该开除。你要是听我的劝，就自己滚蛋。」

大约两小时后，盖斯林想出了一句合适的回敬，但转念想到「迟总比不迟好」这条道理不适用于俏皮话，便忍住没说。

星期天晚饭后出门找哪位舍监抽烟斗（或者一连几斗），是杰夫森先生的惯例。这个特定的晚上，他去了罗伯森宿舍（Robertson’s）——校园另一侧四座宿舍之一。要倾诉委屈，他几乎挑不出比这更好的人选。罗伯森先生（Mr. Robertson）是个瘦长沉默的人，头发花白，一双眼睛像手钻一样钻人。他享有全校教师中最会维持秩序的令人艳羡的名声，也是教职员中最得人心者之一。鉴于他不参加任何运动，这一点就愈发难能可贵了。

===Original===
To him came Mr. Jephson, primed to bursting point with his grievance.

“Anything wrong, Jephson?” said Mr. Robertson.

“Wrong? I should just think there was. Did you happen to be looking at the match yesterday, Robertson?”

Mr. Robertson nodded.

“I always watch School matches. Good match. Norris missed a bad catch in the slips. He was asleep.”

===Chinese===
杰夫森先生找上门来，满腹委屈充到了爆点。

「出什么事了吗，杰夫森？」罗伯森先生说。

「出事？可不嘛。你昨天碰巧看比赛了吗，罗伯森？」

罗伯森先生点点头。

「校队比赛我向来都看。好比赛。诺里斯在滑区漏了一个必接之球。他睡着了。」

===Original===
Mr. Jephson conceded the point. It was trivial.

“Yes,” he said, “he should certainly have held it. But that’s a mere detail. I want to talk about Gethryn. Do you know what he did yesterday? I never heard of such a thing in my life, never. Went off during the luncheon interval without a word, and never appeared again till lockup. And now he refuses to offer any explanation whatever. I shall report the whole thing to Beckett. I told Gethryn so this morning.”

“I shouldn’t,” said Mr. Robertson; “I really think I shouldn’t. Beckett finds the ordinary duties of a Headmaster quite sufficient for his needs. This business is not in his province at all.”

===Chinese===
杰夫森先生认下了这一点。那只是小事。

「是啊，」他说，「那球他当然该接住。不过那只是细枝末节。我要谈的是盖斯林。你知道他昨天干了什么吗？我平生从未听说过这种事，从未。午饭间歇一声不吭地走了，直到锁门才再露面。现在他又拒绝作出任何解释。我要把整件事报告给贝克特（Beckett）。我今天早晨已经这么警告过盖斯林了。」

「要是我，就不报。」罗伯森先生说，「我真的认为不该报。贝克特觉得校长的日常公务已经够他消受的了。这档子事根本不归他管。」

===Original===
“Not in his province? My dear sir, what is a headmaster for, if not to manage affairs of this sort?”

Mr. Robertson smiled in a sphinx-like manner, and answered, after the fashion of Socrates, with a question.

“Let me ask you two things, Jephson. You must proceed gingerly. Now, firstly, it is a headmaster’s business to punish any breach of school rules, is it not?”

“Well?”

“And school prefects do not attend roll-call, and have no restrictions placed upon them in the matter of bounds?”

“No. Well?”

===Chinese===
「不归他管？我亲爱的先生，校长要是连这种事都不管，还要他做什么？」

罗伯森先生斯芬克斯式地微微一笑，并以苏格拉底的方式，用一个问题作答。

「让我问你两件事，杰夫森。这种事可得谨慎着来。首先，惩罚任何违反校规的行为，是校长的职责，对不对？」

「怎么样？」

「而级长不参加点名，在越界问题上也不受任何限制，对不对？」

「对。怎么样？」

===Original===
“Then perhaps you’ll tell me what School rule Gethryn has broken?” said Mr. Robertson.

“You see you can’t,” he went on. “Of course you can’t. He has not broken any School rule. He is a prefect, and may do anything he likes with his spare time. He chooses to play cricket. Then he changes his mind and goes off to some unknown locality for some reason at present unexplained. It is all perfectly legal. Extremely quaint behaviour on his part, I admit, but thoroughly legal.”

“Then nothing can be done,” exclaimed Mr. Jephson blankly. “But it’s absurd. Something must be done. The thing can’t be left as it is. It’s preposterous!”

“I should imagine,” said Mr. Robertson, “from what small knowledge I possess of the Human Boy, that matters will be made decidedly unpleasant for the criminal.”

“Well, I know one thing; he won’t play for the team again.”

===Chinese===
「那么，也许你能告诉我，盖斯林违反了哪条校规？」罗伯森先生说。

「你看，你说不出来，」他接着说，「当然说不出来。他没违反任何校规。他是级长，课余时间想干什么就可以干什么。他选择打板球，后来改了主意，出于某种目前尚未解释的原因去了某个不为人知的去处。这一切完全合法。行为古怪至极，我承认，但彻头彻尾合法。」

「那就没办法治他了，」杰夫森先生茫然失措地叫道，「可这太荒唐了。总得想个办法。这件事不能就这么撂着。岂有此理！」

「依我对『人类男孩』这种生物的一知半解，」罗伯森先生说，「我猜想，那位罪人今后的日子会相当不好过。」

「哼，有一件事我知道：他别想再进队打球了。」

===Original===
“There is something very refreshing about your logic, Jephson. Because a boy does not play in one match, you will not let him play in any of the others, though you admit his absence weakens the team. However, I suppose that is unavoidable. Mind you, I think it is a pity. Of course Gethryn has some explanation, if he would only favour us with it. Personally I think rather highly of Gethryn. So does poor old Leicester. He is the only Head-prefect Leicester has had for the last half-dozen years who knows even the rudiments of his business. But it’s no use my preaching his virtues to you. You wouldn’t listen. Take another cigar, and let’s talk about the weather.”

Mr. Jephson took the proffered weed, and the conversation, though it did not turn upon the suggested topic, ceased to have anything to do with Gethryn.

The general opinion of the School was dead against the Bishop. One or two of his friends still clung to a hope that explanations might come out, while there were also a few who always made a point of thinking differently from everybody else. Of this class was Pringle. On the Monday after the match he spent the best part of an hour of his valuable time reasoning on the subject with Lorimer. Lorimer’s vote went with the majority. Although he had fielded for the Bishop, he was not, of course, being merely a substitute, allowed to bowl, as the Bishop had had his innings, and it had been particularly galling to him to feel that he might have saved the match, if it had only been possible for him to have played a larger part.

===Chinese===
「你的逻辑真叫人耳目一新，杰夫森。因为一个男孩一场比赛没打，你就不许他打其余任何一场，尽管你承认他的缺阵削弱了球队。不过我想这也是在所难免。说真的，我觉得很可惜。盖斯林当然有他的解释，只要他肯赏光告诉我们。就我个人而言，我对盖斯林评价相当高。可怜的老莱斯特（Leicester）也是。他是近六七年来莱斯特宿舍唯一一位连本职门道都懂的舍长。不过我向你宣讲他的美德也是白费口舌，你听不进去。再来一支雪茄，咱们聊聊天气吧。」

杰夫森先生接过递来的烟卷。谈话虽未转到提议的话题上去，却不再与盖斯林有任何干系了。

全校的舆论一面倒地反对主教。他的一两个朋友还抱着解释终会大白的希望，另外也总有那么几位，一向以与众不同为处世准则。普林格尔（Pringle）即属此类。比赛后的星期一，他把自己宝贵时光中足足将近一个小时花在了同洛里默（Lorimer）推究这个问题上。洛里默的票投给了多数派。虽然他替主教守过场，但既然只是替补，自然不许投球——主教的击球局已经打过了；一想到只要能让他多上场一分，自己兴许就能挽救这场比赛，他就格外窝火。

===Original===
“It’s no good jawing about it,” he said, “there isn’t a word to say for the man. He hasn’t a leg to stand on. Why, it would be bad enough in a House or form match even, but when it comes to first matches—!” Here words failed Lorimer.

“Not at all,” said Pringle, unmoved. “There are heaps of reasons, jolly good reasons, why he might have gone away.”

“Such as?” said Lorimer.

“Well, he might have been called away by a telegram, for instance.”

“What rot! Why should he make such a mystery of it if that was all?”

“He’d have explained all right if somebody had asked him properly. You get a chap like Norris, who, when he loses his hair, has got just about as much tact as a rhinoceros, going and ballyragging the man, and no wonder he won’t say anything. I shouldn’t myself.”

===Chinese===
「这事再嚼舌头也没用，」他说，「替这人连半个字都说不出口。他毫无站得住脚的地方。咳，这种事就算是宿舍赛、班级赛也够恶劣的了，可要是摊到一队赛上——！」话到此，洛里默语塞了。

「未必，」普林格尔无动于衷地说，「他可能离开的原因多得很，而且都是顶呱呱的好理由。」

「比如？」洛里默说。

「嗯，比方说，他可能是被一封电报叫走的。」

「胡扯！要是真那么简单，他干吗弄得这么神神秘秘？」

「要是有人好好问他，他早就解释了。偏偏碰上诺里斯这么一号人——他一急起来，随机应变的本事跟犀牛差不多——跑去对人家横加痛骂，那人家当然什么都不肯说了。换了我也不说。」

===Original===
“Well, go and talk to him decently, then. Let’s see you do it, and I’ll bet it won’t make a bit of difference. What the chap has done is to go and get himself mixed up in some shady business somewhere. That’s the only thing it can be.”

“Rot,” said Pringle, “the Bishop isn’t that sort of chap.”

“You can’t tell. I say,” he broke off suddenly, “have you done that poem yet?”

Pringle started. He had not so much as begun that promised epic.

“I—er—haven’t quite finished it yet. I’m thinking it out, you know. Getting a sort of general grip of the thing.”

===Chinese===
「那好啊，你就去好好地跟他说说。倒要看看你去说，能顶什么用。我敢打赌一点儿用都没有。这家伙干的事，就是跑到哪儿卷进了什么见不得人的勾当。只可能是这一种解释。」

「胡说，」普林格尔说，「主教不是那种人。」

「这你可说不准。我说，」他猛地转了话头，「你那首诗写完了没有？」

普林格尔吓了一跳。那首许诺的史诗，他连开头都还没开。

「我——呃——还没完全写完。你知道，我正在构思。先把这个东西的整体框架琢磨出来。」

===Original===
“Oh. Well, I wish you’d buck up with it. It’s got to go in tomorrow week.”

“Tomorrow week. Tuesday the what? Twenty-second, isn’t it? Right. I’ll remember. Two days after the O.B.s’ match. That’ll fix it in my mind. By the way, your people are going to come down all right, aren’t they? I mean, we shall have to be getting in supplies and so on.”

“Yes. They’ll be coming. There’s plenty of time, though, to think of that. What you’ve got to do for the present is to keep your mind glued on the death of Dido.”

“Rather,” said Pringle, “I won’t forget.”

This was at six twenty-two p.m. By the time six-thirty boomed from the College clock-tower, Pringle was absorbing a thrilling work of fiction, and Dido, her death, and everything connected with her, had faded from his mind like a beautiful dream.

===Chinese===
「哦。那我希望你抓紧。下下星期二就得交了。」

「下下星期二。几号星期二来着？二十二号，是吧？好。我记住了。O.B.（老贝克福德校友）赛两天之后。这下忘不掉了。对了，你家里人到时候一定下来，是吧？我是说，我们得开始采办吃食什么的了。」

「来，他们会来。不过那还有的是时间考虑。你眼下该做的，是把心思牢牢粘在狄多（Dido）之死上。」

「包管不忘，」普林格尔说，「忘不了。」

这是下午六点二十二分的事。等到学院钟楼敲响六点半，普林格尔已沉浸在一部惊险小说之中，而狄多、她的死，以及与她有关的一切，早已像一场美梦似的从他脑海里消散了。

## II Introduces an Unusual Uncle / 二、介绍一位不同寻常的叔叔

===Original===
On the following day, at nine o’clock, the term formally began. There is nothing of Black Monday about the first day of term at a public school. Black Monday is essentially a private school institution.

At Beckford the first day of every term was a half holiday. During the morning a feeble pretence of work was kept up, but after lunch the school was free, to do as it pleased and to go where it liked. The nets were put up for the first time, and the School professional emerged at last from his winter retirement with his, “Coom *right* out to ’em, sir, right forward,” which had helped so many Beckford cricketers to do their duty by the School in the field. There was one net for the elect, the remnants of last year’s Eleven and the “probables” for this season, and half a dozen more for lesser lights.

===Chinese===
第二天九点，学期正式开始。公学开学第一天里没有半点「黑色星期一」的意味。黑色星期一从根本上说，是私立走读学校的特产。

在贝克福德（Beckford），每学期第一天都是半天假。上午还勉强装出一副上课的样子，午饭之后全校便自由了，爱干什么干什么，爱去哪儿去哪儿。击球网第一次支了起来，校队的职业教练终于结束他的冬季蛰居，重新露面，嘴里念着他那句「可劲儿往球上迎喽，先生，可劲儿往前送喽」——多少年来，正是这句话帮着一代代贝克福德球手在球场上为学校尽了本分。有一张网是给精英们留的，即去年一队的余部和本赛季的「有望入选者」；另外六张归亮度较低的星星们用。

===Original===
At the first net Norris was batting to the bowling of Gosling, a long, thin day boy, Gethryn, and the professional—as useful a trio as any school batsman could wish for. Norris was captain of the team this year, a sound, stylish bat, with a stroke after the manner of Tyldesley between cover and mid-off, which used to make Miles the professional almost weep with joy. But today he had evidently not quite got into form. Twice in successive balls Gosling knocked his leg stump out of the ground with yorkers, and the ball after that, Gethryn upset his middle with a beauty.

“Hat-trick, Norris,” shouted Gosling.

“Can’t see ’em a bit today. Bowled, Bishop.”

===Chinese===
第一张网里，诺里斯（Norris）正对着三人的投球练击球：戈斯林（Gosling）——一个瘦长条的走读生——盖斯林（Gethryn），还有职业教练——随便哪个校级击球手想要这么一套称手的三角组合，也就莫过于此了。诺里斯今年是队长，击球稳健而有派头，在掩护位与中前卫之间有一手蒂尔德斯利（Tyldesley）式的打法，常令职业教练迈尔斯（Miles）喜得几乎落泪。可今天他显然还没进入状态。戈斯林接连两球用约克球把他的腿侧柱打飞出地，紧接着下一球，盖斯林又一记妙球掀翻了他的中间柱。

「帽子戏法喽，诺里斯。」戈斯林嚷道。

「今天压根儿看不见球。投杀得漂亮，主教。」

===Original===
A second teaser from Gethryn had almost got through his defence. The Bishop was undoubtedly a fine bowler. Without being quite so fast as Gosling, he nevertheless contrived to work up a very considerable speed when he wished to, and there was always something in every ball he bowled which made it necessary for the batsman to watch it all the way. In matches against other schools it was generally Gosling who took the wickets. The batsmen were bothered by his pace. But when the M.C.C. or the Incogniti came down, bringing seasoned county men who knew what fast bowling really was, and rather preferred it on the whole to slow, then Gethryn was called upon.

===Chinese===
盖斯林又一记刁球险些穿透他的防守。主教无疑是一位优秀的投球手。他虽不及戈斯林那么快，却也能在愿意的时候攒起相当可观的速度，而且他投出的每一球里总有点名堂，逼得击球手必须目送它走完全程。与别校比赛时，斩获得分柱的一般是戈斯林，击球手们被他的球速搅得心烦意乱。但等 M.C.C. 或者「无名氏队」（the Incogniti）莅临、带来那些深知快速投球为何物、而且总体上更偏爱快球胜过慢球的老练郡队球手时，就该盖斯林登场了。

===Original===
Most Beckfordians who did not play cricket on the first day of term went on the river. A few rode bicycles or strolled out into the country in couples, but the majority, amongst whom on this occasion was Marriott, sallied to the water and hired boats. Marriott was one of the six old cricket colours—the others were Norris, Gosling, Gethryn, Reece, and Pringle of the School House—who formed the foundation of this year’s Eleven. He was not an ornamental bat, but stood quite alone in the matter of tall hitting. Twenty minutes of Marriott when in form would often completely alter the course of a match. He had been given his colours in the previous year for making exactly a hundred in sixty-one minutes against the Authentics when the rest of the team had contributed ninety-eight. The Authentics made a hundred and eighty-four, so that the School just won; and the story of how there were five men out in the deep for him, and how he put the slow bowler over their heads and over the ropes eight times in three overs, had passed into a school legend.

But today other things than cricket occupied his attention. He had run Wilson to earth, and was engaged in making his acquaintance, according to instructions received.

===Chinese===
开学第一天不打板球的贝克福德人，多数都上了河。少数人骑自行车，或成双成对地遛达到乡间去，但大多数人——这回马里奥特（Marriott）也在其中——都涌到水边雇船。马里奥特是六名保有队色的老队员之一——其余五位是诺里斯、戈斯林、盖斯林、里斯，以及学校宿舍的普林格尔（Pringle）——他们是今年一队的班底。他击球不算好看，但在高打高击这件事上独步全校。状态好的马里奥特打上二十分钟，常常能彻底扭转一场比赛的走向。他的队色是去年得的：对「正宗队」（the Authentics）一战，他恰好在六十一分钟里打满一百分，而队里其他人合计贡献了九十八分。正宗队拿下一百八十四分，学校队险胜；至于对方如何布下五名深区野手对付他、他又如何在三个轮次里八次把慢速投球手的球打过这五人的头顶送出界绳，这一段佳话早已载入校内传说。

可今天占据他心神的不是板球。他把威尔逊（Wilson）逮了个正着，正依照接到的指示，着手与他结识。

===Original===
“Are you Wilson?” he asked. “P. V. Wilson?”

Wilson confirmed the charge.

“My name’s Marriott. Does that convey any significance to your young mind?”

“Oh, yes. My mater knows somebody who knows your aunt.”

“It is a true bill.”

===Chinese===
「你是威尔逊吗？」他问，「P. V. 威尔逊？」

威尔逊供认不讳。

「我叫马里奥特。这几个字在你年轻的心里激起任何涟漪了吗？」

「哦，有。我老妈认识一个人，那人认识你姑妈。」

「情报属实。」

===Original===
“And she said you would look after me. I know you won’t have time, of course.”

“I expect I shall have time to give you all the looking after you’ll require. It won’t be much, from all I’ve heard. Was all that true about you and young Skinner?”

Wilson grinned.

“I did have a bit of a row with a chap called Skinner,” he admitted.

“So Skinner seems to think,” said Marriott. “What was it all about?”

“Oh, he made an ass of himself,” said Wilson vaguely.

Marriott nodded.

===Chinese===
「她还说你会照应我。我知道你当然不会有空的。」

「我估计，你要多少照应，我的时间都够用。据我所知，你需要的照应不会多。你跟小斯金纳（Skinner）那档子事，都是真的吗？」

威尔逊咧嘴一笑。

「我确实跟一个叫斯金纳的家伙小小吵了一架。」他承认道。

「斯金纳也是这么觉得的。」马里奥特说，「到底为什么事？」

「哦，他自己蠢呗。」威尔逊含糊地说。

马里奥特点点头。

===Original===
“He would. I know the man. I shouldn’t think you’d have much trouble with Skinner in the future. By the way, I’ve got you for a fag this term. You don’t have to do much in the summer. Just rot around, you know, and go to the shop for biscuits and things, that’s all. And, within limits of course, you get the run of the study.”

“I see,” said Wilson gratefully. The prospect was pleasant.

“Oh yes, and it’s your privilege to pipe-clay my cricket boots occasionally before First matches. You’ll like that. Can you steer a boat?”

“I don’t think so. I never tried.”

“It’s easy enough. I’ll tell you what to do. Anyhow, you probably won’t steer any worse than I row, so let’s go and get a boat out, and I’ll try and think of a few more words of wisdom for your benefit.”

===Chinese===
「他准会。这人我了解。我看你往后跟斯金纳不会有什么大麻烦。对了，这学期你归我使唤。夏天用不着干多少活，就是闲逛逛，去小卖部跑跑腿买点饼干什么的，仅此而已。而且，当然有分寸——书房随你进出。」

「明白了。」威尔逊感激地说。这前景颇为宜人。

「哦对了，一队比赛前，偶尔用白泥给我擦擦板球靴，也是你的殊荣。你会喜欢这活儿的。会掌船舵吗？」

「大概不会。从来没试过。」

「容易得很。我会教你怎么弄。反正你掌舵多半不会比我划桨更糟，走，咱们去雇条船，我再想想还能为你憋出几句金玉良言来。」

===Original===
At the nets Norris had finished his innings, and Pringle was batting in his stead. Gethryn had given up his ball to Baynes, who bowled slow leg-breaks, and was the most probable of the probables above-mentioned. He went to where Norris was taking off his pads, and began to talk to him. Norris was the head of Jephson’s House, and he and the Bishop were very good friends, in a casual sort of way.
