「喂，威廉，」马里奥特说，「你好吗？还在慢慢熬着？那可真是万幸。我说，你听着，我想跟当局进一句及时的忠言。他们一定知道我今天晚上回来。他们当然知道。嗐，他们还专门写信请我回来呢。那么，红地毯在哪儿？遮阳棚在哪儿？本该到车站迎接我的铜管乐队在哪儿？什么在哪儿都找不着。我告诉你吧，威廉，我的老伙伴，校长要是再不留神他的言行，苦日子就要来了。他必须懂得，人生是严峻的，人生是认真的，威廉。盖斯林回来了没有？」

===Original===
William, who had been gasping throughout this harangue, for the intellectual pressure of Marriott’s conversation (of which there was always plenty) was generally too much for him, caught thankfully at the last remark as being the only intelligible one uttered up to present date, and made answer﻿—

“Mr. Gethryn ’e’s gorn out on to the field, Mr. Marriott. ’E come ’arf an hour ago.”

“Oh! Right. Thanks. Goodbye, William. Give my respects to the cook, and mind you don’t work too hard. Think what it would be if you developed heart disease. Awful! You mustn’t do it, William.”

Marriott vanished, and William, slightly dazed, went about his professional duties once more. Marriott walked out into the grounds in search of Gethryn. Gethryn was the head of Leicester’s this term, *vice* Reynolds departed, and Marriott, who was second man up, shared a study with him. Leicester’s had not a good name at Beckford, in spite of the fact that it was generally in the running for the cricket and football cups. The fact of the matter was that, with the exception of Gethryn, Marriott, a boy named Reece, who kept wicket for the School Eleven, and perhaps two others, Leicester’s seniors were not a good lot. To the School in general, who gauged a fellow’s character principally by his abilities in the cricket and football fields, it seemed a very desirable thing to be in Leicester’s. They had been runners-up for the House football cup that year, and this term might easily see the cricket cup fall to them. Amongst the few, however, it was known that the House was passing through an unpleasant stage in its career. A House is either good or bad. It is seldom that it can combine the advantages of both systems. Leicester’s was bad.

===Chinese===
马里奥特这篇宏论从头到尾听得威廉直倒气——马里奥特谈话的脑力压强（向来充足）一般而言总叫他消受不起——末尾这句总算唯一听得明白，他赶紧抓住，答道——

「盖斯林先生他上球场去咧，马里奥特先生。他半拉钟头前来的。」

「哦！好。谢了。再见，威廉。替我向厨娘致意，还有，千万别太卖力气。想想看，你要是得了心脏病可怎么好。可怕！可不能这么干，威廉。」

马里奥特一闪而没，威廉微微发懵，重又干他的本职去了。马里奥特走到庭园里找盖斯林。本学期盖斯林是莱斯特宿舍的头儿，接替卸任的雷诺兹（Reynolds），而马里奥特是二号人物，与他合用一间书房。莱斯特宿舍在贝克福德名声不佳，尽管它在板球杯和足球杯的争夺中通常都在候选之列。问题在于，除盖斯林、马里奥特、一个名叫里斯（Reece）的——学校一队的守门员——或许再加另外两人之外，莱斯特宿舍的高年级生不是一批好料。对全校大多数人而言——他们衡量一个人的人品，主要看他在板球场和足球场上的能耐——进莱斯特宿舍似乎是件极美的事。那年他们拿了宿舍足球杯的亚军，这学期板球杯也很可能落到他们手里。然而在知情的少数人中间，大家都知道这宿舍正经历着一段不体面的时期。一个宿舍要么好，要么坏，难得能兼具两种体制的长处。莱斯特宿舍是坏的。

===Original===
This was due partly to a succession of bad Head-prefects, and partly to Leicester himself, who was well-meaning but weak. His spirit was willing, but his will was not spirited. When things went on that ought not to have gone on, he generally managed to avoid seeing them, and the things continued to go on. Altogether, unless Gethryn’s rule should act as a tonic, Leicester’s was in a bad way.

The Powers that Be, however, were relying on Gethryn to effect some improvement. He was in the Sixth, the First Fifteen, and the First Eleven. Also a backbone was included in his anatomy, and if he made up his mind to a thing, that thing generally happened.

The Rev. James Beckett, the Headmaster of Beckford, had formed a very fair estimate of Gethryn’s capabilities, and at the moment when Marriott was drawing the field for the missing one, that worthy was sitting in the Headmaster’s study with a cup in his right hand and a muffin (half-eaten) in his left, drinking in tea and wisdom simultaneously. The Head was doing most of the talking. He had led up to the subject skilfully, and, once reached, he did not leave it. The text of his discourse was the degeneracy of Leicester’s.

“Now, you know, Gethryn﻿—another muffin? Help yourself. You know, Reynolds﻿—well, he was a capital boy in his way, capital, and I’m sure we shall all miss him very much﻿—*but* he was not a good head of a House. He was weak. Much too weak. Too easygoing. You must avoid that, Gethryn. Reynolds.﻿ ﻿…” And much more in the same vein. Gethryn left the room half an hour later full of muffins and good resolutions. He met Marriott at the fives-courts.

===Chinese===
这一部分要归咎于接连几任不济事的首席级长，一部分归咎于莱斯特本人，他心是好的，人却软弱。他精神上是乐意的，可他的意志没有精神。遇到不该继续下去的事情，他总能有法子视而不见，于是那些事情继续了下去。总而言之，除非盖斯林的治理能起强心剂的作用，莱斯特宿舍的日子不好过。

然而，当权的诸位把改善的希望寄托在盖斯林身上。他在六年级、足球一队和板球一队。而且他的骨架里生来装有脊梁骨，他一旦拿定主意要办一件事，那件事多半就办成了。

贝克福德的校长、可敬的詹姆斯·贝克特牧师（the Rev. James Beckett），对盖斯林的能耐早已有一份相当公允的估计。当马里奥特正在球场上为那失踪的一位搜遍全场时，这位可敬的人正坐在校长书房里，右手一杯茶，左手一个吃了一半的松饼，茶水与智慧同时吸纳。讲话的主要是校长。他巧妙地把话题引到了正题上，一旦抵达，便不再离开。他这篇宏文的主题是莱斯特宿舍的堕落。

「你瞧，盖斯林——再来一个松饼？自己动手。你知道，雷诺兹——唔，他按他那套来说是个顶好的孩子，顶好，我确信我们大家都会非常想念他——*可是*他不是当宿舍头儿的好材料。他软弱。太软弱了。太好说话。你可千万别这样，盖斯林。雷诺兹……」以及同一调门的大篇后续。半小时后盖斯林离开房间，满肚子松饼，满脑子决心。他在墙手球场遇见了马里奥特。

===Original===
“Where have you been to?” asked Marriott. “I’ve been looking for you all over the shop.”

“I and my friend the Headmaster,” said Gethryn, “have been having a quiet pot of tea between us.”

“Really? Was he affable?”

“Distinctly affable.”

“You know,” said Marriott confidentially, “he asked me in, but I told him it wasn’t good enough. I said that if he would consent to make his tea with water that wasn’t two degrees below lukewarm, and bring on his muffins cooked instead of raw, and supply some butter to eat with them, I might look him up now and then. Otherwise it couldn’t be done at the price. But what did he want you for, really?”

===Chinese===
「你上哪儿去了？」马里奥特问。「我满世界找你。」

「我和我这位校长朋友，」盖斯林说，「刚私下共享了一壶茶。」

「真的？他和气吗？」

「和气极了。」

「你知道，」马里奥特推心置腹地说，「他也请了我，可我告诉他不够格。我说，只要他肯答应沏茶别用只差两度不到温吞的水，端上来的松饼别是生的而是烤熟的，再配点黄油佐餐，我或许偶尔去拜访拜访他。否则这个价钱免谈。不过，他找你到底干什么？」

===Original===
“He was ragging me about the House. Quite right, too. You know, there’s no doubt about it, Leicester’s does want bucking up.”

“We’re going to get the cricket cup,” said Marriott, for the defence.

“We may. If it wasn’t for the Houses in between. School House and Jephson’s especially. And anyhow, that’s not what I meant. The games are all right. It’s﻿—”

“The moral *je-ne-sais-quoi*, so to speak,” said Marriott. “That’ll be all right. Wait till we get at ’em. What I want you to turn your great brain to now is this letter.”

He produced a letter from his pocket. “Don’t you bar chaps who show you their letters?” he said. “This was written by an aunt of mine. I don’t want to inflict the whole lot on you. Just look at line four. You see what she says: ‘A boy is coming to Mr. Leicester’s House this term, whom I particularly wish you to befriend. He is the son of a great friend of a friend of mine, and is a nice, bright little fellow, very jolly and full of spirits.’ ”

===Chinese===
「他为宿舍的事训了我一顿。训得也对。你知道，莱斯特宿舍确实需要打打气，这没什么可怀疑的。」

「我们要拿板球杯的，」马里奥特为辩护方发言。

「也许吧。若不是中间隔着那几个宿舍的话。尤其是学校宿舍（School House）和杰夫森宿舍（Jephson's）。可不管怎么说，我不是那个意思。比赛是没问题的。问题是——」

「也就是所谓道德上的*只可意会*，」马里奥特说，「那个不成问题。等咱们下手就知道了。现在我要你那颗伟大的脑袋来对付的，是这封信。」

他从口袋里掏出一封信。「你最烦给人看信的家伙，对吧？」他说。「这是我一位姑妈写的。我不想拿全文折磨你。只看第四行。你瞧她写的：『本学期有个男孩要来莱斯特先生的宿舍，我特别希望你对他多多关照。他是我一位朋友的好友之子，是个讨人喜欢、机灵的小家伙，快快活活，精力充沛。』」

===Original===
“That means,” interpolated Gethryn grimly, “that he is up to the eyes in pure, undiluted cheek, and will want kicking after every meal and before retiring to rest. Go on.”

“His name is﻿—”

“Well?”

“That’s the point. At this point the manuscript becomes absolutely illegible. I have conjectured Percy for the first name. It may be Richard, but I’ll plunge on Percy. It’s the surname that stumps me. Personally, I think it’s MacCow, though I trust it isn’t, for the kid’s sake. I showed the letter to my brother, the one who’s at Oxford. He swore it was Watson, but, on being pressed, hedged with Sandys. You may as well contribute your little bit. What do you make of it?”

Gethryn scrutinized the document with care.

===Chinese===
「这意思是说，」盖斯林冷冷插话，「这家伙的无礼纯粹是不掺假的浓缩货，得每顿饭后外加就寝前各踢一脚。接着念。」

「他的名字是——」

「嗯？」

「问题就出在这儿。写到这儿，手稿变得完全无法辨认。我猜教名是珀西（Percy）。也可能叫理查德（Richard），不过我赌珀西。让我犯难的是姓。依我看是麦考（MacCow），不过看这孩子的分上，我盼望不是。我把信拿给我那位在牛津的哥哥看了。他咬定是沃森（Watson），可一再追问之下，又改口含糊成桑迪斯（Sandys）。你也不妨贡献一点高见。你看是什么？」

盖斯林仔细审视了这份文件。

===Original===
“She begins with a D. You can see that.”

“Well?”

“Next letter a or u. I see. Of course. It’s Duncan.”

“Think so?” said Marriott doubtfully. “Well, let’s go and ask the matron if she knows anything about him.”

“Miss Jones,” he said, when they had reached the House, “have you on your list of new boys a sportsman of the name of MacCow or Watson? I am also prepared to accept Sandys or Duncan. The Christian name is either Richard or Percy. There, that gives you a fairly wide field to choose from.”

===Chinese===
「开头是个 D。这个你看得出来。」

「嗯？」

「第二个字母是 a 或者 u。我明白了。当然是。是邓肯（Duncan）。」

「是吗？」马里奥特将信将疑。「那好，咱们去问问宿舍女管家，看她知道点什么。」

「琼斯小姐（Miss Jones），」到了宿舍，他说，「你的新生名单上，有没有一位姓麦考或者沃森的运动员？桑迪斯或邓肯我也照收。教名不是理查德就是珀西。好了，这下你挑的余地相当宽了。」

===Original===
“There’s a P. V. Wilson on the list,” said the matron, after an inspection of that document.

“That must be the man,” said Marriott. “Thanks very much. I suppose he hasn’t arrived yet?”

“No, not yet. You two are the only ones so far.”

“Oh! Well, I suppose I shall have to see him when he does come. I’ll come down for him later on.”

They strolled out on to the field again.

===Chinese===
「名单上有个 P. V. 威尔逊（P. V. Wilson）。」女管家查过那份名单后说。

「准是这位了，」马里奥特说。「多谢。想必他还没到吧？」

「对，还没到。到现在为止就你们俩。」

「哦！那等他真来了，我总得去见见。回头我再下来等他。」

两人又踱回球场。

===Original===
“In *re* the proposed bucking-up of the House,” said Marriott, “it’ll be rather a big job.”

“Rather. I should think so. We ought to have a most fearfully sporting time. It’s got to be done. The Old Man talked to me like several fathers.”

“What did he say?”

“Oh, heaps of things.”

“I know. Did he mention amongst other things that Reynolds was the worst idiot on the face of this so-called world?”

“Something of the sort.”

===Chinese===
「关于给宿舍打气的既定方针，」马里奥特说，「这可是桩相当大的工程。」

「可不是。我也这么想。咱们该有一段极其够劲的痛快时光。这活儿非干不可。老头子跟我讲话的架势，赶上好几个亲爹了。」

「他说什么了？」

「哦，一大堆东西。」

「我知道。他有没有顺带提到，雷诺兹是这个所谓世界上头一号的大笨蛋？」

「诸如此类吧。」

===Original===
“So I should think. The late Reynolds was a perfect specimen of the gelatine-backboned worm. That’s not my own, but it’s the only description of him that really suits. Monk and Danvers and the mob in general used to do what they liked with him. Talking of Monk, when you embark on your tour of moral agitation, I should advise you to start with him.”

“Yes. And Danvers. There isn’t much to choose between them. It’s a pity they’re both such good bats. When you see a chap putting them through the slips like Monk does, you can’t help thinking there must be something in him.”

“So there is,” said Marriott, “and it’s all bad. I bar the man. He’s slimy. It’s the only word for him. And he uses scent by the gallon. Thank goodness this is his last term.”

“Is it really? I never heard that.”

“Yes. He and Danvers are both leaving. Monk’s going to Heidelberg to study German, and Danvers is going into his pater’s business in the City. I got that from Waterford.”

===Chinese===
「我也这么想。已故的雷诺兹是凝胶脊梁虫的完美标本。这话不是我发明的，可只有这个描述才真正贴切。蒙克（Monk）、丹弗斯（Danvers）还有那帮乌合之众，从前想把他怎么样就怎么样。说到蒙克，等你着手进行道德鼓动巡回之时，我建议你从他开刀。」

「对，还有丹弗斯。他俩半斤八两。可惜这俩偏都是那么好的击球手。看到有人像蒙克那样把球一记记穿透门后守场区，你没法不觉得此人身上总有点什么。」

「是有点什么，」马里奥特说，「而且全不是好东西。我看不惯这人。他黏糊。这个词专属他。而且他用香水论加仑地用。谢天谢地，这是他最后一学期了。」

「真的？我可从没听说。」

「真的。他和丹弗斯都要走。蒙克要去海德堡（Heidelberg）学德语，丹弗斯要进城进他老爸的公司。这是沃特福德（Waterford）告诉我的。」

===Original===
“Waterford is another beast,” said Gethryn thoughtfully. “I suppose he’s not leaving by any chance?”

“Not that I know of. But he’ll be nothing without Monk and Danvers. He’s simply a sort of bottle-washer to the firm. When they go he’ll collapse. Let’s be strolling towards the House now, shall we? Hullo! Our only Reece! Hullo, Reece!”

“Hullo!” said the new arrival. Reece was a weird, silent individual, whom everybody in the School knew up to a certain point, but very few beyond that point. His manner was exactly the same when talking to the smallest fag as when addressing the Headmaster. He rather gave one the impression that he was thinking of something a fortnight ahead, or trying to solve a chess problem without the aid of the board. In appearance he was on the short side, and thin. He was in the Sixth, and a conscientious worker. Indeed, he was only saved from being considered a swot, to use the vernacular, by the fact that from childhood’s earliest hour he had been in the habit of keeping wicket like an angel. To a good wicket-keeper much may be forgiven.

He handed Gethryn an envelope.

“Letter, Bishop,” he said. Gethryn was commonly known as the Bishop, owing to a certain sermon preached in the College chapel some five years before, in aid of the Church Missionary Society, in which the preacher had alluded at frequent intervals to another Gethryn, a bishop, who, it appeared, had a see, and did much excellent work among the heathen at the back of beyond. Gethryn’s friends and acquaintances, who had been alternating between “Ginger”﻿—Gethryn’s hair being inclined to redness﻿—and “Sneg,” a name which utterly baffles the philologist, had welcomed the new name warmly, and it had stuck ever since. And, after all, there are considerably worse names by which one might be called.

===Chinese===
「沃特福德是另一头畜生，」盖斯林若有所思地说。「他总不至于也要走了吧？」

「据我所知不会。可没了蒙克和丹弗斯，他就什么都不是了。他不过是那家字号里跑腿打杂的。他们一走，他就垮。咱们现在往宿舍溜达去，好不好？喂！我们独一无二的里斯！喂，里斯！」

「喂！」新来的人说。里斯是个古怪而沉默的家伙，全校人人都认识他到某一点为止，越过那一点还认识他的寥寥无几。他跟最小的学仆讲话，与向校长致辞，态度一模一样。他颇给人一种印象：不是在想两个星期以后的事，就是在不用棋盘解一道棋题。论外形，他偏矮偏瘦。他在六年级，是个用功而踏实的人。说实在的，若不是因为他从孩提最早期起便惯于像天使一般守三柱门，按行话讲，他差点就要被归为书呆子了。对一位好捕手，人们可以宽恕很多。

他递给盖斯林一个信封。

「信，主教。」他说。盖斯林通称「主教」（the Bishop），起因是五年前大学校教堂里为教会传道会（Church Missionary Society）募捐而讲的那篇道。讲道人在道中频频提及另一位盖斯林——一位主教——此人据称坐拥一个教区，在极偏远之地对异教徒做了大量出色的工作。盖斯林的亲友故旧们，此前一直在「金吉」（Ginger——盖斯林的头发偏红）和「斯内格」（Sneg——一个完全难倒语文学家的名字）之间摇摆不定，热烈欢迎了这个新名字，此后它便叫开了。再说了，天底下叫人难听得多的大有人在。

===Original===
“What the dickens!” he said, as he finished reading the letter.

“Tell us the worst,” said Marriott. “You must read it out now out of common decency, after rousing our expectations like that.”

“All right! It isn’t private. It’s from an aunt of mine.”

“Seems to be a perfect glut of aunts,” said Marriott. “What views has your representative got to air? Is *she* springing any jolly little fellow full of spirits on this happy community?”

“No, it’s not that. It’s only an uncle of mine who’s coming down here. He’s coming tomorrow, and I’m to meet him. The uncanny part of it is that I’ve never heard of him before in my life.”

===Chinese===
「搞什么名堂！」他读完信说。

「把最坏的消息招了吧，」马里奥特说。「你这样吊足了我们的胃口，出于起码的礼数，现在总得念出来。」

「行！不是什么私信。是我一位姑妈写来的。」

「看来姑妈真是供过于求，」马里奥特说。「你这位代言人要发表什么高见？*她*是不是也要往这个快乐的集体里塞一个快快活活、精力充沛的小家伙？」

「不，不是那回事。只是我一位叔叔要来这儿。他明天到，我得去接他。邪门的地方在于，我这辈子从没听说过这个人。」

===Original===
“That reminds me of a story I heard﻿—” began Reece slowly. Reece’s observations were not frequent, but when they came, did so for the most part in anecdotal shape. Somebody was constantly doing something which reminded him of something he had heard somewhere from somebody. The unfortunate part of it was that he exuded these reminiscences at such a leisurely rate of speed that he was rarely known to succeed in finishing any of them. He resembled those serial stories which appear in papers destined at a moderate price to fill an obvious void, and which break off abruptly at the third chapter, owing to the premature decease of the said periodicals. On this occasion Marriott cut in with a few sage remarks on the subject of uncles as a class. “Uncles,” he said, “are tricky. You never know where you’ve got ’em. You think they’re going to come out strong with a sovereign, and they make it a shilling without a blush. An uncle of mine once gave me a threepenny bit. If it hadn’t been that I didn’t wish to hurt his feelings, I should have flung it at his feet. Also I particularly wanted threepence at the moment. Is your uncle likely to do his duty, Bishop?”

===Chinese===
「这倒让我想起一个我听来的故事——」里斯慢吞吞地开讲。里斯的发言不勤，可一旦发言，大多以掌故的形态出现。总有人在做某件事，让他想起他从前从什么人那里听来的某件事。糟糕的是，他泄露这些回忆的速度闲散到那种地步，以致据知他极少成功讲完过其中任何一个。他酷似那些连载小说——登在立志以适中价格填补明显空白的报纸上，连载到第三章便因上述刊物的夭折而戛然中断。这回马里奥特横插进来，就叔叔这个阶层发表了几句审慎的高见。「叔叔，」他说，「是狡猾的。你永远摸不透他们的路数。你以为他们要慷慨解囊掏一枚沙弗林了，他们却面不改色地给一先令。我有位叔叔有回给了我一枚三便士硬币。要不是不愿伤他的感情，我差点把它摔在他脚边。再说，我当时恰好特别缺那三便士。你那位叔叔肯尽本分吗，主教？」

===Original===
“I tell you I don’t know the man. Never heard of him. I thought I knew every uncle on the list, but I can’t place this one. However, I suppose I shall have to meet him.”

“Rather,” said Marriott, as they went into the House; “we should always strive to be kind, even to the very humblest. On the off chance, you know. The unknown may have struck it rich in sheep or something out in Australia. Most uncles come from Australia. Or he may be the boss of some trust, and wallowing in dollars. He may be anything. Let’s go and brew, Bishop. Come on, Reece.”

“I don’t mind watching you two chaps eat,” said Gethryn, “but I can’t join in myself. I have assimilated three pounds odd of the Headmagisterial muffins already this afternoon. Don’t mind me, though.”

They went upstairs to Marriott’s study, which was also Gethryn’s. Two in a study was the rule at Beckford, though there were recluses who lived alone, and seemed to enjoy it.

===Chinese===
「我都说了我不认识这个人。从没听说过。我原以为名单上每个叔叔我都认得，可这位我对不上号。不过，我想我总得去接他。」

「那当然，」两人进宿舍时马里奥特说；「我们应当永远力求仁厚，哪怕对最微贱的人也是。万一呢，你懂的。那位无名氏说不定在澳洲靠羊还是什么发了大财。大多数叔叔都来自澳洲。要不然他就是哪家托拉斯的大老板，在美元里打滚。他什么都可能是。咱们沏茶去吧，主教。来，里斯。」

「看着你们俩吃我不介意，」盖斯林说，「可我自己加入不了。今天下午我已经吸纳了三磅出头的校长级松饼。不过别管我。」

他们上楼到马里奥特的书房，那也是盖斯林的。贝克福德的规矩是一间书房住两人，尽管也有独居的隐士，而且似乎乐在其中。

===Original===
When the festive board had ceased to groan, and the cake, which Marriott’s mother had expected to last a fortnight, had been reduced to a mere wreck of its former self, the thought of his aunt’s friend’s friend’s son returned to Marriott, and he went down to investigate, returning shortly afterwards unaccompanied, but evidently full of news.

“Well?” said Gethryn. “Hasn’t he come?”

“A little,” said Marriott, “just a little. I went down to the fags’ room, and when I opened the door I noticed a certain weird stillness in the atmosphere. There is usually a row going on that you could cut with a knife. I looked about. The room was apparently empty. Then I observed a quaint object on the horizon. Do you know one Skinner by any chance?”

“My dear chap!” said Gethryn. Skinner was a sort of juvenile Professor Moriarty, a Napoleon of crime. He reeked of crime. He revelled in his wicked deeds. If a Dormitory-prefect was kept awake at night by some diabolically ingenious contrivance for combining the minimum of risk with the maximum of noise, then it was Skinner who had engineered the thing. Again, did a master, playing nervously forward on a bad pitch at the nets to Gosling, the School fast bowler, receive the ball gaspingly in the small ribs, and look round to see whose was that raucous laugh which had greeted the performance, he would observe a couple of yards away Skinner, deep in conversation with some friend of equally villainous aspect. In short, in a word, the only adequate word, he was Skinner.

===Chinese===
等到盛宴的桌面不再呻吟，马里奥特母亲原指望撑两个星期的蛋糕已被糟蹋得只剩昔日尊容的一堆残骸，马里奥特想起了他姑妈的朋友的朋友的儿子，便下楼去侦查，不多时独自回来，显然满载新闻。

「怎么样？」盖斯林说。「他没来？」

「来了一点点，」马里奥特说，「就一点点。我下到学仆室，一开门就察觉气氛里有一种异样的静止。平常那儿的吵闹都能用刀切。我四下一看，屋里显然是空的。随后我望见地平线上一个古怪的物体。你碰巧认识一个姓斯金纳的吗？」

「亲爱的伙计！」盖斯林说。斯金纳（Skinner）是某种少年版的莫里亚蒂教授，犯罪界的拿破仑。他浑身散发罪恶的气息。他以自己的恶行为乐。倘若某位寝室级长夜里被某种鬼才的精巧装置吵得睡不着——那种装置把最小风险与最大噪音结合得恰到好处——那么这东西准是斯金纳设计的。又如某位教员在练习网上战战兢兢地朝前推球，球道又糟糕，被学校快球手戈斯林（Gosling）一球接一球气若游丝地砸进小肋骨，回头看那阵祝贺此番表演的沙哑大笑是谁发的，他就会看见两码开外的斯金纳，正与一位面目同样凶恶的朋友谈得入神。简言之，一言以蔽之，唯一贴切的词是：他就是斯金纳。

===Original===
“Well?” said Reece.

“Skinner,” proceeded Marriott, “was seated in a chair, bleeding freely into a rather dirty pocket-handkerchief. His usual genial smile was hampered by a cut lip, and his right eye was blacked in the most graceful and pleasing manner. I made tender inquiries, but could get nothing from him except grunts. So I departed, and just outside the door I met young Lee, and got the facts out of him. It appears that P. V. Wilson, my aunt’s friend’s friend’s son, entered the fags’ room at four-fifteen. At four-fifteen-and-a-half, punctually, Skinner was observed to be trying to rag him. Apparently the great Percy has no sense of humour, for at four-seventeen he got tired of it, and hit Skinner crisply in the right eyeball, blacking the same as per illustration. The subsequent fight raged gorily for five minutes odd, and then Wilson, who seems to be a professional pugilist in disguise, landed what my informant describes as three corkers on his opponent’s proboscis. Skinner’s reply was to sit down heavily on the floor, and give him to understand that the fight was over, and that for the next day or two his face would be closed for alterations and repairs. Wilson thereupon harangued the company in well-chosen terms, tried to get Skinner to shake hands, but failed, and finally took the entire crew out to the shop, where they made pigs of themselves at his expense. I have spoken.”

===Chinese===
「然后呢？」里斯说。

「斯金纳，」马里奥特继续道，「当时坐在一把椅子里，正往一块相当脏的手帕里大出血。他那惯常的亲切微笑因嘴唇破了个口子而受阻，右眼则以最优雅、最悦目的方式乌青了一大片。我温柔地询问，可只换来几声咕哝。于是我撤离，刚出门就撞见小李（Lee），从他嘴里套出了全部事实。事情的经过是：P. V. 威尔逊，我姑妈的朋友的朋友的儿子，于四点一刻进入学仆室。四点一刻过半分，分秒不差，有人目睹斯金纳企图作弄他。显然，伟大的珀西毫无幽默感，因为到四点十七分他腻烦了，干净利索地一拳打在斯金纳的右眼珠上，将其打成如上所述的乌青。随后的大战血战了五分钟有零，接着威尔逊——他似乎是位化了装的职业拳击手——照我这位情报员的说法，朝对手的鼻子结结实实擂了三记重炮。斯金纳的回应是沉重地坐到地板上，让他明白战斗已经结束，而且未来一两天内他的脸将停止营业，进行改装整修。威尔逊随即用字斟句酌的言辞向全体在场者发表了演说，试着让斯金纳握手，未果，最后带着全体人马去了小卖部，让他们花他的钱大吃大嚼了个痛快。吾言毕矣。」

===Original===
“And that’s the kid you’ve got to look after,” said Reece, after a pause.

“Yes,” said Marriott. “What I maintain is that I require a kid built on those lines to look after me. But you ought to go down and see Skinner’s eye sometime. It’s a beautiful bit of work.”

===Chinese===
「这就是你要照看的那个小孩，」半晌，里斯说。

「是啊，」马里奥特说。「我的主张是，该照看人的是我，得给我弄个照这个路数打造的小孩。不过你们哪天该下去看看斯金纳的眼睛。那是一件漂亮的作品。」

## IX The Bishop Finishes His Ride / 九 主教结束了他的骑行

===Original===
Gethryn had started on his ride handicapped by two things. He did not know his way after the first two miles, and the hedges at the roadside had just been clipped, leaving the roads covered with small thorns.

It was the former of these circumstances that first made itself apparent. For two miles the road ran straight, but after that it was unexplored country. The Bishop, being in both cricket and football teams, had few opportunities for cycling. He always brought his machine to School, but he very seldom used it.

===Chinese===
盖斯林上路之时背负着两重不利。一是出了头两英里他便不认得路，二是路边的树篱刚修剪过，路面上落满了小刺。

先显形的是前者。头两英里道路笔直，再往前便是未勘之地了。「主教」身兼板球、足球两队队员，骑车的机会寥寥。他每学期总把车带到学校，却极少骑用。

===Original===
At the beginning of the unexplored country, an irresponsible person recommended him to go straight on. He couldn’t miss the road, said he. It was straight all the way. Gethryn thanked him, rode on, and having gone a mile came upon three roads, each of which might quite well have been considered a continuation of the road on which he was already. One curved gently off to the right, the other two equally gently to the left. He dismounted and the feelings of gratitude which he had borne towards his informant for his lucid directions vanished suddenly. He gazed searchingly at the three roads, but to single out one of them as straighter than the other two was a task that baffled him completely. A signpost informed him of three things. By following road one he might get to Brindleham, and ultimately, if he persevered, to Corden. Road number two would lead him to Old Inns, whatever they might be, with the further inducement of Little Benbury, while if he cast in his lot with road three he might hope sooner or later to arrive at Much Middlefold-on-the-Hill, and Lesser Middlefold-in-the-Vale. But on the subject of Anfield and Anfield Junction the board was silent.

Two courses lay open to him. Should he select a route at random, or wait for somebody to come and direct him? He waited. He went on waiting. He waited a considerable time, and at last, just as he was about to trust to luck, and make for Much Middlefold-on-the-Hill, a figure loomed in sight, a slow-moving man, who strolled down the Old Inns road at a pace which seemed to argue that he had plenty of time on his hands.

===Chinese===
在未勘之地的入口处，一位不负责任的先生指点他一直往前骑。他说，这条路你错不了。一路都是直的。盖斯林道了谢，继续前行，骑出一英里后，眼前出现了三条路，每一条都完全有资格算作他脚下这条路的延伸。一条朝右边缓缓弯过去，另外两条以同样和缓的弧度弯向左边。他下了车，先前对那位指点者清晰指路所怀的感激之情霎时烟消云散。他细细端详这三条路，可要从中挑出一条比另外两条更直的来，这个任务彻底难倒了他。一块路牌向他通报了三件事：沿第一条路走，可以到布林德尔汉（Brindleham），而且只要坚持到底，最终能到科登（Corden）。第二条路会把他引向老客栈村（Old Inns）——不管那是个什么所在——外加小本伯里（Little Benbury）的进一步诱惑；而若把命运押在第三条路上，他可望迟早抵达山上大米德尔福德（Much Middlefold-on-the-Hill）和谷中小米德尔福德（Lesser Middlefold-in-the-Vale）。可关于安菲尔德（Anfield）和安菲尔德枢纽站（Anfield Junction），这块牌子缄口不言。

面前摆着两条路。是随便挑一条走，还是等人来指点？他等。接着等。等了相当久，最后，正当他打算听天由命、朝山上大米德尔福德进发时，一个人影映入眼帘——一个慢吞吞的人，以一副仿佛手头时间大把的步子，沿老客栈村那条路踱了下来。

===Original===
“I say, can you tell me the way to Anfield, please?” said the Bishop as he came up.

The man stopped, apparently rooted to the spot. He surveyed the Bishop with a glassy but determined stare from head to foot. Then he looked earnestly at the bicycle, and finally, in perfect silence, began to inspect the Bishop again.

“Eh?” he said at length.

“Can you tell me the way to Anfield?”

“Anfield?”

“Yes. How do I get there?”

===Chinese===
「我说，请问您能告诉我去安菲尔德怎么走吗？」「主教」迎上去说。

那人站住，仿佛在地上扎了根。他目光呆滞而坚决，把「主教」从头到脚打量了一番。然后他恳切地端详那辆自行车，最后，在一片完满的寂静中，又开始检视「主教」本人。

「啊？」半晌他说。

「您能告诉我去安菲尔德的路吗？」

「安菲尔德？」

「对。我怎么才能到那儿？」

===Original===
The man perpended, and when he replied did so after the style of the late and great Ollendorf.

“Old Inns,” he said dreamily, waving a hand down the road by which he had come, “be over there.”

“Yes, yes, I know,” said Gethryn.

“Was born at Old Inns, I was,” continued the man, warming to his subject. “Lived there fifty-five years, I have. Yeou go straight down the road an’ yeou cam t’ Old Inns. Yes, that be the way t’ Old Inns.”

Gethryn nobly refrained from rending the speaker limb from limb.

“I don’t want to know the way to Old Inns,” he said desperately. “Where I want to get is Anfield. Anfield, you know. Which way do I go?”

===Chinese===
那人沉思半晌，作答时取法于已故的伟人奥伦多夫（Ollendorf）。

「老客栈村，」他悠悠地说，一只手朝他来时的路一挥，「在那边儿。」

「是，是，我知道，」盖斯林说。

「俺就是在老客栈村生的，俺是，」那人接着说，谈兴渐浓。「俺在那儿住了五十五年，俺住了。你顺着这条路一直走，你就到老客栈咧。对咧，去老客栈就是这么个走法。」

盖斯林以贵族气度克制住了把说话人大卸八块的冲动。

「我不想知道去老客栈村的路，」他孤注一掷地说，「我要去的是安菲尔德。安菲尔德，明白吗。我该走哪条道？」

===Original===
“Anfield?” said the man. Then a brilliant flash of intelligence illumined his countenance. “Whoy, Anfield be same road as Old Inns. Yeou go straight down the road, an’﻿—”

“Thanks very much,” said Gethryn, and without waiting for further revelations shot off in the direction indicated. A quarter of a mile farther he looked over his shoulder. The man was still there, gazing after him in a kind of trance.

===Chinese===
「安菲尔德？」那人说。随即一道智慧的光芒照亮了他的脸。「嗬，安菲尔德跟老客栈是一条道。你顺着这条路一直走，然后——」

「多谢了，」盖斯林说，不等更多天启降下，便朝所指的方向射了出去。四分之一英里之后他回头一望。那人还站在原地，神情恍惚地目送着他。

===Original===
The Bishop passed through Old Inns with some way on his machine. He had much lost time to make up. A signpost bearing the legend “Anfield four miles” told him that he was nearing his destination. The notice had changed to three miles and again to two, when suddenly he felt that jarring sensation which every cyclist knows. His back tyre was punctured. It was impossible to ride on. He got off and walked. He was still in his cricket clothes, and the fact that he had on spiked boots did not make walking any the easier. His progress was not rapid.

Half an hour before his one wish had been to catch sight of a fellow-being. Now, when he would have preferred to have avoided his species, men seemed to spring up from nowhere, and every man of them had a remark to make or a question to ask about the punctured tyre. Reserve is not the leading characteristic of the average yokel.

===Chinese===
「主教」一路带速穿过老客栈村。他耽误的时间太多，得补回来。一块写着「安菲尔德四英里」的路牌告诉他，目的地近了。告示先后换成三英里、又换成两英里，突然，每个骑车人都熟识的那种震动感传了上来。他的后胎爆了。没法再骑。他下车推行。他还穿着板球衣，脚上的钉鞋也丝毫没让走路变得轻松。他的行进速度不快。

半小时前，他唯一的愿望是望见一个同类。如今他巴不得躲开人类，男人们却仿佛从地缝里冒出来，而且人人都要对那条爆胎发表一句评论或提出一个问题。缄默并非寻常乡巴佬的主导性格。

===Original===
Gethryn, however, refused to be drawn into conversation on the subject. At last one, more determined than the rest, brought him to bay.

“Hoy, mister, stop,” called a voice. Gethryn turned. A man was running up the road towards him.

He arrived panting.

“What’s up?” said the Bishop.

“You’ve got a puncture,” said the man, pointing an accusing finger at the flattened tyre.

===Chinese===
然而盖斯林拒绝被拖入有关此事的交谈。终于有一个比其余人更执着的，把他逼到了墙角。

「喂，先生，站住，」一个声音喊道。盖斯林回头。一个人正沿路跑来。

他跑到跟前，直喘气。

「什么事？」「主教」说。

「你的车爆胎了，」那人伸出一根控诉的手指，指着瘪掉的轮胎说。

===Original===
It was not worth while killing the brute. Probably he was acting from the best motives.

“No,” said Gethryn wearily, “it isn’t a puncture. I always let the air out when I’m riding. It looks so much better, don’t you think so? Why did they let you out? Goodbye.”

And feeling a little more comfortable after this outburst, he wheeled his bicycle on into Anfield High Street.

===Chinese===
犯不着宰了这畜生。他多半是出于一片好意。

「不，」盖斯林疲惫地说，「不是爆胎。我骑车向来把气放掉。这样看着体面多了，你觉不觉得？他们怎么把你放出来的？再见。」

这一通发作之后心里舒坦了些，他推着自行车进了安菲尔德的大街。

===Original===
Minds in the village of Anfield worked with extraordinary rapidity. The first person of whom he asked the way to the Junction answered the riddle almost without thinking. He left his machine out in the road and went on to the platform. The first thing that caught his eye was the station clock with its hands pointing to five past four. And when he realized that, his uncle’s train having left a clear half hour before, his labours had all been for nothing, the full bitterness of life came home to him.

He was turning away from the station when he stopped. Something else had caught his eye. On a bench at the extreme end of the platform sat a youth. And a further scrutiny convinced the Bishop of the fact that the youth was none other than Master Reginald Farnie, late of Beckford, and shortly, or he would know the reason why, to be once more of Beckford. Other people besides himself, it appeared, could miss trains.

===Chinese===
安菲尔德村里的人脑子转动极快。他向头一个问路的人打听去枢纽站怎么走，那人对这条谜语几乎不假思索便给出了答案。他把车撂在路边，径直走上站台。第一样撞进眼帘的东西便是车站大钟，指针指着四点过五分。待他意识到，叔叔的火车早在整整半小时前就开走了，他这番辛劳全都白费，人生全部的苦涩一齐涌上心头。

他正要转身离开车站，忽然停住了。另一样东西撞进了眼帘。站台最尽头的一条长凳上坐着一个少年。再细看一眼，「主教」便确信，那少年不是别人，正是雷金纳德·法尼小少爷——不久前还在贝克福德，而不久之后——否则他就得知道个缘故——便又要回到贝克福德。看来除他之外，别人也赶得上误火车。

===Original===
Farnie was reading one of those halfpenny weeklies which﻿—with a nerve which is the only creditable thing about them﻿—call themselves comic. He did not see the Bishop until a shadow falling across his paper caused him to look up.

It was not often that he found himself unequal to a situation. Monk in a recent conversation had taken him aback somewhat, but his feelings on that occasion were not to be compared with what he felt on seeing the one person whom he least desired to meet standing at his side. His jaw dropped limply, *Comic Blitherings* fluttered to the ground.

The Bishop was the first to speak. Indeed, if he had waited for Farnie to break the silence, he would have waited long.

===Chinese===
法尼正在读那种半便士的周刊——这类刊物以一股胆气自称「喜剧」，那股胆气也是它们身上唯一可取之处。直到一道影子落在报纸上，他才看见「主教」。

他很少有力不从心的场面。蒙克最近一次谈话颇让他吃了一惊，可他当时的感受，比起看见自己最不想遇见的人站在身边时的感受，实在不值一提。他的下巴颓然垂下，《瞎扯漫画》（*Comic Blitherings*）飘然落地。

「主教」先开的口。说实在的，他若等法尼来打破沉默，怕是要等到地老天荒。

===Original===
“Get up,” he said. Farnie got up.

“Come on.” Farnie came.

“Go and get your machine,” said Gethryn. “Hurry up. And now you will jolly well come back to Beckford, you little beast.”

===Chinese===
「站起来。」他说。法尼站了起来。

「走吧。」法尼走了。

「去把你的车取来，」盖斯林说。「快点。这下你乖乖给我回贝克福德去，你这个小畜生。」

===Original===
But before that could be done there was Gethryn’s back wheel to be mended. This took time. It was nearly half past four before they started.

“Oh,” said Gethryn, as they were about to mount, “there’s that money. I was forgetting. Out with it.”

Ten pounds had been the sum Farnie had taken from the study. Six was all he was able to restore. Gethryn enquired after the deficit.

“I gave it to Monk,” said Farnie.

===Chinese===
可在这之前，盖斯林的后轮还得先补好。这费了些工夫。等他们上路，已近四点半。

「哦，」盖斯林跨上车前说，「还有那笔钱。我差点忘了。掏出来。」

法尼从书房拿走的总数是十镑。能归还的只有六镑。盖斯林追问差额的下落。

「我给蒙克了，」法尼说。

===Original===
To Gethryn, in his present frame of mind, the mere mention of Monk was sufficient to uncork the vials of his wrath.

“What the blazes did you do that for? What’s Monk got to do with it?”

“He said he’d get me sacked if I didn’t pay him,” whined Farnie.

This was not strictly true. Monk had not said. He had hinted. And he had hinted at flogging, not expulsion.

“Why?” pursued the Bishop. “What had you and Monk been up to?”

===Chinese===
对此刻心境的盖斯林来说，单是提到蒙克这个名字，便足以拔开他怒火的瓶塞。

「你干那个到底是图什么？蒙克跟这事有什么相干？」

「他说我要是不给他钱，就设法把我轰出去，」法尼哭腔哭调地说。

这话不完全属实。蒙克没有明说，只是暗示过。而且他暗示的是鞭刑，不是开除。

「为什么？」「主教」追问。「你和蒙克干了什么勾当？」

===Original===
Farnie, using his out-of-bounds adventures as a foundation, worked up a highly artistic narrative of doings, which, if they had actually been performed, would certainly have entailed expulsion. He had judged Gethryn’s character correctly. If the matter had been simply a case for a flogging, the Bishop would have stood aside and let the thing go on. Against the extreme penalty of School law he felt bound as a matter of family duty to shield his relative. And he saw a bad time coming for himself in the very near future. Either he must expose Farnie, which he had resolved not to do, or he must refuse to explain his absence from the M.C.C. match, for by now there was not the smallest chance of his being able to get back in time for the visitors’ innings. As he rode on he tried to imagine what would happen in consequence of that desertion, and he could not do it. His crime was, so far as he knew, absolutely without precedent in the School history.

As they passed the cricket field he saw that it was empty. Stumps were usually drawn early in the M.C.C. match if the issue of the game was out of doubt, as the Marylebone men had trains to catch. Evidently this had happened today. It might mean that the School had won easily﻿—they had looked like making a big score when he had left the ground﻿—in which case public opinion would be more lenient towards him. After a victory a school feels that all’s well that ends well. But it might, on the other hand, mean quite the reverse.

===Chinese===
法尼以他越界游荡的历险为底本，加工出一段高度艺术性的事迹叙述——那些事若果真干过，肯定要吃开除。他把盖斯林的品性估摸对了。倘若事情仅仅是一桩该打鞭子的案子，「主教」本会袖手旁观，任它发展。可面对校规的极刑，他觉得自己有家族义务，非得庇护这位亲戚不可。同时他也看到，自己紧日子眼看就要来了。他要么揭发法尼——这他已打定主意不干——要么就拒绝解释自己在 M.C.C. 比赛中的缺席，因为事到如今，赶回去参加客队局次的可能性已微乎其微。骑行路上，他试着想象这场开小差会有什么后果，可想不出来。据他所知，他的罪案在学校史上绝对空前。

经过板球场时，他看见场子空了。M.C.C. 比赛中，若胜负已无悬念，通常早早拔桩收场，因为玛丽勒本众将要赶火车。今天显然就是这么办的。这或许意味着学校队轻松获胜——他离场时他们正像要刷出大分的样子——果真如此，舆论对他会宽容些。打了胜仗，学校便觉得结局好一切都好。可反过来，这也可能意味着截然相反的局面。

===Original===
He put his machine up, and hurried to the study. Several boys, as he passed them, looked curiously at him, but none spoke to him.

Marriott was in the study, reading a book. He was still in flannels, and looked as if he had begun to change but had thought better of it. As was actually the case.

“Hullo,” he cried, as Gethryn appeared. “Where the dickens have you been all the afternoon? What on earth did you go off like that for?”

“I’m sorry, old chap,” said the Bishop, “I can’t tell you. I shan’t be able to tell anyone.”

“But, man! Try and realize what you’ve done. Do you grasp the fact that you’ve gone and got the School licked in the M.C.C. match, and that we haven’t beaten the M.C.C. for about a dozen years, and that if you’d been there to bowl we should have walked over this time? Do try and grasp the thing.”

===Chinese===
他把车放好，匆匆赶回书房。路过的几个男孩好奇地看他，却没有一个跟他说话。

马里奥特在书房里读书。他还穿着法兰绒球衣，看样子像刚要换衣服又改变了主意。事实也确是如此。

「喂，」盖斯林一露面他便嚷道，「你整个下午到底死哪儿去了？你干嘛那样一走了之？」

「对不起，老伙计，」「主教」说，「我不能告诉你。我谁也没法告诉。」

「可是，老兄！你倒是想想你干下的事啊。你明不明白，你这一走，让学校在 M.C.C. 比赛里输了，而我们打败 M.C.C. 差不多还是十几年前的事了，而且你要是在场投球，这回我们本可以轻松碾压的？你倒是明白明白这事儿啊。」

===Original===
“Did they win?”

“Rather. By a wicket. Two wickets, I mean. We made 213. Your bowling would just have done it.”

Gethryn sat down.

“Oh Lord,” he said blankly, “this is awful!”

===Chinese===
「他们赢了？」

「赢了。差一个三柱门。我是说差两个。我们得了 213 分。你的投球本可正好拿下他们。」

盖斯林坐了下来。

「哦，天哪，」他茫然地说，「这可糟透了！」

===Original===
“But, look here, Bishop,” continued Marriott, “this is all rot. You can’t do a thing like this, and then refuse to offer any explanation, and expect things to go on just as usual.”

“I don’t,” said Gethryn. “I know there’s going to be a row, but I can’t explain. You’ll have to take me on trust.”

“Oh, as far as I am concerned, it’s all right,” said Marriott. “I know you wouldn’t be ass enough to do a thing like that without a jolly good reason. It’s the other chaps I’m thinking about. You’ll find it jolly hard to put Norris off, I’m afraid. He’s most awfully sick about the match. He fielded badly, which always makes him shirty. Jephson, too. You’ll have a bad time with Jephson. His one wish after the match was to have your gore and plenty of it. Nothing else would have pleased him a bit. And think of the chaps in the House, too. Just consider what a pull this gives Monk and his mob over you. The House’ll want some looking after now, I fancy.”

===Chinese===
「可是，听着，主教，」马里奥特接着说，「这也太岂有此理了。你不能干了这么一档子事，又拒绝作出任何解释，还指望一切照旧。」

「我没指望，」盖斯林说。「我知道准有一场风波，可我解释不了。你们只能信我这个人。」

「哦，就我而言，没关系，」马里奥特说。「我知道你不会蠢到无缘无故干这种事。我担心的是旁人。恐怕你很难把诺里斯那关打发过去。他为这场球窝了一肚子火。他守备守砸了，那总让他冒火。还有杰夫森。你在杰夫森那儿有苦头吃。赛后他唯一的愿望就是要你的血，多多益善。别的事一概不能让他开心。还有宿舍里那帮人，也替他们想想吧。想想这让蒙克和他那伙人抓了你多大的把柄。我看这宿舍往后需要好好看管了。」

===Original===
“And they’ll get it,” said Gethryn. “If Monk gives me any of his beastly cheek, I’ll knock his head off.”

But in spite of the consolation which such a prospect afforded him, he did not look forward with pleasure to the next day, when he would have to meet Norris and the rest. It would have been bad in any case. He did not care to think what would happen when he refused to offer the slightest explanation.

===Chinese===
「他们会得到看管的，」盖斯林说。「蒙克要是敢冲我使他那副讨厌的无礼劲儿，我拧下他的脑袋。」

然而，尽管这一前景给了他些许安慰，第二天要面对诺里斯和其余众人的场面，他依旧不觉得愉快。换哪种情形都够呛。他不敢去想，等他拒绝给出半句解释时会发生什么。

## VII The Bishop Goes for a Ride / 七 主教出门骑行

===Original===
The M.C.C. match opened auspiciously. Norris, for the first time that season, won the toss. Tom Brown, we read, in a similar position, “with the usual liberality of young hands,” put his opponents in first. Norris was not so liberal. He may have been young, but he was not so young as that. The sun was shining on as true a wicket as was ever prepared when he cried “Heads,” and the coin, after rolling for some time in diminishing circles, came to a standstill with the dragon undermost. And Norris returned to the Pavilion and informed his gratified team that, all things considered, he rather thought that they would bat, and he would be obliged if Baker would get on his pads and come in first with him.

===Chinese===
M.C.C. 之战开局吉利。诺里斯本赛季头一回猜对了硬币。据我们读到的记载，汤姆·布朗（Tom Brown）在类似处境下，「以年轻人惯常的慷慨」，让对手先攻。诺里斯可没这么慷慨。他年纪也许不大，可还没小到那个份上。他喊「正面」时，阳光正照在一条有史以来整备得最规矩的球道上；硬币打着越来越小的圈儿转了一阵，龙纹一面朝下停住。于是诺里斯回到凉棚，向他那欢欣的队伍宣布：通盘考虑，他倾向于本方击球，并请贝克系上护腿，随他首先出场。

===Original===
The M.C.C. men took the field﻿—O. T. Blackwell, by the way, had shrunk into a mere brother of the century-making A. T.﻿—and the two School House representatives followed them. An amateur of lengthy frame took the ball, a man of pace, to judge from the number of slips. Norris asked for “two leg.” An obliging umpire informed him that he had got two leg. The long bowler requested short slip to stand finer, swung his arm as if to see that the machinery still worked, and dashed wildly towards the crease. The match had begun.

There are few pleasanter or more thrilling moments in one’s school career than the first over of a big match. Pleasant, that is to say, if you are actually looking on. To have to listen to a match being started from the interior of a form-room is, of course, maddening. You hear the sound of bat meeting ball, followed by distant clapping. Somebody has scored. But who and what? It may be a four, or it may be a mere single. More important still, it may be the other side batting after all. Some miscreant has possibly lifted your best bowler into the road. The suspense is awful. It ought to be a School rule that the captain of the team should send a message round the form-rooms stating briefly and lucidly the result of the toss. Then one would know where one was. As it is, the entire form is dependent on the man sitting under the window. The form-master turns to write on the blackboard. The only hope of the form shoots up like a rocket, gazes earnestly in the direction of the Pavilion, and falls back with a thud into his seat. “They haven’t started yet,” he informs the rest in a stage whisper. “Si-*lence*,” says the form-master, and the whole business must be gone through again, with the added disadvantage that the master now has his eye fixed coldly on the individual nearest the window, your only link with the outer world.

===Chinese===
M.C.C. 众将入场——顺便一提，O. T. 布莱克维尔（O. T. Blackwell）已缩水成打百分的 A. T. 的一个区区兄弟——学校宿舍两名代表随他们之后出场。一位身材细长的业余球员接过球，从身后滑位区的人数判断，是个快球手。诺里斯要求「两个腿侧」。一位殷勤的裁判通知他，腿侧他已经有俩了。高个子投球手请近滑位再站偏一点，摆臂挥了一圈，仿佛查看机器是否运转如常，随即朝击球线狂奔而去。比赛开始了。

学生生涯里鲜有比大赛第一回合更惬意、更惊心动魄的时刻。惬意，是说如果你亲临观战的话。若不得不在教室里听比赛开场，那当然能把人逼疯。你听见球拍击球的声响，接着是远处的掌声。有人得分了。可是谁？得的什么？也许是四分，也许不过一分。更要紧的是，说不定击球的根本是对方。说不定哪个恶棍已经把你最好的投球手打到马路上了。这份悬念可怕极了。本该立一条校规：队长应派人到各教室传话，简明扼要地通报掷币结果。这样大家心里就有数了。可如今，全班全指望坐在窗下的那个人。教员转身往黑板上写字。全班唯一的希望火箭般蹿起，朝凉棚方向恳切一望，又扑通栽回座位。「他们还没开场呢，」他以舞台耳语通知大家。「安——*静*，」教员说。于是全套程序必须重来一遍，而且这回多了层不利：教员的目光已冷冷锁定离窗最近的那个人，也就是你与外部世界的唯一联络线。

===Original===
Various masters have various methods under such circumstances. One more than excellent man used to close his book and remark, “I think we’ll make up a little party to watch this match.” And the form, gasping its thanks, crowded to the windows. Another, the exact antithesis of this great and good gentleman, on seeing a boy taking fitful glances through the window, would observe acidly, “You are at perfect liberty, Jones, to watch the match if you care to, but if you do you will come in in the afternoon and make up the time you waste.” And as all that could be seen from that particular window was one of the umpires and a couple of fieldsmen, Jones would reluctantly elect to reserve himself, and for the present to turn his attention to Euripides again.

If you are one of the team, and watch the match from the Pavilion, you escape these trials, but there are others. In the first few overs of a School match, every ball looks to the spectators like taking a wicket. The fiendish ingenuity of the slow bowler, and the lightning speed of the fast man at the other end, make one feel positively ill. When the first ten has gone up on the scoring-board matters begin to right themselves. Today ten went up quickly. The fast man’s first ball was outside the off-stump and a half-volley, and Norris, whatever the state of his nerves at the time, never forgot his forward drive. Before the bowler had recovered his balance the ball was halfway to the ropes. The umpire waved a large hand towards the Pavilion. The bowler looked annoyed. And the School inside the form-rooms asked itself feverishly what had happened, and which side it was that was applauding.

===Chinese===
各教员各有各的对策。有一位再好不过的先生，惯于合上书说：「我看咱们组个小队去看这场比赛吧。」于是全班喘着气谢恩，挤到窗前。另一位，恰是这位伟大善良绅士的反面，见到哪个男孩朝窗外偷瞄，便会刻薄地说：「琼斯，你尽管去看比赛好了，我绝不拦你，不过要是去了，下午你就得进来，把浪费的时间补上。」而那扇窗能看到的不过是一名裁判加两三个守场员，琼斯只得悻悻作罢，眼下暂且把注意力重新转回欧里庇得斯。

你若是队中一员，从凉棚里观赛，固然躲开了这些煎熬，可另有别的。校际赛的头几个回合里，每个球在观众眼里都像要拿下一个三柱门。慢球手魔鬼般的诡计，加上另一端快球手的闪电速度，让人简直要犯病。记分牌上第一个十分亮起后，局势才开始回稳。今天十分上得很快。快球手第一球落在外柱外侧，又是个半截好球，诺里斯不论当时神经状态如何，从未忘掉他的前挥击球。投球手还没回过身来，球已行程过半，直奔围绳。裁判朝凉棚挥了挥大手。投球手一脸恼火。而教室里的全校师生正焦灼地自问出了什么事，鼓掌的又是哪一方。

===Original===
Having bowled his first ball too far up, the M.C.C. man, on the principle of anything for a change, now put in a very short one. Norris, a new man after that drive, steered it through the slips, and again the umpire waved his hand.

The rest of the over was more quiet. The last ball went for four byes, and then it was Baker’s turn to face the slow man. Baker was a steady, plodding bat. He played five balls gently to mid-on, and glanced the sixth for a single to leg. With the fast bowler, who had not yet got his length, he was more vigorous, and succeeded in cutting him twice for two.

With thirty up for no wickets the School began to feel more comfortable. But at forty-three Baker was shattered by the man of pace, and retired with twenty to his credit. Gethryn came in next, but it was not to be his day out with the bat.

===Chinese===
M.C.C. 那位第一球投得离柱太近吃了亏，本着怎么也得换换花样的原则，这回投了个极短的球。经那一记前挥而焕然一新的诺里斯，把球导穿滑位区，裁判的手又挥了起来。

这个回合余下的球平静些。最后一球漏成四分野球，随后轮到贝克面对慢球手。贝克是位沉稳、勤恳的击球手。他轻轻把五个球推向中前位，第六球顺势往腿侧一拨，挣了一分。对付那位还没找准落点的快球手，他更来劲，两度斜切得手，各取两分。

学校队三十分未失一柱，开始觉得舒坦了。可到四十三分时，贝克被那位快球手击碎，带着二十分退场。下一个上场的是盖斯林，可惜今天不属于他的球拍。

===Original===
The fast bowler, who was now bowling excellently, sent down one rather wide of the off-stump. The Bishop made most of his runs from off balls, and he had a go at this one. It was rising when he hit it, and it went off his bat like a flash. In a School match it would have been a boundary. But today there was unusual talent in the slips. The man from Middlesex darted forward and sideways. He took the ball one-handed two inches from the ground, and received the applause which followed the effort with a rather bored look, as if he were saying, “My good sirs, *why* make a fuss over these trifles!” The Bishop walked slowly back to the Pavilion, feeling that his luck was out, and Pringle came in.

A boy of Pringle’s character is exactly the right person to go in in an emergency like the present one. Two wickets had fallen in two balls, and the fast bowler was swelling visibly with determination to do the hat-trick. But Pringle never went in oppressed by the fear of getting out. He had a serene and boundless confidence in himself.

The fast man tried a yorker. Pringle came down hard on it, and forced the ball past the bowler for a single. Then he and Norris settled down to a lengthy stand.

===Chinese===
投得正出色的快球手送出一记偏出外柱颇远的球。「主教」的分数多半从偏球上挣来，这一球他出手了。击中时球正在上升，离拍的瞬间快如闪电。要在校际赛里，这是一记边界球。可今天滑位区里人才超常。那位米德尔塞克斯（Middlesex）来的球员横里斜里蹿出，单手在离地两英寸处将球捕获，对随后而来的掌声报以百无聊赖的神情，仿佛在说：「各位仁兄，*何苦*为这点小事大惊小怪！」「主教」慢步走回凉棚，自觉运势不佳，普林格尔登场。

普林格尔这种性子的男孩，正是眼下这种危局最合适的出场人选。两球连折两柱，快球手明显膨胀着拿下帽子戏法的决心。可普林格尔上场从不背负出局的恐惧。他对自己怀有一种安详而无边的自信。

快球手试了一记贴地球。普林格尔狠狠压下去，把球从投球手身侧强行挤出，得了一分。随后他与诺里斯扎下阵来，打起了持久战。

===Original===
“I do like seeing Pringle bat,” said Gosling. “He always gives you the idea that he’s doing you a personal favour by knocking your bowling about. Oh, well hit!”

Pringle had cut a full-pitch from the slow bowler to the ropes. Marriott, who had been silent and apparently in pain for some minutes, now gave out the following homemade effort:

===Chinese===
「我就爱看普林格尔击球，」戈斯林说。「他总给你一种感觉：他把你的投球打得七零八落，是赏了你个人的脸面。哦，好球！」

普林格尔把慢球手的一记不到点的直接球斜切到围绳。沉默且显然痛苦了好几分钟的马里奥特，这时发布了下面这件自制作品：

===Original===
> “A dashing young sportsman named Pringle,

On breaking his duck (with a single),

Observed with a smile,

‘Just notice my style,

How science with vigour I mingle.’ ”

===Chinese===
> 「有位潇洒的运动少年普林格尔，

破鸭蛋（单分一球）之际乐呵呵，

他含笑说道：

「瞧我这套路多妙，

科学与猛劲一块儿搅和。」」

===Original===
“Little thing of my own,” he added, quoting England’s greatest librettist. “I call it ‘Heart Foam.’ I shall not publish it. Oh, run it out!”

Both Pringle and Norris were evidently in form. Norris was now not far from his fifty, and Pringle looked as if he might make anything. The century went up, and a run later Norris off-drove the slow bowler’s successor for three, reaching his fifty by the stroke.

===Chinese===
「自己胡诌的小玩意儿，」他补了一句，引用的是英格兰最伟大的歌词作者。「我管它叫《心之泡沫》。我不打算发表。哦，快跑分！」

普林格尔和诺里斯显然都在状态。诺里斯此时距五十分不远，普林格尔看着像多少分都拿得出来。百分上去了，再添一分后，诺里斯把慢球手继任者的一球反位击往斜前，三分到手，五十分就此达成。

===Original===
“Must be fairly warm work fielding today,” said Reece.

“By Jove!” said Gethryn, “I forgot. I left my white hat in the House. Any of you chaps like to fetch it?”

There were no offers. Gethryn got up.

“Marriott, you slacker, come over to the House.”

“My good sir, I’m in next. Why don’t you wait till the fellows come out of school and send a kid for it?”

“He probably wouldn’t know where to find it. I don’t know where it is myself. No, I shall go, but there’s no need to fag about it yet. Hullo! Norris is out.”

===Chinese===
「今天守场准是桩热得可以的美差，」里斯说。

「哎呀！」盖斯林说，「我忘了。我的白帽子落在宿舍了。你们哪位愿意去取？」

无人应征。盖斯林站起身。

「马里奥特，你这懒骨头，跟我去趟宿舍。」

「我的好先生，我下一个上场。干嘛不等放学，派个小家伙去取？」

「他多半不知道上哪儿找。我自己都不知道搁哪儿了。不，我得去，不过还不急。喂！诺里斯出局了。」

===Original===
Norris had stopped a straight one with his leg. He had made fifty-one in his best manner, and the School, leaving the form-rooms at the exact moment when the fatal ball was being bowled, were just in time to applaud him and realize what they had missed.

Gethryn’s desire for his hat was not so pressing as to make him deprive himself of the pleasure of seeing Marriott at the wickets. Marriott ought to do something special today. Unfortunately, after he had played out one over and hit two fours off it, the luncheon interval began.

It was, therefore, not for half an hour that the Bishop went at last in search of the missing headgear. As luck would have it, the hat was on the table, so that whatever chance he might have had of overlooking the note which his uncle had left for him on the empty cashbox disappeared. The two things caught his eye simultaneously. He opened the note and read it. It is not necessary to transcribe the note in detail. It was no masterpiece of literary skill. But it had this merit, that it was not vague. Reading it, one grasped its meaning immediately.

===Chinese===
诺里斯用腿挡下了一记直线球。他以他最拿手的方式拿了五十一分，而全校师生恰在那一致命之球投出的当口涌出教室，正好赶上为他鼓掌，并意识到自己错过了什么。

盖斯林对帽子的渴望，还没急到让他牺牲观看马里奥特击球的乐趣。马里奥特今天该有一番特别作为。可惜，他打满一个回合、从中轰出两记四分之后，午餐间歇开始了。

因此，直到半小时后，「主教」才终于出发寻找那顶失踪的帽子。运气不错，帽子就在桌上，于是无论他原本有几分可能忽略掉叔叔留在空钱箱上的字条，这点可能也消失了。两样东西同时撞进他的眼帘。他展开字条读了一遍。没必要全文转录这张字条。它算不上文学技巧的杰作。但它有一条优点：不含糊。一读之下，其意立现。

===Original===
The Bishop’s first feeling was that the bottom had dropped out of everything suddenly. Surprise was not the word. It was the arrival of the absolutely unexpected.

Then he began to consider the position.

Farnie must be brought back. That was plain. And he must be brought back at once, before anyone could get to hear of what had happened. Gethryn had the very strongest objections to his uncle, considered purely as a human being; but the fact remained that he was his uncle, and the Bishop had equally strong objections to any member of his family being mixed up in a business of this description.

===Chinese===
「主教」的第一个感觉是，万事万物的底儿忽然掉了。惊讶已不足以形容。这是绝对意料之外者的降临。

随后他开始考量局势。

必须把法尼带回来。这很明白。而且必须立刻带回来，赶在任何人听闻此事之前。纯粹作为一个人类来看，盖斯林对他这位叔叔抱着最强烈的反感；可事实终归是事实：他是他叔叔，而「主教」同样强烈地反感自家任何成员搅进这一类勾当。

===Original===
Having settled that point, he went on to the next. How was he to be brought back? He could not have gone far, for he could not have been gone much more than half an hour. Again, from his knowledge of his uncle’s character, he deduced that he had in all probability not gone to the nearest station, Horton. At Horton one had to wait hours at a time for a train. Farnie must have made his way﻿—on his bicycle﻿—straight for the junction, Anfield, fifteen miles off by a good road. A train left Anfield for London at three-thirty. It was now a little past two. On a bicycle he could do it easily, and get back with his prize by about five, if he rode hard. In that case all would be well. Only three of the School wickets had fallen, and the pitch was playing as true as concrete. Besides, there was Pringle still in at one end, well set, and surely Marriott and Jennings and the rest of them would manage to stay in till five. They couldn’t help it. All they had to do was to play forward to everything, and they must stop in. He himself had got out, it was true, but that was simply a regrettable accident. Not one man in a hundred would have caught that catch. No, with luck he ought easily to be able to do the distance and get back in time to go out with the rest of the team to field.

===Chinese===
这一点定了，他接着想下一件。怎么把他带回来？他走不远，因为走了顶多不过半个多小时。再者，凭对他叔叔性格的了解，他推断此人十有八九没去最近的车站霍顿。在霍顿，等一趟车要等上几个钟头。法尼准是骑上自行车，直奔十五英里外、大路畅通的枢纽站安菲尔德去了。三点半有一班火车离安菲尔德去伦敦。此刻刚过两点。骑车的话，他轻松赶得上，若玩命骑，五点左右便能带着他的战利品回来。果真如此，一切好说。学校只折了三个三柱门，球道硬得跟混凝土一样真。何况普林格尔还在一端坐镇，扎得很稳，而马里奥特、詹宁斯（Jennings）和其余几位准能撑到五点。他们不撑也得撑。他们只需对来球一律前推，就必然守得住。他自己出局了，不假，可那不过是桩遗憾的意外。一百个人里也没有一个接得住那一球。不，只要运气不坏，他轻松骑完全程，赶得及随全队出场守备。

===Original===
He ran downstairs and out of the House. On his way to the bicycle-shed he stopped, and looked towards the field, part of which could be seen from where he stood. The match had begun again. The fast bowler was just commencing his run. He saw him tear up to the crease and deliver the ball. What happened then he could not see, owing to the trees which stood between him and the School grounds. But he heard the crack of ball meeting bat, and a great howl of applause went up from the invisible audience. A boundary, apparently. Yes, there was the umpire signalling it. Evidently a long stand was going to be made. He would have oceans of time for his ride. Norris wouldn’t dream of declaring the innings closed before five o’clock at the earliest, and no bowler could take seven wickets in the time on such a pitch. He hauled his bicycle from the shed, and rode off at racing speed in the direction of Anfield.

===Chinese===
他奔下楼梯，冲出宿舍。去车棚的路上他停下脚，朝球场望了一眼——从他所站的地方能看见球场的一角。比赛重新开始了。快球手正要开跑。他望见他一路狂冲到击球线，将球投出。随后的事他看不见了，隔着他和校场之间的那些树。但他听见了球拍击球的脆响，还有看不见的观众掀起的巨大欢呼。一记边界，显然。对，裁判正在打信号。看来一场长局要开始了。他骑这一趟有的是充裕时间。最早五点之前，诺里斯做梦也不会宣布收盘，而在这等球道上，没有哪个投球手能在这么点时间里拿下七个三柱门。他把自行车从棚里拽出来，朝安菲尔德方向竞速驰去。

## XVIII The Bishop Scores / 第十八章 主教得分

===Original===

At this point Wilson finished the toast, and went out. As he went he thought over what he had just heard. Marriott and Gethryn frequently talked the most important School politics before him, for they had discovered at an early date that he was a youth of discretion, who could be trusted not to reveal state secrets. But matters now seemed to demand such a revelation. It was a serious thing to do, but there was nobody else to do it, and it obviously must be done, so, by a simple process of reasoning, he ought to do it. Half an hour had to elapse before the bell rang for lockup. There was plenty of time to do the whole thing and get back to the House before the door was closed. He took his cap, and trotted off to Jephson’s.

Norris was alone in his study when Wilson knocked at the door. He seemed surprised to see his visitor. He knew Wilson well by sight, he being captain of the First Eleven and Wilson a distinctly promising junior bat, but this was the first time he had ever exchanged a word of conversation with him.


===Chinese===

就在这时，威尔逊（Wilson）烤好了吐司，走了出去。一路上，他把刚才听到的话在心里盘算了一遍。马里奥特（Marriott）和盖斯林（Gethryn）常在他面前谈论最要紧的校内大事，因为他们早就发现这位少年口风严实，把国家机密托付给他也保险。可眼下这情形，看来非得走漏一回风声不可了。这可是件非同小可的事，但除他之外再无旁人可托，而这事又明摆着非做不可，于是经过一番简单的推理，结论便是：该他去干。距离锁门铃响还有半个钟头，足够他把整件事办完，赶在楼门关闭之前回到宿舍。他抓起帽子，一路小跑奔杰夫森宿舍（Jephson's）去了。

威尔逊敲门的时候，诺里斯（Norris）正独自待在书房里。见到来客他似乎有些意外。他对威尔逊的脸熟得很——他本人是板球一队队长，而威尔逊是个前途相当看好的低年级击球手——可两人之间真正攀谈，这还是破天荒头一回。



===Original===

“Hullo,” he said, putting down his book.

“Oh, I say, Norris,” began Wilson nervously, “can I speak to you for a minute?”

“All right. Go ahead.”

After two false starts, Wilson at last managed to get the thread of his story. He did not mention Marriott’s remarks on football subjects, but confined himself to the story of Farnie and the bicycle ride, as he had heard it from Gethryn on the second evening of the term.


===Chinese===

“喂，”他放下书，招呼道。

“呃，那个，诺里斯，”威尔逊紧张地开了口，“能跟你说会儿话吗？”

“行啊，说吧。”

威尔逊两次张嘴都没接上茬，第三回总算把话头理顺了。马里奥特关于足球的那番议论他没有提，只把法尼（Farnie）骑车出游的事情原原本本讲了一遍——就是开学第二天晚上他从盖斯林嘴里听来的那个版本。



===Original===

“So that’s how it was, you see,” he concluded.

There was a long silence. Wilson sat nervously on the edge of his chair, and Norris stared thoughtfully into the fire.

“So shall I tell him it’s all right?” asked Wilson at last.

“Tell who what’s all right?” asked Norris politely.


===Chinese===

“事情就是这样，你明白了吧，”他最后说道。

接下来是长久的沉默。威尔逊局促不安地坐在椅子边儿上，诺里斯则若有所思地盯着炉火出神。

“那……我去告诉他没事了？”威尔逊终于忍不住问道。

“告诉谁没事了？”诺里斯客客气气地问。



===Original===

“Oh, er, Gethryn, you know,” replied Wilson, slightly disconcerted. He had had a sort of idea that Norris would have rushed out of the room, sprinted over to Leicester’s, and flung himself on the Bishop’s bosom in an agony of remorse. He appeared to be taking things altogether too coolly.

“No,” said Norris, “don’t tell him anything. I shall have lots of chances of speaking to him myself if I want to. It isn’t as if we were never going to meet again. You’d better cut now. There’s the bell just going. Good night.”

“Good night, Norris.”

“Oh, and, I say,” said Norris, as Wilson opened the door, “I meant to tell you some time ago. If you buck up next cricket season, it’s quite possible that you’ll get colours of some sort. You might bear that in mind.”

“I will,” said Wilson fervently. “Good night, Norris. Thanks awfully.”


===Chinese===

“哦，呃，盖斯林啊，你知道的，”威尔回答，心里有点发慌。他原本朦朦胧胧地以为，诺里斯会立刻冲出房门，一路飞奔赶到莱斯特宿舍（Leicester's），痛悔交加地扑进“主教（the Bishop）”怀里。可眼前这位未免也太不当回事了。

“不，”诺里斯说，“什么也别告诉他。只要我想找他谈，往后有的是机会。又不是从此见不着面了。你最好现在赶紧走，铃马上要响了。晚安。”

“晚安，诺里斯。”

“哦，对了，”威尔逊开门时，诺里斯又开口了，“这话我早就想跟你说了。下个板球赛季你要是肯加把劲，弄个队色什么的相当有指望。记在心里吧。”

“我一定记住，”威尔逊热切地说，“晚安，诺里斯。多谢了。”



===Original===

The Nomads brought down a reasonably hot team against Beckford as a general rule, for the School had a reputation in the football world. They were a big lot this year. Their forwards looked capable, and when, after the School fullback had returned the ball into touch on the halfway line, the line-out had resulted in a handball and a scrum, they proved that appearances were not deceptive. They broke through in a solid mass﻿—the Beckford forwards never somehow seemed to get together properly in the first scrum of a big match﻿—and rushed the ball down the field. Norris fell on it. Another hastily-formed scrum, and the Nomads’ front rank was off again. Ten yards nearer the School line there was another halt. Grainger, the Beckford fullback, whose speciality was the stopping of rushes, had curled himself neatly round the ball. Then the School forwards awoke to a sense of their responsibilities. It was time they did, for Beckford was now penned up well within its own twenty-five line, and the Nomad halves were appealing pathetically to their forwards to let that ball out, for goodness’ *sake*. But the forwards fancied a combined rush was the thing to play. For a full minute they pushed the School pack towards their line, and then some rash enthusiast kicked a shade too hard. The ball dribbled out of the scrum on the School side, and Marriott punted into touch.


===Chinese===

游牧人队（Nomads）每逢对阵贝克福德（Beckford），照例都会拉来一支相当硬朗的队伍，因为这所学校在足球界颇有名头。他们今年更是一帮人高马大的家伙。他们的前锋一看就不好惹，而事实很快证明，眼见为实：校队最后卫把球回踢出中线边线后，争边球（line-out）中球被用手拍回场内，随即形成并列争球（scrum），对方便以密集阵形一举突破——大赛的头一个争球里，贝克福德的前锋不知怎么总也拢不齐——一路把球冲下了场地。诺里斯扑上去压住了球。又一个仓促结成的争球，游牧人队前排又冲了起来。离校队得分线又近了十码，攻势再度受挫。贝克福德最后卫格兰杰（Grainger）的看家本领正是拦截集团冲锋，只见他把身子利落地一蜷，稳稳裹住了球。这时校队前锋们才如梦初醒，想起自己责任重大。也该醒了——贝克福德此刻已被死死压在自家二十五码线之内，游牧人队的前卫们正可怜巴巴地央求前锋们把那球传出来，看在老天的*份*上。可前锋们偏偏认定集团冲锋才是正道。他们足足推着校队前锋群朝得分线拱了一分钟，随后某个莽撞的热心人一脚踢重了那么一丝。球从争球堆里滚出来，滚到校队一侧，马里奥特一记长踢，踢出边线。



===Original===

“You *must* let it out, you men,” said the aggrieved halfbacks.

Marriott’s kick had not brought much relief. The visitors were still inside the Beckford twenty-five line, and now that their forwards had realized the sin and folly of trying to rush the ball through, matters became decidedly warm for the School outsides. Norris and Gethryn in the centre and Grainger at back performed prodigies of tackling. The wing three-quarter hovered nervously about, feeling that their time might come at any moment.

The Nomad attack was concentrated on the extreme right.


===Chinese===

“你们*千万*得把球传出来啊，各位，”满腹委屈的前卫们说。

马里奥特这一脚并没换来多少喘息。客队仍压在贝克福德二十五码线之内，而等他们的前锋终于领悟到硬冲是既造孽又愚蠢之后，校队外围球员的日子顿时吃紧起来。中路有诺里斯和盖斯林，后面有格兰杰，个个扑抱救险得神乎其技。两翼三分卫提心吊胆地来回游走，总觉得随时会轮到自己上场。

游牧人队的进攻集中在极右一路。



===Original===

Philips, the International, was officiating for them as wing-three-quarters on that side, and they played to him. If he once got the ball he would take a considerable amount of stopping. But the ball never managed to arrive. Norris and Gethryn stuck to their men closer than brothers.

A prolonged struggle on the goal-line is a great spectacle. That is why (purely in the opinion of the present scribe) Rugby is such a much better game than Association. You don’t get that sort of thing in Soccer. But such struggles generally end in the same way. The Nomads were now within a couple of yards of the School line. It was a question of time. In three minutes the whistle would blow for halftime, and the School would be saved.

But in those three minutes the thing happened. For the first time in the match the Nomad forwards heeled absolutely cleanly. Hitherto, the ball had always remained long enough in the scrum to give Marriott and Wogan, the School halves, time to get round and on to their men before they could become dangerous. But this time the ball was in and out again in a moment. The Nomad half who was taking the scrum picked it up, and was over the line before Marriott realized that the ball was out at all. The school lining the ropes along the touchline applauded politely but feebly, as was their custom when the enemy scored.

The kick was a difficult one﻿—the man had got over in the corner﻿—and failed. The referee blew his whistle for halftime. The teams sucked lemons, and the Beckford forwards tried to explain to Hill, the captain, why they never got that ball in the scrums. Hill having observed bitterly, as he did in every match when the School did not get thirty points in the first half, that he “would chuck the whole lot of them out next Saturday,” the game recommenced.


===Chinese===

国际级球星菲利普斯（Philips）正给他们充当那一侧的边锋三分卫，全队的球都往他那儿喂。这球一旦到他手里，要拦住他可得费上一番大功夫。可球偏偏始终送不到。诺里斯和盖斯林盯自己的人，盯得比亲兄弟还紧。

得分线前的持久鏖战是一大奇观。正因为如此（纯粹出自本记录者的私见），拉格比式橄榄球才远比协会足球好看。足球里可瞧不见这种场面。不过这类鏖战往往都以同一种方式收场。游牧人队此刻距校队得分线只剩两三码，攻破只是时间问题。再过三分钟，半场的哨声就要吹响，校队就得救了。

可偏偏就在这三分钟里，事情发生了。全场比赛头一回，游牧人队的前锋把球从争球堆里用脚跟拨得干干净净。此前，球总会在争球里多留一会儿，好让校队两位前卫马里奥特和沃根（Wogan）来得及绕到自己的对位身边、趁其未成气候先摁住他。可这回球刚进去就出来了。负责争球的那名游牧人队前卫一把抄起球，等马里奥特反应过来球已出堆，人已经压过线了。沿边线围栏站立的校内学生们礼貌地鼓了掌，掌声却有气无力——每逢对方得分，他们向来如此。

这一脚射门难度不小——那人是从角上压过线的——结果没进。裁判吹响半场哨。两队人吮着柠檬，贝克福德的前锋们则竭力向队长希尔（Hill）解释，为什么争球里他们永远抢不到那个球。希尔咬牙切齿地撂下一句——每逢上半场校队拿不到三十分，他场场都要来这么一句——说他“下周六非把这一整帮人统统扫地出门不可”，随后比赛重新开始。



===Original===

Beckford started on the second half with three points against them, but with both wind, what there was of it, and slope in their favour. Three points, especially in a club match, where one’s opponents may reasonably be expected to suffer from lack of training and combination, is not an overwhelming score.

Beckford was hopeful and determined.

To record all the fluctuations of the game for the next thirty-five minutes is unnecessary. Copies of *The Beckfordian* containing a full report, crammed with details, and written in the most polished English, may still be had from the editor at the modest price of sixpence. Suffice it to say that two minutes from the kickoff the Nomads increased their score with a goal from a mark, and almost immediately afterwards Marriott gave the School their first score with a neat dropkick. It was about five minutes from the end of the game, and the Nomads still led, when the event of the afternoon took place. The Nomad forwards had brought the ball down the ground with one of their combined dribbles, and a scrum had been formed on the Beckford twenty-five line. The visitors heeled as usual. The half who was taking the scrum whipped the ball out in the direction of his colleague. But before it could reach him, Wogan had intercepted the pass, and was off down the field, through the enemy’s three-quarter line, with only the back in front of him, and with Norris in close attendance, followed by Gethryn.


===Chinese===

下半场开打时，贝克福德落后三分，但风——姑且算有风的话——和坡度都站在他们这边。三分，尤其在俱乐部比赛里——对手缺训练、少配合，输这么点分实在算不上什么了不得的差距。

贝克福德上下满怀希望，斗志昂扬。

接下来三十五分钟里战局如何风云变幻，没有必要一一赘述。刊有完整赛况报道的《贝克福德人》（*The Beckfordian*）——细节详尽，文笔典雅——至今仍可向主编订阅，价格不过区区六便士。只需说这么一句就够了：开球两分钟后，游牧人队凭一记“马克”（mark，自由接球后的定位踢）射门命中，扩大了比分；紧接着，马里奥特一记利落的落踢为校队拿下第一分。离终场大约还剩五分钟、游牧人队仍居领先时，当天下午的重头戏上演了。游牧人队前锋以一次拿手的集团盘带把球推向前场，在贝克福德二十五码线上形成争球。客队照例脚跟出球。负责争球的前卫把球一抽，朝同事的方向甩了出去。可球还没到，沃根已中途截下，撒腿直奔前场，穿透对方三分卫线，眼前只剩最后一名后卫，诺里斯紧随策应，盖斯林再随其后。



===Original===

There is nothing like an intercepted pass for adding a dramatic touch to a close game. A second before it had seemed as though the School must be beaten, for though they would probably have kept the enemy out for the few minutes that remained, they could never have worked the ball down the field by ordinary give-and-take play. And now, unless Wogan shamefully bungled what he had begun so well, victory was certain.

There was a danger, though. Wogan might in the excitement of the moment try to get past the back and score himself, instead of waiting until the back was on him and then passing to Norris. The School on the touchline shrieked their applause, but there was a note of anxiety as well. A slight reputation which Wogan had earned for playing a selfish game sprang up before their eyes. Would he pass? Or would he run himself? If the latter, the odds were anything against his succeeding.

But everything went right. Wogan arrived at the back, drew that gentleman’s undivided attention to himself, and then slung the ball out to Norris, the model of what a pass ought to be. Norris made no mistake about it.


===Chinese===

要给一场僵持不下的比赛添上戏剧性的一笔，没有什么比一次中途截传更灵的了。一秒钟之前，校队似乎败局已定：虽说剩下那几分钟他们多半守得住，可要靠寻常的你来我往把球推到前场去，是万万不能了。而此刻，只要沃根别没出息地把这手好牌打砸，胜利已是囊中之物。

不过风险也不是没有。沃根兴许会一时头脑发热，想亲自晃过那名后卫自己得分，而不是等后卫扑上来再把球传给诺里斯。边线外的校众尖声喝彩，欢呼声里却也夹着一丝焦虑。沃根平日里落下的那点“打球自私”的名声，此刻齐刷刷浮现在众人眼前。他会传吗？还是自己带？若是后者，成算微乎其微。

可一切都顺顺当当。沃根冲到那名后卫跟前，把这位先生的全部注意力稳稳吸在自己身上，随即将球斜甩给诺里斯——这一传堪称传球教科书。诺里斯稳稳接住，毫厘不差。



===Original===

Then the remarkable thing happened. The Bishop, having backed Norris up for fifty yards at full speed, could not stop himself at once. His impetus carried him on when all need for expenditure of energy had come to an end. He was just slowing down, leaving Norris to complete the thing alone, when to his utter amazement he found the ball in his hands. Norris had passed to him. With a clear run in, and the nearest foeman yards to the rear, Norris had passed. It was certainly weird, but his first duty was to score. There must be no mistake about the scoring. Afterwards he could do any thinking that might be required. He shot at express speed over the line, and placed the ball in the exact centre of the white line which joined the posts. Then he walked back to where Norris was waiting for him.

“Good man,” said Norris, “that was awfully good.”

His tone was friendly. He spoke as he had been accustomed to speak before the M.C.C. match. Gethryn took his cue from him. It was evident that, for reasons at present unexplained, Norris wished for peace, and such being the case, the Bishop was only too glad to oblige him.

“No,” he said, “it was jolly good of you to let me in like that. Why, you’d only got to walk over.”


===Chinese===

接着，那桩奇事便发生了。“主教”一路全速尾随诺里斯策应了五十码，一时收不住脚。明明已经到了不必再花半分力气的地步，惯性却推着他继续向前。他正放缓脚步、把压线得分的收尾工作留给诺里斯独享，却惊愕万分地发现球已在自家手中。诺里斯传球给他了。前面一片坦途，最近的敌兵也在身后好几码开外——诺里斯竟然传了。这事委实邪门，可他的头等职责是得分。得分的事决不容有失。至于思考，回头有的是工夫。他特快列车一般射过线区，把球端端正正压在两门柱之间那条白线的正中央。然后他走回诺里斯等候的地方。

“好样的，”诺里斯说，“方才那一球漂亮极了。”

他的口气很友好，是M.C.C.比赛之前他惯用的那种腔调。盖斯林立刻心领神会。显而易见，出于某种眼下尚未挑明的原因，诺里斯想要休战；既然如此，“主教”巴不得成人之美。

“哪儿的话，”他说，“你这么让我压线得分，实在够意思。凭你那时局面，自己走过去都是稳的。”



===Original===

“Oh, I don’t know. I might have slipped or something. Anyhow I thought I’d better pass. What price Beckford combination? The homemade article, eh?”

“Rather,” said the Bishop.

“Oh, by the way,” said Norris, “I was talking to young Wilson yesterday evening. Or rather he was talking to me. Decent kid, isn’t he? He was telling me about Farnie. The M.C.C. match, you know, and so on.”

“Oh!” said the Bishop. He began to see how things had happened.

“Yes,” said Norris. “Hullo, that gives us the game.”


===Chinese===

“哦，那可说不准。万一我滑一跤什么的呢。总之我想还是传出去稳妥。咱贝克福德的配合怎么样？纯自家出品，嗯？”

“没得说，”“主教”说。

“哦，对了，”诺里斯说，“昨晚我跟小威尔逊聊了聊。或者说，是他跟我聊。这孩子不错，是不是？他跟我说了法尼的事。就是M.C.C.比赛那一回，等等。”

“哦！”“主教”应了一声。他开始明白这些事是怎么凑到一块儿的了。

“是啊，”诺里斯说。“瞧，这一下比赛到手了。”



===Original===

A roar of applause from the touchline greeted the successful attempt of Hill to convert Gethryn’s try into the necessary goal. The referee performed a solo on the whistle, and immediately afterwards another, as if as an encore.

“No side,” he said pensively. The School had won by two points.

“That’s all right,” said Norris. “I say, can you come and have tea in my study when you’ve changed? Some of the fellows are coming. I’ve asked Reece and Marriott, and Pringle said he’d turn up too. It’ll be rather a tight fit, but we’ll manage somehow.”

“Right,” said the Bishop. “Thanks very much.”


===Chinese===

希尔一记追加射门命中，把盖斯林的达阵换成了所需的那记进球，边线外顿时欢声雷动。裁判在哨子上来了一段独奏，紧接着又是一段，仿佛是在返场。

“终场，不分边喽。”他若有所思地说。校队以两分之差获胜。

“这就对了，”诺里斯说。“我说，换好衣服后你能来我书房喝杯茶吗？有几个人要来。我请了里斯（Reece）和马里奥特，普林格尔（Pringle）也说会露面。地方会挤得够呛，不过总归有法子。”

“好嘞，”“主教”说。“多谢。”



===Original===

Norris was correct. It was a tight fit. But then a study brew loses half its charm if there is room to breathe. It was a most enjoyable ceremony in every way. After the serious part of the meal was over, and the time had arrived when it was found pleasanter to eat wafer biscuits than muffins, the Bishop obliged once more with a recital of his adventures on that distant day in the summer term.

There were several comments when he had finished. The only one worth recording is Reece’s.

Reece said it distinctly reminded him of a thing which had happened to a friend of a chap his brother had known at Sandhurst.


===Chinese===

诺里斯没说错。确实挤得够呛。不过话说回来，书房茶会这玩意儿，要是宽敞得还能喘气，魅力便先失了一半。这场仪式从头到尾都令人尽兴。饭的正事完结、到了吃威化饼干比吃松饼更惬意的时辰，“主教”又应众人之请，把夏季学期那个遥远日子里的历险从头演绎了一遍。

他讲完之后，评论有好几条。值得记录的只有里斯的那一条。

里斯说，这事分明让他想起一桩旧闻——他哥哥在桑德赫斯特（Sandhurst，英国陆军军官学校）认识的一个人的一位朋友，就遭遇过一模一样的事。

## VIII The M.C.C. Match / 第八章 M.C.C.比赛

===Original===

But out in the field things were going badly with Beckford. The aspect of a game often changes considerably after lunch. For a while it looked as if Marriott and Pringle were in for their respective centuries. But Marriott was never a safe batsman.

A hundred and fifty went up on the board off a square leg hit for two, which completed Pringle’s half-century, and then Marriott faced the slow bowler, who had been put on again after lunch. The first ball was a miss-hit. It went behind point for a couple. The next he got fairly hold of and drove to the boundary. The third was a very simple-looking ball. Its sole merit appeared to be the fact that it was straight. Also it was a trifle shorter than it looked. Marriott jumped out, and got too much under it. Up it soared, straight over the bowler’s head. A trifle more weight behind the hit, and it would have cleared the ropes. As it was, the man in the deep-field never looked like missing it. The batsmen had time to cross over before the ball arrived, but they did it without enthusiasm. The run was not likely to count. Nor did it. Deep-field caught it like a bird. Marriott had made twenty-two.


===Chinese===

可场上的局势对贝克福德却颇为不妙。一场比赛的行情，往往在午饭后大变样。有一阵子，马里奥特和普林格尔眼看都要各奔自己的一百分（century）去了。但马里奥特从来算不上一个稳当的击球员。

记分牌上的一百五十分，是靠方腿位（square leg）方向一记两分击球添上的，这一下也成全了普林格尔的半百分（half-century）。随后马里奥特迎上了那位慢速投球手——午饭后此人又被派上了场。第一球击偏了，从切位（point）身后漏过去，得了两分。第二球他结结实实咬中了，一直打到边界。第三球看着再家常不过，唯一的长处似乎就是路线笔直，而且落点比看上去还要短那么一点。马里奥特跃出击球，杆子捅得太靠下。球腾空而起，正正越过投球手的头顶。这一击若再多压上几分力气，便能飞出围绳。可惜差了那么点意思，外野深处（deep field）那位从头到尾没露出半点要漏接的样子。两位击球员趁球未到交叉换位，只是换得无精打采——这一分多半是要泡汤的。它也确实泡了汤。外野手接球接得像接一只鸟。马里奥特得了二十二分。



===Original===

And now occurred one of those rots which so often happen without any ostensible cause in the best regulated school elevens. Pringle played the three remaining balls of the over without mishap, but when it was the fast man’s turn to bowl to Bruce, Marriott’s successor, things began to happen. Bruce, temporarily insane, perhaps through nervousness, played back at a half-volley, and was clean bowled. Hill came in, and was caught two balls later at the wicket. And the last ball of the over sent Jennings’s off-stump out of the ground, after that batsman had scored two.

“I can always bowl like blazes after lunch,” said the fast man to Pringle. “It’s the lobster salad that does it, I think.” Four for a hundred and fifty-seven had changed to seven for a hundred and fifty-nine in the course of a single over. Gethryn’s calculations, if he had only known, could have done now with a little revision.


===Chinese===

接着便发生了那种崩盘——再规矩齐整的校队里也屡见其外、且总是毫无明显来由。普林格尔把这一轮剩下的三球平安送了过去，可轮到快球手投给马里奥特的接替者布鲁斯（Bruce）时，事情开始接二连三。布鲁斯一时神经错乱——兴许是紧张所致——对一记半高球回身防守，三柱门被砸了个正着。希尔上场，两球之后在三柱门后被接杀。这一轮的最后一球，则把已经得了两分的詹宁斯（Jennings）的外柱直接轰出了场地。

“我一到午后就能投得邪门地猛，”快球手对普林格尔说。“我琢磨着，是龙虾沙拉的功劳。”一百五十七分失四门，一轮之间变成了一百五十九分失七门。盖斯林的那些盘算——倘若他能知道的话——此刻真该小小修订一下了。



===Original===

Gosling was the next man. He was followed, after a brief innings of three balls, which realized eight runs, by Baynes. Baynes, though abstaining from runs himself, helped Pringle to add three to the score, all in singles, and was then yorked by the slow man, who meanly and treacherously sent down, without the slightest warning, a very fast one on the leg stump. Then Reece came in for the last wicket, and the rot stopped. Reece always went in last for the School, and the School in consequence always felt that there were possibilities to the very end of the innings.

The lot of a last-wicket man is somewhat trying. As at any moment his best innings may be nipped in the bud by the other man getting out, he generally feels that it is hardly worth while to play himself in before endeavouring to make runs. He therefore tries to score off every ball, and thinks himself lucky if he gets half a dozen. Reece, however, took life more seriously. He had made quite an art of last-wicket batting. Once, against the Butterflies, he had run up sixty not out, and there was always the chance that he would do the same again. Today, with Pringle at the other end, he looked forward to a pleasant hour or two at the wicket.

No bowler ever looks on the last man quite in the same light as he does the other ten. He underrates him instinctively. The M.C.C. fast bowler was a man with an idea. His idea was that he could bowl a slow ball of diabolical ingenuity. As a rule, public feeling was against his trying the experiment. His captains were in the habit of enquiring rudely if he thought he was playing marbles. This was exactly what the M.C.C. captain asked on the present occasion, when the head ball sailed ponderously through the air, and was promptly hit by Reece into the Pavilion. The bowler grinned, and resumed his ordinary pace.


===Chinese===

下一个出场的是戈斯林（Gosling）。他打了短短三板，挣了八分，随后上场的是贝恩斯（Baynes）。贝恩斯自己虽然颗粒无收，却帮着普林格尔往总分上添了三分——全是单分——接着便吃了慢速投球手一记约克球（yorker，贴地快球）：这家伙卑鄙狡诈，事前毫无征兆地砸来一记直奔腿柱的快速球。随后里斯上场，守最后一门，崩盘总算止住了。校队的最后一个位置向来留给里斯，校队也因此总觉得一轮打到最后一刻都还有戏。

末门击球员的命，多少有些难熬。因为他打出的最佳局面随时可能因搭档出局而胎死腹中，所以他一般觉得，先稳住状态再去谋分实在不值当，于是逢球就想抢分，能捞着六分八分便自认走运。里斯却把生命看得更严肃些。他把末门击球练成了一门艺术。有一回对蝴蝶队（Butterflies），他一口气拿下六十分保持不离场；他总有再来一场的可能。今天，另一头站着的又是普林格尔，他满心指望在桩门边消磨上愉快的一两个钟头。

投球手看最后一名击球员的眼光，和看前面十个从不一样，本能地就要小瞧他三分。M.C.C.的快球手是个有想法的人。他的想法是：他能投出一种巧夺天工的慢球。往常，舆论都反对他拿实战来试验。历任队长都惯于粗声粗气地质问他是不是在打弹子。眼下M.C.C.队长问的也正是这句——只见那颗球慢吞吞地飘过半空，被里斯干脆利落地一杆抽进看台（Pavilion）。投球手咧嘴一笑，恢复了常规球速。



===Original===

But everything came alike to Reece. Pringle, too, continued his career of triumph. Gradually the score rose from a hundred and seventy to two hundred. Pringle cut and drove in all directions, with the air of a prince of the blood royal distributing largesse. The second century went up to the accompaniment of cheers.

Then the slow bowler reaped his reward, for Pringle, after putting his first two balls over the screen, was caught on the boundary off the third. He had contributed eighty-one to a total of two hundred and thirteen.

So far Gethryn’s absence had not been noticed. But when the umpires had gone out, and the School were getting ready to take the field, inquiries were made.


===Chinese===

可什么球到了里斯手里都是一样。普林格尔也继续着他的凯旋征程。比分从一百七十分渐渐爬到了两百分。普林格尔切球抽球，四面开花，那气派宛如王室亲王在广施恩赏。第二百分挂上记分牌时，伴随着一片欢呼。

随后慢速投球手收获了他的报偿：普林格尔把头两球打过挡板，第三球却在边界被接杀了。他为二百一十三分的总成绩贡献了八十一分。

到此为止，还没人留意到盖斯林不在。可等裁判们出场、校队整装待发上场时，有人开始打听了。



===Original===

“You might begin at the top end, Gosling,” said Norris.

“Right,” said Samuel. “Who’s going on at the other?”

“Baynes. Hullo, where’s Gethryn?”

“Isn’t he here? Perhaps he’s in the Pavi﻿—”

“Any of you chaps seen Gethryn?”


===Chinese===

“戈斯林，你就从上端先投吧，”诺里斯说。

“成，”塞缪尔（Samuel）应道。“另一头谁上？”

“贝恩斯。咦，盖斯林人呢？”

“他不在吗？说不定在看——”

“你们谁见着盖斯林了？”



===Original===

“He isn’t in the Pav.,” said Baker. “I’ve just come out of the First room myself, and he wasn’t there. Shouldn’t wonder if he’s over at Leicester’s.”

“Dash the man,” said Norris, “he might have known we’d be going out to field soon. Anyhow, we can’t wait for him. We shall have to field a sub. till he turns up.”

“Lorimer’s in the Pav., changed,” said Pringle.

“All right. He’ll do.”

And, reinforced by the gratified Lorimer, the team went on its way.


===Chinese===

“他不在看台里，”贝克（Baker）说。“我自己刚从一队更衣室出来，没瞧见他。多半是去莱斯特宿舍那边了。”

“这该死的家伙，”诺里斯说。“他也该想到我们马上就要上场防守了。不管怎样，总不能干等他。他露面之前，咱们只好先派个替补顶着。”

“洛里默（Lorimer）在看台里，球衣都换好了，”普林格尔说。

“行，就他吧。”

于是，得到喜出望外的洛里默补充之后，队伍开拔了。



===Original===

In the beginning the fortunes of the School prospered. Gosling opened, as was his custom, at a tremendous pace, and seemed to trouble the first few batsmen considerably. A worried-looking little person who had fielded with immense zeal during the School innings at cover-point took the first ball. It was very fast, and hit him just under the kneecap. The pain, in spite of the pad, appeared to be acute. The little man danced vigorously for some time, and then, with much diffidence, prepared himself for the second instalment.

Now, when on the cricket field, the truculent Samuel was totally deficient in all the finer feelings, such as pity and charity. He could see that the batsman was in pain, and yet his second ball was faster than the first. It came in quickly from the off. The little batsman went forward in a hesitating, halfhearted manner, and played a clear two inches inside the ball. The off-stump shot out of the ground.

“Bowled, Sammy,” said Norris from his place in the slips.


===Chinese===

开局阶段，校队运势颇佳。戈斯林照他的老规矩，开局便全速开火，头几位击球员似乎被他折腾得不轻。校队进攻轮里那位在掩切位（cover-point）防守得热火朝天的矮个子愁面客，迎来了第一球。球速极快，正撞在他膝盖骨下方。隔着护腿板，疼得显然不轻。小个子原地蹦跶了好一阵，然后战战兢兢地摆好架势，迎接第二份馈赠。

须知塞缪尔这人一上板球场，怜悯呀、仁慈呀这类高级情感便一概欠奉。他明瞧见击球员在疼，第二球却比第一球更快。球从外角急促切进来。矮个击球员犹犹豫豫、无精打采地迎前一步，球杆结结实实空出了两英寸。外柱应声飞出场地。

“投杀了，萨米（Sammy），”诺里斯在滑位区（slips）上他的位置说道。



===Original===

The next man was a clergyman, a large man who suggested possibilities in the way of hitting. But Gosling was irresistible. For three balls the priest survived. But the last of the over, a fast yorker on the leg stump, was too much for him, and he retired.

Two for none. The critic in the deck chair felt that the match was as good as over.

But this idyllic state of things was not to last. The newcomer, a tall man with a light moustache, which he felt carefully after every ball, soon settled down. He proved to be a conversationalist. Until he had opened his account, which he did with a strong drive to the ropes, he was silent. When, however, he had seen the ball safely to the boundary, he turned to Reece and began.

“Rather a nice one, that. Eh, what? Yes. Got it just on the right place, you know. Not a bad bat this, is it? What? Yes. One of Slogbury and Whangham’s Sussex Spankers, don’t you know. Chose it myself. Had it in pickle all the winter. Yes.”


===Chinese===

下一位是个牧师，块头不小，一看就颇有几分击球的本钱。可戈斯林锐不可当。这位教士撑过了三球。但这一轮的最后一球——一记直奔腿柱的快速约克球——他实在消受不起，就此退场。

两门零分。躺椅里那位评论家觉得，这场比赛已经等于是赢了。

可这般田园牧歌的景象没能持久。新上场的是个高个子，唇上一撇淡色小胡子，每打完一球都要伸手细细摸一遍。他很快就站稳了脚跟，而且事实证明，这位是个话痨。开张之前他一言不发——他的开张，是一记力道十足、直奔围绳的抽击。可等他目送那球稳稳到了边界，便转过脸对里斯开了腔。

“这一杆相当漂亮，是吧？呃，什么？是啊。正好打在点子上，您瞧。这球杆不赖吧？什么？是的。斯洛格伯里与王汉姆（Slogbury and Whangham）家的‘萨塞克斯重炮（Sussex Spankers）’，知道吧。我亲手挑的。抹好油收了一冬天。没错。”



===Original===

“Play, sir,” from the umpire.

“Eh, what? Oh, right. Yes, good make these Sussex﻿—*Spankers*. Oh, well fielded.”

At the word spankers he had effected another drive, but Marriott at mid-off had stopped it prettily.

Soon it began to occur to Norris that it would be advisable to have a change of bowling.
