Gosling was getting tired, and Baynes apparently offered no difficulties to the batsman on the perfect wicket, the conversational man in particular being very severe upon him. It was at such a crisis that the Bishop should have come in. He was Gosling’s understudy. But where was he? The innings had been in progress over half an hour now, and still there were no signs of him. A man, thought Norris, who could cut off during the M.C.C. match (of all matches!), probably on some rotten business of his own, was beyond the pale, and must, on reappearance, be fallen upon and rent. He﻿—here something small and red whizzed at his face. He put up his hands to protect himself. The ball struck them and bounded out again. When a fast bowler is bowling a slip he should not indulge in absentmindedness. The conversational man had received his first life, and, as he was careful to explain to Reece, it was a curious thing, but whenever he was let off early in his innings he always made fifty, and as a rule a century. Gosling’s analysis was spoilt, and the match in all probability lost. And Norris put it all down to Gethryn. If he had been there, this would not have happened.


===Chinese===

“请击球，先生。”裁判发话。

“呃，什么？哦，好。是啊，这萨塞克斯——*重炮*做工没话说。哦，防得漂亮。”

说到“重炮”二字的同时他又完成了一记抽击，只可惜马里奥特在投球侧内场（mid-off）截得干净漂亮。

很快诺里斯便琢磨起来：换投怕是势在必行了。戈斯林渐显疲态，而在这片完好的球道上，贝恩斯显然难不倒击球员——那位话痨尤其对他痛下杀手。正是在这种关口，“主教”本该顶上来。他是戈斯林的替补。可他人呢？这轮进攻已经打了半个多钟头，还是不见踪影。诺里斯心想，一个能在M.C.C.比赛——天底下的比赛挑哪场不好偏偏这场！——当逃兵、八成是忙他自己那点破事的人，已经出了圈，等他再露面，必须扑上去把他撕个稀碎。他正——这时一个红色的小东西嗖地直奔他面门。他抬手护脸。球撞上双手又弹了出去。快球手在投球而自己守滑位区时，切忌心不在焉。话痨捡回了第一条命，而且，正如他郑重向里斯解释的那样：说来也怪，但每逢一轮之初被人放一马，他总能打出五十分，惯例而言是一百分。戈斯林的战绩统计毁了，比赛十有八九也输了。而诺里斯把这一切统统记在了盖斯林头上。他要是到场，这事故本不会发生。



===Original===

“Sorry, Gosling,” he said.

“All right,” said Gosling, though thinking quite the reverse. And he walked back to bowl his next ball, conjuring up a beautiful vision in his mind. J. Douglas and Braund were fielding slip to him in the vision, while in the background Norris appeared, in a cauldron of boiling oil.

“Tut, tut,” said Baker facetiously to the raging captain.

Baker’s was essentially a flippant mind. Not even a moment of solemn agony, such as this, was sacred to him.

Norris was icy and severe.


===Chinese===

“对不住，戈斯林，”他说。

“没事儿，”戈斯林嘴上说着，心里的想法恰恰相反。他走回去投下一球，脑中升起一幅美妙的图景：图景里，杰·道格拉斯（J. Douglas）和布劳恩德（Braund）替他镇守滑位区，而在背景处，诺里斯正泡在一口沸油大锅里。

“啧啧，”贝克冲怒火中烧的队长打趣道。

贝克骨子里就是个轻浮头脑。就连眼下这种庄严苦痛的时刻，在他眼里也无甚神圣可言。

诺里斯冷若冰霜，声色俱厉。



===Original===

“If you want to rot about, Baker,” he said, “perhaps you’d better go and play stump-cricket with the juniors.”

“Well,” retorted Baker, with great politeness, “I suppose seeing you miss a gaper like that right into your hands made me think I was playing stump-cricket with the juniors.”

At this point the conversation ceased, Baker suddenly remembering that he had not yet received his First Eleven colours, and that it would therefore be rash to goad the captain too freely, while Norris, for his part, recalled the fact that Baker had promised to do some Latin verse for him that evening, and might, if crushed with some scathing repartee, refuse to go through with that contract. So there was silence in the slips.


===Chinese===

“你要是想闹着玩，贝克，”他说，“最好去跟低年级的玩残桩板球（stump-cricket）。”

“哦，”贝克彬彬有礼地回敬，“眼瞧着那么大一个高飞球正掉进您手里还给漏了，我大概是错以为自己正在跟低年级的玩残桩板球。”

话到此处便中断了：贝克猛然想起自己的一队队色还没到手，把队长撩拨得太狠未免莽撞；诺里斯则记起贝克答应过当晚替他赶几行拉丁韵文，倘若被一句刻薄的妙语碾得体无完肤，对方保不齐要撕毁合同。于是滑位区上一片肃静。



===Original===

The partnership was broken at last by a lucky accident. The conversationalist called his partner for a short run, and when that unfortunate gentleman had sprinted some twenty yards, reconsidered the matter and sent him back. Reece had the bails off before the victim had completed a third of the return journey.

For some time after this matters began to favour the School again. With the score at a hundred and five, three men left in two overs, one bowled by Gosling, the others caught at point and in the deep off Jennings, who had deposed Baynes. Six wickets were now down, and the enemy still over a hundred behind.

But the M.C.C. in its school matches has this peculiarity. However badly it may seem to stand, there is always something up its sleeve. In this case it was a professional, a man indecently devoid of anything in the shape of nerves. He played the bowling with a stolid confidence, amounting almost to contempt, which struck a chill to the hearts of the School bowlers. It did worse. It induced them to bowl with the sole object of getting the conversationalist at the batting end, thus enabling the professional to pile up an unassuming but rapidly increasing score by means of threes and singles.

As for the conversationalist, he had made thirty or more, and wanted all the bowling he could get.


===Chinese===

这对搭档最终毁在一场走运的意外上。话痨喊搭档跑个短分，等那位倒霉先生冲刺了约莫二十码，又重新斟酌了一下，把他唤了回去。里斯摘下横木的时候，那位牺牲品返程还不到三分之一。

此后一段时间，天平又向校队倾斜。比分一百零五分时，两轮之内连折三人：一个被戈斯林投杀，另两个分别在三切位和外野深处接杀于詹宁斯之手——他已取代了贝恩斯。六门已失，对方还落后一百多分。

但M.C.C.打学校比赛自有一样怪癖：无论局面看着多糟，袖子里总还藏着点东西。这回藏着的是一位职业球员，此人神经粗得没天理。他应付投球时的那股木然自信近乎轻蔑，看得校队众投手心里发凉。这还不算，它更诱得众投手专攻击球端的话痨，好让那位职业球员靠着一分三分，不声不响地、飞快地攒起一串分数。

至于那位话痨，他已得了三十分开外，来者不拒，有多少球接多少球。



===Original===

“It’s a very curious thing,” he said to Reece, as he faced Gosling, after his partner had scored a three off the first ball of the over, “but some fellows simply detest fast bowling. Now I﻿—” He never finished the sentence. When he spoke again it was to begin a new one.

“How on earth did that happen?” he asked.

“I think it bowled you,” said Reece stolidly, picking up the two stumps which had been uprooted by Gosling’s express.

“Yes. But how? Dash it! What? I can’t underst﻿—. Most curious thing I ever﻿—dash it all, you know.”

He drifted off in the direction of the Pavilion, stopping on the way to ask short leg his opinion of the matter.


===Chinese===

“说来真是怪事一桩，”他迎着戈斯林站定，对里斯说道——此前他的搭档刚在这一轮第一球上拿下三分，“有些人偏偏恨死了快速投球。可我——”这句话他没能说完。再开口时，已是另一句的开头。

“这到底是怎么发生的？”他问。

“我想是把你投杀了，”里斯木着脸说，一边捡起那两根被戈斯林的特快球连根掀翻的柱子。

“是啊。可怎么投杀的？见鬼！什么？我想不通——。我这辈子头一回见这么邪门的——真见鬼，你知道。”

他失魂落魄地朝看台飘去，半路还停下来，征求近腿位（short leg）对此事的见解。



===Original===

“Bowled, Sammy,” said Reece, putting on the bails.

“Well bowled, Gosling,” growled Norris from the slips.

“Sammy the marvel, by Jove,” said Marriott. “Switch it on, Samuel, more and more.”

“I wish Norris would give me a rest. Where on earth is that man Gethryn?”

“Rum, isn’t it? There’s going to be something of a row about it. Norris seems to be getting rather shirty. Hullo! here comes the Deathless Author.”


===Chinese===

“投杀了，萨米，”里斯一边把横木装回去，一边说。

“投得好，戈斯林，”诺里斯在滑位区上低吼。

“萨米真是个奇才，老天，”马里奥特说。“加足马力，塞缪尔，越来越猛。”

“但愿诺里斯能让我歇口气。盖斯林那家伙到底死哪儿去了？”

“蹊跷，是不是？这事儿要起一场风波了。诺里斯好像火气不小。咦！不朽作家（the Deathless Author）驾到了。”



===Original===

The author referred to was the new batsman, a distinguished novelist, who played a good deal for the M.C.C. He broke his journey to the wicket to speak to the conversationalist, who was still engaged with short leg.

“Bates, old man,” he said, “if you’re going to the Pavilion you might wait for me. I shall be out in an hour or two.”

Upon which Bates, awaking suddenly to the position of affairs, went on his way.

With the arrival of the Deathless Author an unwelcome change came over the game. His cricket style resembled his literary style. Both were straightforward and vigorous. The first two balls he received from Gosling he drove hard past cover point to the ropes. Gosling, who had been bowling unchanged since the innings began, was naturally feeling a little tired. He was losing his length, and bowling more slowly than was his wont. Norris now gave him a rest for a few overs, Bruce going on with rather innocuous medium left-hand bowling. The professional continued to jog along slowly. The novelist hit. Everything seemed to come alike to him. Gosling resumed, but without effect, while at the other end bowler after bowler was tried. From a hundred and ten the score rose and rose, and still the two remained together. A hundred and ninety went up, and Norris in despair threw the ball to Marriott.


===Chinese===

此位作家便是新上场的击球员，一位名头不小的小说家，常替M.C.C.出战。他走到半路还停下来，跟仍在与近腿位深谈的话痨搭话。

“贝茨（Bates），老伙计，”他说，“你要是回看台，不妨等等我。我一两个钟头内准出局。”

闻听此言，贝茨猛然认清了形势，接着赶他的路去了。

不朽作家一到，比赛便起了个不受欢迎的变化。他的板球风格酷似他的文风：两者都朴实无华而力道千钧。戈斯林投给他的头两球，都被他硬生生从掩切位身边抽过，直奔围绳。戈斯林自这一轮开赛起便没歇过手，自然已经有点乏了。他的落点渐渐失准，球速也慢于往常。诺里斯于是让他休息了几轮，换布鲁斯上一手不痛不痒的左手中速球。职业球员继续不紧不慢地往前磨。小说家则大打出手。什么球到他手里好像都一个样。戈斯林重新上场，却毫无建树；另一头更是换了一个投球手又一个。比分从一百一十分起节节攀升，那两位却始终厮守在一起。一百九十分挂上了牌，绝望的诺里斯把球扔给了马里奥特。



===Original===

“Here you are, Marriott,” he said, “I’m afraid we shall have to try you.”

“That’s what I call really nicely expressed,” said Marriott to the umpire. “Yes, over the wicket.”

Marriott was a slow, “House-match” sort of bowler. That is to say, in a House match he was quite likely to get wickets, but in a First Eleven match such an event was highly improbable. His bowling looked very subtle, and if the ball was allowed to touch the ground it occasionally broke quite a remarkable distance.

The forlorn hope succeeded. The professional for the first time in his innings took a risk. He slashed at a very mild ball almost a wide on the off side. The ball touched the corner of the bat, and soared up in the direction of cover-point, where Pringle held it comfortably.


===Chinese===

“接着，马里奥特，”他说，“恐怕只好请你出马试试了。”

“这话说的，可真够婉转动听的，”马里奥特对裁判说。“好，从三柱门外侧投。”

马里奥特是个慢速的“宿舍赛”级投球手。也就是说，在宿舍赛里他拿下三柱门相当有戏，可在一队比赛里，这种事概率渺茫。他的投球看着极尽诡谲，而只要那球被允许沾一次地，偶尔还能变向变出相当可观的一段距离。

孤注一掷居然奏了效。职业球员一轮下来头一回冒了险。他劈砍一记几乎是外角歪球的温吞球。球擦着球杆边角，朝掩切位方向腾空而起，普林格尔在那里舒舒服服把它收进手中。



===Original===

“There you are,” said Marriott, “when you put a really scientific bowler on you’re bound to get a wicket. Why on earth didn’t I go on before, Norris?”

“You wait,” said Norris, “there are five more balls of the over to come.”

“Bad job for the batsman,” said Marriott.

There had been time for a run before the ball reached Pringle, so that the novelist was now at the batting end. Marriott’s next ball was not unlike his first, but it was straighter, and consequently easier to get at. The novelist hit it into the road. When it had been brought back he hit it into the road again. Marriott suggested that he had better have a man there.

The fourth ball of the over was too wide to hit with any comfort, and the batsman let it alone. The fifth went for four to square leg, almost killing the umpire on its way, and the sixth soared in the old familiar manner into the road again. Marriott’s over had yielded exactly twenty-two runs. Four to win and two wickets to fall.


===Chinese===

“瞧见没，”马里奥特说，“派上一位真正讲科学的投球手，三柱门还不是手到擒来。诺里斯，早干嘛不让我上？”

“你等着，”诺里斯说，“这一轮还有五球呢。”

“那可要苦了击球员喽，”马里奥特说。

球到普林格尔手里之前来得及跑一分，于是此刻轮到小说家站上击球端。马里奥特的下一球与第一球相去不远，只是更直，因而也更好下杆。小说家把它打上了大路。球捡回来之后，他又把它打上了大路。马里奥特提议，不如在那边马路上安排个人。

这一轮的第四球偏得太远，打着费劲，击球员干脆放行。第五球直奔方腿位拿了四分，半路上差点要了裁判的命；第六球则按那熟悉的老路子，又一次腾空飞上大路。马里奥特这一轮整整送出二十二分。还差四分获胜，还剩两门。



===Original===

“I’ll never read another of that man’s books as long as I live,” said Marriott to Gosling, giving him the ball. “You’re our only hope, Sammy. Do go in and win.”

The new batsman had the bowling. He snicked his first ball for a single, bringing the novelist to the fore again, and Samuel Wilberforce Gosling vowed a vow that he would dismiss that distinguished novelist.

But the best intentions go for nothing when one’s arm is feeling like lead. Of all the miserable balls sent down that afternoon that one of Gosling’s was the worst. It was worse than anything of Marriott’s. It flew sluggishly down the pitch well outside the leg stump. The novelist watched it come, and his eye gleamed. It was about to bounce for the second time when, with a pleased smile, the batsman stepped out. There was a loud, musical report, the note of a bat when it strikes the ball fairly on the driving spot.

The man of letters shaded his eyes with his hand, and watched the ball diminish in the distance.

“I rather think,” said he cheerfully, as a crash of glass told of its arrival at the Pavilion, “that that does it.”

He was perfectly right. It did.


===Chinese===

“这辈子我再不读这人写的书了，”马里奥特把球递给戈斯林，说道。“你是我们唯一的指望了，萨米。千万顶住拿下。”

新击球员迎战投球。第一球他杆边一削拿了个单分，又把小说家换到了前头。塞缪尔·威尔伯福斯·戈斯林（Samuel Wilberforce Gosling）当即对天起誓：定要将那位声名显赫的小说家送出局。

可人的胳膊一旦沉得像灌了铅，再好的决心也是白搭。那天下午所有倒霉球里，戈斯林这一记是最倒霉的，比马里奥特的任何一球都惨。它有气无力地顺着球道飘下来，离腿柱还老远。小说家望着它过来，眼里放光。球将要第二次弹地时，击球员面带愉快的微笑跨步出击。一声响亮而悦耳的脆响——那是球杆正正咬中抽击点时的音色。

这位文坛中人抬手搭起凉棚，望着球在远处越缩越小。

“我琢磨着，”玻璃一声脆响宣告球抵达看台时，他喜滋滋地说，“这一下成了。”

他说得再对不过。成了。

## III The Uncle Makes Himself at Home / 第三章 叔叔安顿下来

===Original===

“But, dash it,” said Gethryn, when he had finished gasping, “that must be rot!”

“Not a bit,” said the self-possessed youth. “Your mater was my elder sister. You’ll find it works out all right. Look here. A, the daughter of B and C, marries. No, look here. I was born when you were four. See?”

Then the demoralized Bishop remembered. He had heard of his juvenile uncle, but the tales had made little impression upon him. Till now they had not crossed one another’s tracks.


===Chinese===

“可是，见鬼，”盖斯林好不容易喘匀了气，说道，“这不可能是真的吧！”

“千真万确，”那位镇定自若的少年说。“你妈是我姐。你算算，保准对得上。听着。设甲为乙、丙之女，婚配——不对，你听我说。你四岁那年，我出生。明白？”

这时方寸大乱的“主教”才想了起来。他听说过自己有这么个年幼的舅舅，可那些传闻没给他留下多少印象。在此之前，两人的足迹从未交会。



===Original===

“Oh, all right,” said he, “I’ll take your word for it. You seem to have been getting up the subject.”

“Yes. Thought you might want to know about it. I say, how far is it to Beckford, and how do you get there?”

Up till now Gethryn had scarcely realized that his uncle was actually coming to the School for good. These words brought the fact home to him.


===Chinese===

“哦，行吧，”他说，“这话我就信了。你小子对这个题目下过功夫啊。”

“是啊。想着你兴许想了解了解。我说，到贝克福德还有多远，怎么个走法？”

直到这时，盖斯林才咂摸出味儿来：他这位舅舅是当真要来学校长住了。这几句话让他把事实看了个通透。



===Original===

“Oh, Lord,” he said, “are you coming to Beckford?”

The thought of having his footsteps perpetually dogged by an uncle four years younger than himself, and manifestly a youth with a fine taste in cheek, was not pleasant.

“Of course,” said his uncle. “What did you think I was going to do? Camp out on the platform?”

“What House are you in?”

“Leicester’s.”


===Chinese===

“老天爷，”他说，“你要来贝克福德？”

一想到自己身后要永远跟条尾巴——一个比他小四岁、且显然脸皮功夫颇为精湛的少年——他就乐不起来。

“那当然，”他舅舅说。“不然你以为我打算干什么？在站台上扎营？”

“你分在哪个宿舍？”

“莱斯特舍。”



===Original===

The worst had happened. The bitter cup was full, the iron neatly inserted in Gethryn’s soul. In his most pessimistic moments he had never looked forward to the coming term so gloomily as he did now. His uncle noted his lack of enthusiasm, and attributed it to anxiety on behalf of himself.

“What’s up?” he asked. “Isn’t Leicester’s all right? Is Leicester a beast?”

“No. He’s a perfectly decent sort of man. It’s a good enough House. At least it will be this term. I was only thinking of something.”


===Chinese===

最坏的情形成了真。苦杯斟到了满溢，一根烧红的铁条利利索索插进了盖斯林的灵魂。他就算在最悲观的时刻，也没像现在这样把即将到来的学期想得如此灰暗。他舅舅注意到他热情缺缺，把这归因于对方替自己担忧。

“怎么了？”他问。“莱斯特舍不行吗？莱斯特是个混蛋？”

“不是。他是个再正派不过的人。那宿舍挺好。至少这学期会挺好。我刚才只是想起了别的事。”



===Original===

“I see. Well, how do you get to the place?”

“Walk. It isn’t far.”

“How far?”

“Three miles.”

“The porter said four.”


===Chinese===

“我懂了。那么，这地方怎么去？”

“走路。不远。”

“多远？”

“三英里。”

“搬运工说是四英里。”



===Original===

“It may be four. I never measured it.”

“Well, how the dickens do you think I’m going to walk four miles with luggage? I wish you wouldn’t rot.”

And before Gethryn could quite realize that he, the head of Leicester’s, the second-best bowler in the School, and the best centre three-quarter the School had had for four seasons, had been requested in a peremptory manner by a youth of fourteen, a mere kid, not to rot, the offender was talking to a cabman out of the reach of retaliation. Gethryn became more convinced every minute that this was no ordinary kid.


===Chinese===

“兴许是四英里。我又没量过。”

“好啊，那你倒是说说看，我拖着行李，你指望我怎么走完四英里？拜托你别闹了。”

还没等盖斯林完全反应过来——他，莱斯特舍的舍长、全校第二好的投球手、四个赛季以来全校最好的中路三分卫——竟被一个十四岁的少年、一个乳臭未干的小毛孩，用不容分说的口吻告诫“别闹”，这位冒犯者已经跑去跟马车夫搭话，进了报复够不着的射程。盖斯林一分钟比一分钟确信：这不是寻常的小毛孩。



===Original===

“This man says,” observed Farnie, returning to Gethryn, “that he’ll drive me up to the College for seven bob. As it’s a short four miles, and I’ve only got two boxes, it seems to me that he’s doing himself fairly well. What do you think?”

“Nobody ever gives more than four bob,” said Gethryn.

“I told you so,” said Farnie to the cabman. “You are a bally swindler,” he added admiringly.

“Look ’ere,” began the cabman, in a pained voice.


===Chinese===

“这位先生说，”法尼回到盖斯林身边，发表见闻道，“他肯拉我去学院，要七先令。区区四英里短途，我统共两口箱子，要我看，他给自己盘算得相当不薄。你以为如何？”

“从来没有人给超过四先令的，”盖斯林说。

“我早跟你说了，”法尼对马车夫说。“你个地道的坑人老手，”他钦佩地补了一句。

“你听我说——”马车夫声音发苦地开了腔。



===Original===

“Oh, dry up,” said Farnie. “Want a lift, Gethryn?”

The words were spoken not so much as from equal to equal as in a tone of airy patronage which made the Bishop’s blood boil. But as he intended to instil a few words of wisdom into his uncle’s mind, he did not refuse the offer.

The cabman, apparently accepting the situation as one of those slings and arrows of outrageous fortune which no man can hope to escape, settled down on the box, clicked up his horse, and drove on towards the College.


===Chinese===

“哦，闭嘴吧，”法尼说。“要搭车吗，盖斯林？”

这句话说出口，与其说是平等相待，不如说带着一种轻飘飘的施主腔，听得“主教”血往上撞。但他本打算往他舅舅脑袋里灌几句金玉良言，便没有拒绝这份好意。

马车夫显然把这一局面归入了无人能幸免的命运之矢一类，往车座上一坐，一磕马儿，朝学院驶去。



===Original===

“What sort of a hole is Beckford?” asked Farnie, after the silence had lasted some time.

“I find it good enough personally,” said Gethryn. “If you’d let us know earlier that you were coming, we’d have had the place done up a bit for you.”

This, of course, was feeble, distinctly feeble. But the Bishop was not feeling himself. The essay in sarcasm left the would-be victim entirely uncrushed. He should have shrunk and withered up, or at the least have blushed. But he did nothing of the sort. He merely smiled in his supercilious way, until the Bishop felt very much inclined to spring upon him and throw him out of the cab.

There was another pause.


===Chinese===

“贝克福德是个怎样的地界？”沉默了好一阵之后，法尼问。

“我个人觉得够好了，”盖斯林说。“你要是早点通知我们你要来，我们还能把这地方给你拾掇拾掇。”

这话当然很弱，相当地弱。可“主教”此刻不在状态。这篇讽刺小品的预定受害者毫发无损。他本该当场枯萎蔫缩，至少也该红一红脸。可他什么也没做，只是带着他那副居高临下的微笑，笑得“主教”手痒难耐，恨不得扑过去把他从车里扔出去。

又是一阵冷场。



===Original===

“Farnie,” began Gethryn at last.

“Um?”

“Doesn’t it strike you that for a kid like you you’ve got a good deal of edge on?” asked Gethryn.

Farnie effected a masterly counter-stroke. He pretended not to be able to hear. He was sorry, but would the Bishop mind repeating his remark.

“Eh? What?” he said. “Very sorry, but this cab’s making such a row. I say, cabby, why don’t you sign the pledge, and save your money up to buy a new cab? Eh? Oh, sorry! I wasn’t listening.” Now, inasmuch as the whole virtue of the “wretched-little-kid-like-you” argument lies in the crisp despatch with which it is delivered, Gethryn began to find, on repeating his observation for the third time, that there was not quite so much in it as he had thought. He prudently elected to change his style of attack.


===Chinese===

“法尼，”盖斯林终于开了口。

“嗯？”

“你不觉得吗，就你这么个小不点，嘴皮子是不是太不饶人了点？”盖斯林问。

法尼使出了一记大师级的回手。他假装听不见。很抱歉，能不能劳驾“主教”把刚才的话再说一遍。

“呃？什么？”他说。“真抱歉，这马车吵得邪乎。我说车夫，你干嘛不立个戒酒誓，省下钱来买辆新车？呃？哦，对不住！刚才没听着。”须知，“你这可怜小毛孩”论的全部威力，正在于出口时的干脆利落；等到第三遍复述之际，盖斯林开始发现，这话里的斤两不如他原先想的那么足。他很识相地决定换个进攻路数。



===Original===

“It doesn’t matter,” he said wearily, as Farnie opened his mouth to demand a fourth encore, “it wasn’t anything important. Now, look here, I just want to give you a few tips about what to do when you get to the Coll. To start with, you’ll have to take off that white tie you’ve got on. Black and dark blue are the only sorts allowed here.”

“How about yours then?” Gethryn was wearing a somewhat sweet thing in brown and yellow.

“Mine happens to be a First Eleven tie.”

“Oh! Well, as a matter of fact, you know, I was going to take off my tie. I always do, especially at night. It’s a sort of habit I’ve got into.”

“Not quite so much of your beastly cheek, please,” said Gethryn.

“Right-ho!” said Farnie cheerfully, and silence, broken only by the shrieking of the cab wheels, brooded once more over the cab. Then Gethryn, feeling that perhaps it would be a shame to jump too severely on a new boy on his first day at a large public school, began to think of something conciliatory to say. “Look here,” he said, “you’ll get on all right at Beckford, I expect. You’ll find Leicester’s a fairly decent sort of House. Anyhow, you needn’t be afraid you’ll get bullied. There’s none of that sort of thing at School nowadays.”


===Chinese===

“没什么要紧的，”他疲惫地说，这时法尼正张嘴要求第四次返场，“不是什么重要的事。听着，我只是想给你几句忠告，教你到校之后该怎么行事。头一条，你脖子上那条白领带必须摘了。这儿只许戴黑的和深蓝的。”

“那你那条呢？”盖斯林本人脖子上正系着一款棕黄相间的俏货。

“我这条可是一队领带。”

“哦！这个嘛，实不相瞒，我本来也正打算摘领带。我一向如此，尤其夜里。算是养成了这么个习惯。”

“你那臭不要脸的贫嘴，麻烦收一收，”盖斯林说。

“得嘞！”法尼欢快地说。于是，只剩下车轮吱嘎尖叫的寂静，重新笼罩了马车。随后盖斯林寻思，一个新孩子头一天进大学校就往死里踩，未免太不厚道，便开始搜肠刮肚找几句安抚话。“听着，”他说，“你在贝克福德会过得不错的，我估摸。莱斯特舍是个挺像样的宿舍。再说，你也不用怕挨欺负。如今的学校里早没那一套了。”



===Original===

“Really?”

“Yes, and there’s another thing I ought to warn you about. Have you brought much money with you?”

“ ’Bout fourteen pounds, I fancy,” said Farnie carelessly.

“Fourteen *what*!” said the amazed Bishop. “*Pounds!*”

“Or sovereigns,” said Farnie. “Each worth twenty shillings, you know.”


===Chinese===

“真的？”

“真的。还有件事我得提醒你。你随身带了不少钱吧？”

“大概十四镑吧，我猜，”法尼漫不经心地说。

“十四*什么*！”大惊失色的“主教”说。“*镑*！”

“要么说是金镑（sovereign）也行。一枚顶二十先令，你知道吧。”



===Original===

For a moment Gethryn’s only feeling was one of unmixed envy. Previously he had considered himself passing rich on thirty shillings a term. He had heard legends, of course, of individuals who come to School bursting with bullion, but never before had he set eyes upon such an one. But after a time it began to dawn upon him that for a new boy at a public school, and especially at such a House as Leicester’s had become under the rule of the late Reynolds and his predecessors, there might be such a thing as having too much money.

“How the deuce did you get all that?” he asked.

“My pater gave it me. He’s absolutely cracked on the subject of pocket-money. Sometimes he doesn’t give me a sou, and sometimes he’ll give me whatever I ask for.”

“But you don’t mean to say you had the cheek to ask for fourteen quid?”

“I asked for fifteen. Got it, too. I’ve spent a pound of it. I said I wanted to buy a bike. You can get a jolly good bike for five quid about, so you see I scoop ten pounds. What?”


===Chinese===

有那么一刻，盖斯林心里只有一种不掺假的艳羡。此前他一直觉得自己一学期三十先令已算腰缠万贯。当然，那种揣着金锭来上学的传奇人物他也听过，只是从未亲眼得见。可过了一阵他渐渐醒悟：对一个公学新生来说——尤其是在前任舍长雷诺兹（Reynolds）及其历任前任治下已然变成这副光景的莱斯特舍——钱太多，未必不是一种祸事。

“这么多钱你究竟怎么弄到手的？”他问。

“我爸给的。他在零花钱这个题目上彻底没救了。有时一分钱不给我，有时我要多少给多少。”

“你总不至于说，你竟有脸张口要十四镑吧？”

“我要的是十五镑。还真要着了。我已经花掉一镑。我说要买辆自行车。差不多五镑就能买辆挺不赖的车，你算算，我净赚十镑。怎么样？”



===Original===

This ingenious, if slightly unscrupulous, feat gave Gethryn an insight into his uncle’s character which up till now he had lacked. He began to see that the moral advice with which he had primed himself would be out of place. Evidently this youth could take quite good care of himself on his own account. Still, even a budding Professor Moriarty would be none the worse for being warned against Gethryn’s bête noire, Monk, so the Bishop proceeded to deliver that warning.

“Well,” he said, “you seem to be able to look out for yourself all right, I must say. But there’s one tip I really can give you. When you get to Leicester’s, and a beast with a green complexion and an oily smile comes up and calls you ‘Old Cha-a-p,’ and wants you to swear eternal friendship, tell him it’s not good enough. Squash him!”

“Thanks,” said Farnie. “Who is this genial merchant?”

“Chap called Monk. You’ll recognize him by the smell of scent. When you find the place smelling like an Eau de cologne factory, you’ll know Monk’s somewhere near. Don’t you have anything to do with him.”

“You seem to dislike the gentleman.”


===Chinese===

这一手法纵有几分缺德，却不失精巧，让盖斯林对他舅舅的品性有了一个此前一直空缺的认识。他开始明白，自己预先备好的那番道德训导怕是用不上了。显而易见，这位少年照顾自己绰绰有余。不过，即便是一位莫里亚蒂教授（Professor Moriarty）的坯子，先听听针对盖斯林的头号眼中钉蒙克（Monk）的警告也没什么坏处，于是“主教”便开始发布那道警告。

“这个嘛，”他说，“看得出，你照顾自己完全没问题，这我认了。不过有一条忠告我确实给得了。你到了莱斯特舍，要是有个面色发绿、笑容油腻的家伙凑上来管你叫‘老—伙—计’，还要跟你结拜永恒的友谊，你就告诉他：不够格。踩扁他！”

“多谢，”法尼说。“这位和善的买卖人是哪路神仙？”

“一个叫芒克的。闻香味你就能认出他。哪天你发现四周的空气闻着跟科隆香水厂似的，那就是芒克在附近了。你别跟他沾任何关系。”

“你好像很不喜欢这位先生。”



===Original===

“I bar the man. But that isn’t why I’m giving you the tip to steer clear of him. There are dozens of chaps I bar who haven’t an ounce of vice in them. And there are one or two chaps who have got tons. Monk’s one of them. A fellow called Danvers is another. Also a beast of the name of Waterford. There are some others as well, but those are the worst of the lot. By the way, I forgot to ask, have you ever been to school before?”

“Yes,” said Farnie, in the dreamy voice of one who recalls memories from the misty past, “I was at Harrow before I came here, and at Wellington before I went to Harrow, and at Clifton before I went to Wellington.”

Gethryn gasped.

“Anywhere before you went to Clifton?” he enquired.

“Only private schools.”


===Chinese===

“我看这人不顺眼。不过我提醒你躲着他，倒不是为了这个。我看不顺眼的人多了去了，他们可一丁点儿坏水都没有。反倒有一两个家伙，坏水一肚子。蒙克是一个。一个叫丹弗斯（Danvers）的是另一个。还有个姓沃特福德（Waterford）的混蛋。另外还有几个，不过这几个是最坏的一档。对了，忘问了，你以前上过学没有？”

“上过，”法尼说，用的正是那种从迷雾般的往昔里打捞回忆的恍惚声调，“来这儿之前我在哈罗（Harrow），去哈罗之前在惠灵顿（Wellington），去惠灵顿之前在克利夫顿（Clifton）。”

盖斯林倒抽一口气。

“去克利夫顿之前呢？”他追问。

“只上过私立小学。”



===Original===

The recollection of the platitudes which he had been delivering, under the impression that he was talking to an entirely raw beginner, made Gethryn feel slightly uncomfortable. What must this wanderer, who had seen men and cities, have thought of his harangue?

“Why did you leave Harrow?” asked he.

“Sacked,” was the laconic reply.

Have you ever, asks a modern philosopher, gone upstairs in the dark, and trodden on the last step when it wasn’t there? That sensation and the one Gethryn felt at this unexpected revelation were identical. And the worst of it was that he felt the keenest desire to know why Harrow had seen fit to dispense with the presence of his uncle.

“Why?” he began. “I mean,” he went on hurriedly, “why did you leave Wellington?”


===Chinese===

想起自己方才喋喋不休的那些老生常谈——他原以为是在开导一个彻头彻尾的新手——盖斯林觉得颇不自在。这位见过世面、走南闯北的浪游者，听了他那通训话，心里不定怎么想呢。

“你怎么离开哈罗的？”他问。

“被开了，”回答言简意赅。

有位现代哲人问过：你可曾在黑灯瞎火里上楼，一脚踩在并不存在的最后一级台阶上？盖斯林听到这个意外披露时的感受，与那种感受一模一样。更糟的是，他心里腾起一股强烈的欲望：哈罗究竟为何觉得应当礼送他舅舅出境。

“为什么？”他刚起头，又慌忙接下去，“我是说，你怎么离开惠灵顿的？”



===Original===

“Sacked,” said Farnie again, with the monotonous persistence of a Solomon Eagle.

Gethryn felt at this juncture much as the unfortunate gentleman in *Punch* must have felt, when, having finished a humorous story, the point of which turned upon squinting and red noses, he suddenly discovered that his host enjoyed both those peculiarities. He struggled manfully with his feelings for a time. Tact urged him to discontinue his investigations and talk about the weather. Curiosity insisted upon knowing further details. Just as the struggle was at its height, Farnie came unexpectedly to the rescue.

“It may interest you,” he said, “to know that I was not sacked from Clifton.”

Gethryn with some difficulty refrained from thanking him for the information.

“I never stop at a school long,” said Farnie. “If I don’t get sacked my father takes me away after a couple of terms. I went to four private schools before I started on the public schools. My pater took me away from the first two because he thought the drains were bad, the third because they wouldn’t teach me shorthand, and the fourth because he didn’t like the headmaster’s face. I worked off those schools in a year and a half.” Having finished this piece of autobiography, he relapsed into silence, leaving Gethryn to recollect various tales he had heard of his grandfather’s eccentricity. The silence lasted until the College was reached, when the matron took charge of Farnie, and Gethryn went off to tell Marriott of these strange happenings.


===Chinese===

“被开了，”法尼又说一遍，带着所罗门·伊格尔（Solomon Eagle）式的单调执拗。

盖斯林此刻的心情，颇似《笨拙》（*Punch*）画里那位倒霉绅士：讲完一个拿斜眼和红鼻子当包袱的笑话，却猛然发现主人两样俱全。他刚强地同自己的感情搏斗了一阵。分寸劝他就此打住，改聊天气。好奇心非要问个水落石出。正当搏斗难解难分之际，法尼出人意料地施以援手。

“或许你有兴趣知道，”他说，“克利夫顿倒没开除我。”

盖斯林费了好大劲，才忍住没为这条情报道谢。

“我在一所学校从待不长，”法尼说。“要是没被开除，我爸就会在一两个学期后把我带走。上公学之前，我上过四所私立小学。头两所，我爸嫌下水道不好；第三所，嫌人家不教速记；第四所，嫌校长那张脸不顺眼。一年半，我把那些学校全熬了一遍。”这段自传讲完，他重归沉默，留下盖斯林在那里回想他听说过的外祖父的种种轶事。沉默一直持续到马车抵达学院，随后女管家（matron）接管了法尼，盖斯林则跑去把这些奇遇讲给马里奥特听。



===Original===

Marriott was amused, nor did he attempt to conceal the fact. When he had finished laughing, which was not for some time, he favoured the Bishop with a very sound piece of advice. “If I were you,” he said, “I should try and hush this affair up. It’s all fearfully funny, but I think you’d enjoy life more if nobody knew this kid was your uncle. To see the head of the House going about with a juvenile uncle in his wake might amuse the chaps rather, and you might find it harder to keep order; I won’t let it out, and nobody else knows apparently. Go and square the kid. Oh, I say though, what’s his name? If it’s Gethryn, you’re done. Unless you like to swear he’s a cousin.”

“No; his name’s Farnie, thank goodness.”

“That’s all right then. Go and talk to him.”

Gethryn went to the junior study. Farnie was holding forth to a knot of fags at one end of the room. His audience appeared to be amused at something.

“I say, Farnie,” said the Bishop, “half a second.”


===Chinese===

马里奥特乐了，而且并不掩饰。他笑够了——这费了些工夫——之后，送给“主教”一条非常高明的忠告。“我要是你，”他说，“就想法把这事儿捂住。这事儿滑稽透了，可要是没人知道这小毛孩是你舅舅，我看你的日子会好过些。舍长身后跟着个幼年舅舅到处晃，弟兄们怕是要乐不可支，你管起宿舍来兴许就费劲了。我不会说出去，别处看来也没人知道。去跟那小子通个气。哦对了，我说，他姓什么？要是姓盖斯林，你就完了。除非你乐意发誓说他是个表弟。”

“不；他姓法尼，谢天谢地。”

“那就没事了。去跟他谈谈吧。”

盖斯林去了低年级书房。法尼正对屋子一头的一小圈跑腿小子（fags）高谈阔论。他的听众似乎被什么逗乐了。

“我说，法尼，”“主教”喊道，“借一步说话。”



===Original===

Farnie came out, and Gethryn proceeded to inform him that, all things considered, and proud as he was of the relationship, it was not absolutely essential that he should tell everybody that he was his uncle. In fact, it would be rather better on the whole if he did not. Did he follow?

Farnie begged to observe that he did follow, but that, to his sorrow, the warning came too late.

“I’m very sorry,” he said, “I hadn’t the least idea you wanted the thing kept dark. How was I to know? I’ve just been telling it to some of the chaps in there. Awfully decent chaps. They seemed to think it rather funny. Anyhow, I’m not ashamed of the relationship. Not yet, at any rate.”

For a moment Gethryn seemed about to speak. He looked fixedly at his uncle as he stood framed in the doorway, a cheerful column of cool, calm, concentrated cheek. Then, as if realizing that no words that he knew could do justice to the situation, he raised his foot in silence, and “booted” his own flesh and blood with marked emphasis. After which ceremony he went, still without a word, upstairs again.

As for Farnie, he returned to the junior day-room whistling “Down South” in a soft but cheerful key, and solidified his growing popularity with doles of food from a hamper which he had brought with him. Finally, on retiring to bed and being pressed by the rest of his dormitory for a story, he embarked upon the history of a certain Pollock and an individual referred to throughout as the Porroh Man, the former of whom caused the latter to be decapitated, and was ever afterwards haunted by his head, which appeared to him all day and every day (not excepting Sundays and Bank Holidays) in an upside-down position and wearing a horrible grin. In the end Pollock very sensibly committed suicide (with ghastly details), and the dormitory thanked Farnie in a subdued and chastened manner, and tried, with small success, to go to sleep. In short, Farnie’s first evening at Beckford had been quite a triumph.


===Chinese===

法尼走了出来，盖斯林随即向他说明：通盘考虑之下，尽管他为这层亲缘深感自豪，却并非绝对必要让全校都知道他是他舅舅。事实上，总的来说，不说反而更好。他听明白了吗？

法尼谨表遵命，称自己确实听明白了，只是很遗憾，警告来得太迟了。

“真对不住，”他说。“我一点没想到你想把这事儿瞒下。我哪能知道呢？我刚才正跟屋里几位讲这事呢。都是顶呱呱的好人。他们好像觉得挺逗。反正，我可不以这层亲戚关系为耻。至少眼下还不。”

有那么一瞬，盖斯林似乎要开口。他死死盯着他舅舅——那小子立在门框里，一根兴高采烈的、冷静、镇定、浓缩的厚脸皮柱子。随后，仿佛认定自己所会的任何言语都不足以为此情此景增色，他默默抬起脚，加重语气，把自家血亲“踢”了出去。这套仪式完结，他依旧一言不发，上楼去了。

至于法尼，他回到低年级活动室，用轻快喜悦的调子吹着《南下》（"Down South"），又从随身带来的提篮里分发吃食，使他蒸蒸日上的人气进一步凝固。最后，上床就寝时，同寝室的伙伴缠着他讲故事，他便开讲了一个波洛克（Pollock）其人和一位通篇被称作“波罗人”（Porroh Man）的家伙的历史——前者使得后者身首异处，此后便日日夜夜被那颗头颅纠缠，那头颅不分昼夜（星期天和银行假日也不例外）倒悬于他眼前，咧着一个狰狞的笑。最后波洛克非常理智地自尽（细节惨不忍闻），寝室众人在压抑且受到教益的心情下向法尼道了谢，然后努力入睡，收效甚微。总之，法尼在贝克福德的头一晚上，堪称一场大捷。

## XVII The Winter Term / 第十七章 冬季学期

===Original===

It was the first day of the winter term.

The Bishop, as he came back by express, could not help feeling that, after all, life considered as an institution had its points. Things had mended steadily during the last weeks of the term. He had kept up his end as head of the House perfectly. The internal affairs of Leicester’s were going as smoothly as oil. And there was the cricket cup to live up to. Nothing pulls a House together more than beating all comers in the field, especially against odds, as Leicester’s had done. And then Monk and Danvers had left. That had set the finishing touch to a good term’s work. The Mob were no longer a power in the land. Waterford remained, but a subdued, benevolent Waterford, with a wonderful respect for law and order. Yes, as far as the House was concerned, Gethryn felt no apprehensions. As regarded the School at large, things were bound to come right in time. A school has very little memory. And in the present case the Bishop, being second man in the Fifteen, had unusual opportunities of righting himself in the eyes of the multitude. In the winter term cricket is forgotten. Football is the only game that counts.

And to round off the whole thing, when he entered his study he found a letter on the table. It was from Farnie, and revealed two curious and interesting facts. Firstly he had left, and Beckford was to know him no more. Secondly﻿—this was even more remarkable﻿—he possessed a conscience.


===Chinese===

这是冬季学期的第一天。

“主教”坐特快列车返校的路上，不由得感到：归根结底，作为一种制度的人生还是有其可取之处的。学期最后几个礼拜，事态稳步向好。他这个舍长当得毫无瑕疵。莱斯特舍的内务顺滑如油。而且还有板球杯的荣光要传承。没有什么比在球场上痛击各路来敌更能凝聚一间宿舍的了——尤其是以弱胜强，莱斯特舍正是这么干的。然后，蒙克和丹弗斯走了。这给一个漂亮学期的业绩画上了圆满句点。“乌合之众”在这块地界上再成不了气候。沃特福德还留下，却是一个收敛了的、和蔼可亲的沃特福德，对法律与秩序怀有可观的敬意。是的，就宿舍而论，盖斯林毫无忧惧。至于全校层面，事情迟早会拨云见日。学校的记性短得很。何况眼下“主教”身为十五人队的二号人物，有的是在大众眼前洗刷自己的机会。冬季学期里，板球会被忘掉。足球才是唯一算数的运动。

而给这一切锦上添花的是：他一进书房，就在桌上发现一封信。信是法尼写来的，披露了两桩耐人寻味的奇事。其一，他走了，贝克福德从此与他缘尽。其二——这更加了不得——他居然有良心。



===Original===

“Dear Gethryn,” ran the letter, “I am writing to tell you my father is sending me to a school in France, so I shall not come back to Beckford. I am sorry about the M.C.C. match, and I enclose the four pounds you lent me. I utterly bar the idea of going to France. It’s beastly, yours truly, R. Farnie.”

The money mentioned was in the shape of a cheque, signed by Farnie senior.

Gethryn was distinctly surprised. That all this time remorse like a worm i’ the bud should have been feeding upon his uncle’s damask cheek, as it were, he had never suspected. His relative’s demeanour since the M.C.C. match had, it is true, been considerably toned down, but this he had attributed to natural causes, not unnatural ones like conscience. As for the four pounds, he had set it down as a bad debt. To get it back was like coming suddenly into an unexpected fortune. He began to think that there must have been some good in Farnie after all, though he was fain to admit that without the aid of a microscope the human eye might well have been excused for failing to detect it.


===Chinese===

“亲爱的盖斯林，”信上写道，“写信告诉你，我父亲要把我送到法国的一所学校去，所以我不回贝克福德了。M.C.C.比赛那件事我很抱歉，随信奉还你借我的四镑钱。我一万个不愿意去法国。糟透了。你真诚的，R. 法尼（R. Farnie）。”

信中提到的那笔钱，是一张支票，由老法尼（Farnie senior）签字。

盖斯林着实吃了一惊。这些日子以来，竟有一只懊悔的蛆虫藏于花苞，日日啃食着他舅舅那张粉面——他从未起过此等疑心。M.C.C.比赛之后，他这位亲戚的做派确实收敛了不少，不过他把这归因于自然原因，而非良心这种反自然原因。至于那四镑，他早记成坏账了。失而复得，如同天上突然掉下一注意外之财。他开始觉得法尼骨子里终究还是有几分可取之处的，尽管他不得不承认：不借助显微镜的话，肉眼没能瞧出来，也是情有可原的。



===Original===

His next thought was that there was nothing now to prevent him telling the whole story to Reece and Marriott. Reece, if anybody, deserved to have his curiosity satisfied. The way in which he had abstained from questions at the time of the episode had been nothing short of magnificent. Reece must certainly be told.

Neither Reece nor Marriott had arrived at the moment. Both were in the habit of returning at the latest possible hour, except at the beginning of the summer term. The Bishop determined to reserve his story until the following evening.

Accordingly, when the study kettle was hissing on the Etna, and Wilson was crouching in front of the fire, making toast in his own inimitable style, he embarked upon his narrative.


===Chinese===

他的下一个念头是：如今再没有什么能拦着他把整个故事讲给里斯和马里奥特听了。要说谁配满足好奇心，那就是里斯。事发当时他那种一个问题都不问的定力，堪称壮丽。必须讲给里斯听。

里斯和马里奥特此刻都还没到。两人都有拖到最后一刻才返校的习惯，夏季学期开学时除外。“主教”决定把故事留到第二天晚上再讲。

于是，等书房的水壶在那座“埃特纳（Etna）”上嘶嘶作响、威尔逊蹲在炉前以他那无可模仿的独特手法烤吐司的时候，他开讲了。



===Original===

“I say, Marriott.”

“Hullo.”

“Do you notice a subtle change in me this term? Does my expressive purple eye gleam more brightly than of yore? It does. Exactly so. I feel awfully bucked up. You know that kid Farnie has left?”

“I thought I missed his merry prattle. What’s happened to him?”


===Chinese===

“我说，马里奥特。”

“喂。”

“你有没有察觉我这个学期发生了微妙的变化？我这对传神的紫眼睛，是不是比往日更加炯炯有神？是的。正是如此。我心里痛快极了。你知道那个叫法尼的小毛孩走了吗？”

“我就说怎么听不见他那愉快的叽叽喳喳了。他怎么了？”



===Original===

“Gone to a school in France somewhere.”

“Jolly for France.”

“Awfully. But the point is that now he’s gone I can tell you about that M.C.C. match affair. I know you want to hear what really did happen that afternoon.”


===Chinese===

“去法国的某个学校了。”

“法国可走运了。”

“走大运了。不过重点在于：他一走，我就能给你讲讲M.C.C.比赛那档子事了。我知道你想听听那天下午到底发生了什么。”



===Original===

Marriott pointed significantly at Wilson, whose back was turned.

“Oh, that’s all right,” said Gethryn. “Wilson.”

“Yes?”

“You mustn’t listen. Try and think you’re a piece of furniture. See? And if you do happen to overhear anything, you needn’t go gassing about it. Follow?”


===Chinese===

马里奥特意味深长地指了指背对这边的威尔逊。

“哦，没关系，”盖斯林说。“威尔逊。”

“嗯？”

“你不许听。你就当自己是件家具，试试看。懂吗？万一碰巧听见点什么，也不许出去瞎传。明白？”



===Original===

“All right,” said Wilson, and Gethryn told his tale.

“Jove,” he said, as he finished, “that’s a relief. It’s something to have got that off my chest. I do bar keeping a secret.”

“But, I say,” said Marriott.

“Well?”


===Chinese===

“好，”威尔逊说。于是盖斯林讲了他的故事。

“天哪，”他讲完时说，“可算松快了。把这事从胸口卸下来，滋味不错。我可真受不了保守秘密。”

“可是，我说，”马里奥特说。

“怎么？”



===Original===

“Well, it was beastly good of you to do it, and that sort of thing, I suppose. I see that all right. But, my dear man, what a rotten thing to do. A kid like that. A little beast who simply cried out for sacking.”

“Well, at any rate, it’s over now. You needn’t jump on me. I acted from the best motives. That’s what my grandfather, Farnie’s pater, you know, always used to say when he got at me for anything in the happy days of my childhood. Don’t sit there looking like a beastly churchwarden, you ass. Buck up, and take an intelligent interest in things.”

“No, but really, Bishop,” said Marriott, “you must treat this seriously. You’ll have to let the other chaps know about it.”

“How? Put it up on the notice-board? This is to certify that Mr. Allan Gethryn, of Leicester’s House, Beckford, is dismissed without a stain on his character. You ass, how can I let them know? I seem to see myself doing the boy-hero style of things. My friends, you wronged me, you wronged me very grievously. But I forgive you. I put up with your cruel scorn. I endured it. I steeled myself against it. And now I forgive you profusely, every one of you. Let us embrace. It wouldn’t do. You must see that much. Don’t be a goat. Is that toast done yet, Wilson?”


===Chinese===

“这么说吧，你肯那么做，够意思，这我认。这层我看得分明。可是，我亲爱的老兄，这事儿干得多么缺德啊。那么点大的孩子。一个小混蛋，简直天生就该被开除。”

“反正都过去了。你也用不着冲我发难。我动机纯良。我外祖父——就是法尼他爸——在我童年那段幸福岁月里，每逢抓着我的错处，总说这句。别坐在那儿摆出一副讨厌的教堂执事脸，你这蠢驴。振作点，对事物拿出点有智力的兴趣来。”

“不，说正经的，主教，”马里奥特说，“这事你得严肃对待。你得让其他弟兄知道真相。”

“怎么让？贴到布告栏上吗？兹证明，贝克福德莱斯特舍之艾伦·盖斯林（Allan Gethryn）先生，清白无瑕，光荣离任。你这蠢货，我怎么让他们知道？我都能想象自己来那套少年英雄戏码了。朋友们，你们冤枉了我，冤枉得苦大仇深。可我原谅你们。我忍受了你们残酷的鄙夷。我扛住了。我硬起心肠扛住了。如今我大度地原谅你们，一个不落。来拥抱吧。这成何体统。这么多你总该明白。别犯蠢。吐司好了没有，威尔逊？”



===Original===

Wilson exhibited several pounds of the article in question.

“Good,” said the Bishop. “You’re a great man, Wilson. You can make a small selection of those biscuits, and if you bag all the sugar ones I’ll slay you, and then you can go quietly downstairs, and rejoin your sorrowing friends. And don’t you go telling them what I’ve been saying.”

“Rather not,” said Wilson.

He made his small selection, and retired. The Bishop turned to Marriott again.


===Chinese===

威尔逊展示了数磅该商品。

“好，”“主教”说。“你是个伟人，威尔逊。那些饼干你可以小小地挑一份，要是敢把撒糖的一网打尽，我就宰了你。然后你就可以悄悄下楼，回到你那些悲恸的朋友中间去。还有，别把我刚才的话学给他们听。”

“才不呢，”威尔逊说。

他小小地挑了一份，退了下去。“主教”重新转向马里奥特。



===Original===

“I shall tell Reece, because he deserves it, and I rather think I shall tell Gosling and Pringle. Nobody else, though. What’s the good of it? Everybody’ll forget the whole thing by next season.”

“How about Norris?” asked Marriott.

“Now there you have touched the spot. I can’t possibly tell Norris myself. My natural pride is too enormous. Descended from a primordial atomic globule, you know, like Pooh Bah. And I shook hands with a duke once. The man Norris and I, I regret to say, had something of a row on the subject last term. We parted with mutual expressions of hate, and haven’t spoken since. What I should like would be for somebody else to tell him all about it. Not you. It would look too much like a put-up job. So don’t you go saying anything. Swear.”

“Why not?”


===Chinese===

“我要告诉里斯，因为他配。戈斯林和普林格尔嘛，我看多半也会告诉。别的人就算了。图什么呢？到下个赛季，谁都会把这事儿忘个精光。”

“那诺里斯呢？”马里奥特问。

“这话可问到点子上了。让我亲自告诉诺里斯是不可能的。我的天赋尊严太过浩瀚。要知道，我家血统是从原始原子小珠一路传下来的，跟扑-巴（Pooh-Bah）似的。我有回还跟一位公爵握过手呢。至于诺里斯那家伙，很遗憾，上学期我们为这事大吵了一场。分别时互致了仇恨，从此再没说过话。我理想中的方案，是让旁人讲给他听。不能是你。那太像串通好的局了。所以你也不许说。发誓。”

“为什么不许？”



===Original===

“Because you mustn’t. Swear. Let me hear you swear by the bones of your ancestors.”

“All right. I call it awful rot, though.”

“Can’t be helped. Painful but necessary. Now I’m going to tell Reece, though I don’t expect he’ll remember anything about it. Reece never remembers anything beyond his last meal.”

“Idiot,” said Marriott after him as the door closed. “I don’t know, though,” he added to himself.

And, pouring himself out another cup of tea, he pondered deeply over the matter.


===Chinese===

“因为不许。发誓。让我听见你拿你祖宗的骨头起誓。”

“行吧。不过我觉得这蠢透了。”

“没法子。痛苦但必要。现在我可要去讲给里斯听了，虽然我料想他八成不记得这茬。里斯的记性，从来超不过上一顿饭。”

“白痴，”门关上时，马里奥特冲着他的背影说。“不过，也难说，”他又自言自语道。

于是他给自己又斟了一杯茶，就这事陷入了深深的思索。



===Original===

Reece heard the news without emotion.

“You’re a good sort, Bishop,” he said, “I knew something of the kind must have happened. It reminds me of a thing that happened to﻿—”

“Yes, it is rather like it, isn’t it?” said the Bishop. “By the way, talking about stories, a chap I met in the holidays told me a ripper. You see, this chap and his brother﻿—”

He discoursed fluently for some twenty minutes. Reece sighed softly, but made no attempt to resume his broken narrative. He was used to this sort of thing.


===Chinese===

里斯听到消息时不动声色。

“你是个好人，主教，”他说。“我早猜到八成是这一类事。这让我想起一桩旧事，发生在——”

“是啊，是挺像的，是不是？”“主教”说。“对了，说起故事，假期里我碰到一个家伙，他讲了个绝的。是这样，这家伙和他弟弟——”

他滔滔不绝地讲了约莫二十分钟。里斯轻轻叹了口气，但没有试图接上那被打断的叙述。这种事他习惯了。



===Original===

It was a fortnight later, and Marriott and the Bishop were once more seated in their study waiting for Wilson to get tea ready. Wilson made toast in the foreground. Marriott was in football clothes, rubbing his shin gently where somebody had kicked it in the scratch game that afternoon. After rubbing for a few moments in silence, he spoke suddenly.

“You must tell Norris,” he said. “It’s all rot.”

“I can’t.”

“Then I shall.”


===Chinese===

两周以后，马里奥特和“主教”又一次坐在书房里等威尔逊备茶。威尔逊在前景里烤吐司。马里奥特一身足球装束，正揉着小腿——那天下午的练习赛里不知谁踢的。默默揉了一阵之后，他突然开了口。

“你必须告诉诺里斯，”他说。“这么下去不是事儿。”

“我不行。”

“那我去。”



===Original===

“No, don’t. You swore you wouldn’t.”

“Well, but look here. I just want to ask you one question. What sort of a time did you have in that scratch game tonight?”

“Beastly. I touched the ball exactly four times. If I wasn’t so awfully ornamental, I don’t see what would be the use of my turning out at all. I’m no practical good to the team.”

“Exactly. That’s just what I wanted to get at. I don’t mean your remark about your being ornamental, but about your never touching the ball. Until you explain matters to Norris, you never will get a decent pass. Norris and you are a rattling good pair of centre threes, but if he never gives you a pass, I don’t see how we can expect to have any combination in the First. It’s no good my slinging out the ball if the centres stick to it like glue directly they get it, and refuse to give it up. It’s simply sickening.”


===Chinese===

“别去。你发过誓不说。”

“好吧，可你听着。我只问你一个问题。今晚那场练习赛，你踢得怎么样？”

“糟透了。我统共碰到球四次。要不是我装饰性特别强，我真看不出我上场还有个什么用。我对这队毫无实用价值。”

“这就对了。我正想引到这儿。我说的不是你那句自我装饰论，而是你摸不着球这事。不把事情跟诺里斯解释清楚，你永远别想接到一记像样的传球。诺里斯和你本是绝佳的一对中路三分卫，可他一个球都不传给你的话，我看咱们一队就别指望有什么配合了。我这边把球往外送，中路那两位一到手就跟抹了胶似的攥死不放，那还有什么搞头。简直呕死人。”



===Original===

Marriott played half for the First Fifteen, and his soul was in the business.

“But, my dear chap,” said Gethryn, “you don’t mean to tell me that a man like Norris would purposely rot up the First’s combination because he happened to have had a row with the other centre. He’s much too decent a fellow.”

“No. I don’t mean that exactly. What he does is this. I’ve watched him. He gets the ball. He runs with it till his man is on him, and then he thinks of passing. You’re backing him up. He sees you, and says to himself, ‘I can’t pass to that cad’﻿—”

“Meaning me?”


===Chinese===

马里奥特在一队打前卫，而且他把灵魂都押在这门事业上。

“可是，亲爱的老兄，”盖斯林说，“你总不是要说，诺里斯那样的人，会因为跟另一个中路三分卫吵了一架，就存心毁掉一队的配合吧。他是个体面得多的家伙。”

“不。倒也不是存心。他其实是这么回事。我观察过。他拿到球，带球带到自己的人贴上来，这才想起传球的事。你在后面策应。他看见你，心里说：‘球不能传给那个无赖——’”

“说我呢？”



===Original===

“Meaning you.”

“Thanks awfully.”

“Don’t mention it. I’m merely quoting his thoughts, as deduced by me. He says, ‘I can’t pass to that﻿—well, individual, if you prefer it. Where’s somebody else?’ So he hesitates, and gets tackled, or else slings the ball wildly out to somebody who can’t possibly get to it. It’s simply infernal. And we play the Nomads tomorrow, too. Something must be done.”

“Somebody ought to tell him. Why doesn’t our genial skipper assert his authority?”


===Chinese===

“说你。”

“多谢了啊。”

“不客气。我不过是转述他的心理活动，由我推理所得。他说：‘我不能传给那个——嗯，换个斯文词儿，那位先生。还有谁在？’于是他一犹豫，被人扑倒；要么就把球胡乱甩给一个根本够不着的人。简直岂有此理。何况咱们明天就要打游牧人队。必须想个办法。”

“总得有人告诉他。咱们那位和蔼的队长怎么不行使一下权威？”



===Original===

“Hill’s a forward, you see, and doesn’t get an opportunity of noticing it. I can’t tell him, of course. I’ve not got my colours﻿—”

“You’re a cert. for them.”

“Hope so. Anyway, I’ve not got them yet, and Norris has, so I can’t very well go slanging him to Hill. Sort of thing rude people would call side.”

“Well, I’ll look out tomorrow, and if it’s as bad as you think, I’ll speak to Hill. It’s a beastly thing to have to do.”

“Beastly,” agreed Marriott. “It’s got to be done, though. We can’t go through the season without any combination in the three-quarter line, just to spare Norris’s feelings.”

“It’s a pity, though,” said the Bishop, “because Norris is a ripping good sort of chap, really. I wish we hadn’t had that bust-up last term.”


===Chinese===

“希尔是前锋，你明白，没机会留意这些。我当然不能说。我的队色还没到手——”

“你是板上钉钉能拿。”

“但愿如此。反正还没到手，而诺里斯有了，所以我没法跑到希尔跟前数落他。这种事，没教养的人管它叫摆架子。”

“行，明天我留意着，要是真像你想的那么糟，我就去跟希尔说。这差事真是脏透了。”

“脏透了，”马里奥特附和。“可非干不可。总不能为了照顾诺里斯的情绪，就带着一条毫无配合的三分卫线打完整个赛季。”

“可惜是真的，”“主教”说，“因为诺里斯实在是个顶呱呱的好人。真希望咱们上学期没闹那一场。”

## XV Versus Charchester (At Charchester) / 第十五章 对阵查彻斯特（在查彻斯特）

===Original===

From the fact that he had left his team so basely in the lurch on the day of an important match, a casual observer might have imagined that Norris did not really care very much whether his House won the cup or not. But this was not the case. In reality the success of Jephson’s was a very important matter to him. A sudden whim had induced him to accept his uncle’s invitation, but now that that acceptance had had such disastrous results, he felt inclined to hire a sturdy menial by the hour to kick him till he felt better. To a person in such a frame of mind there are three methods of consolation. He can commit suicide, he can take to drink, or he can occupy his mind with other matters, and cure himself by fixing his attention steadily on some object, and devoting his whole energies to the acquisition of the same.

Norris chose the last method. On the Saturday week following his performance for Little Bindlebury, the Beckford Eleven was due to journey to Charchester, to play the return match against that school on their opponents’ ground, and Norris resolved that that match should be won. For the next week the team practised assiduously, those members of it who were not playing in House matches spending every afternoon at the nets. The treatment was not without its effect. The team had been a good one before. Now every one of the eleven seemed to be at the very summit of his powers. New and hitherto unsuspected strokes began to be developed, leg glances which recalled the Hove and Ranjitsinhji, late cuts of Palairetical brilliance. In short, all Nature may be said to have smiled, and by the end of the week Norris was beginning to be almost cheerful once more. And then, on the Monday before the match, Samuel Wilberforce Gosling came to school with his right arm in a sling. Norris met him at the School gates, rubbed his eyes to see whether it was not after all some horrid optical illusion, and finally, when the stern truth came home to him, almost swooned with anguish.


===Chinese===

从他在一场重要比赛的日子里如此卑劣地临阵弃队这一点看，随便哪个旁观者都可能以为，诺里斯并不怎么在乎他的宿舍能不能捧杯。但事实并非如此。实际上，杰夫森舍（Jephson's）的胜利对他是一件极要紧的事。一时心血来潮让他接受了舅舅的邀请，可如今那邀请竟招来这般灾难性的后果，他恨不得按小时雇一名壮实的仆役来踢他，踢到他心里痛快为止。对处于这种心境的人，世上有三条安慰之路。他可以自尽，可以酗酒，也可以让脑子被别的事占住，把注意力稳稳钉在某个目标上，把全部精力投入对该目标的夺取，从而实现自愈。

诺里斯选了第三条。他替小宾德尔伯里（Little Bindlebury）出赛之后的第二个星期六，贝克福德十一人队要远征查彻斯特（Charchester），在对方场地上打回访赛，诺里斯打定主意：这场比赛必须赢。接下来一周，全队苦练，那些没有宿舍赛任务的队员天天下午泡在练习网里。这疗法不是没有效果。这支队伍本来就不弱。如今十一个人个个都像站在自己竞技状态的顶点。新的、前所未见的击球招式纷纷开发出来：腿侧轻擦让人想起霍夫（Hove）球场上的兰吉辛吉（Ranjitsinhji），迟切闪着帕莱雷特（Palairet）式的光辉。总之，可以说大自然一片欣欣向荣，到了周末，诺里斯几乎重新快活起来。然后，就在比赛前的星期一，塞缪尔·威尔伯福斯·戈斯林吊着右胳膊来上学了。诺里斯在校门口撞见他，揉了揉眼睛，看这是不是终究只是一场骇人的视错觉；最后，等严酷的真相扎进心里，他险些当场痛晕过去。



===Original===

“What? How? Why?” he enquired lucidly.

The injured Samuel smiled feebly.

“I’m fearfully sorry, Norris,” he said.

“Don’t say you can’t play on Saturday,” moaned Norris.


===Chinese===

“什么？怎么？为什么？”他条理清晰地问道。

受伤的塞缪尔虚弱地笑了笑。

“实在对不住，诺里斯，”他说。

“别告诉我你星期六上不了场，”诺里斯哀鸣道。



===Original===

“Frightfully sorry. I know it’s a bit of a sickener. But I don’t see how I can, really. The doctor says I shan’t be able to play for a couple of weeks.”

Now that the blow had definitely fallen, Norris was sufficiently himself again to be able to enquire into the matter.

“How on earth did you do it? How did it happen?”

Gosling looked guiltier than ever.


===Chinese===

“抱歉透了。我知道这打击不小。可我实在没法子。医生说，两个星期内我都打不了球。”

这一闷棍既然已经实实在在落了下来，诺里斯反倒恢复了镇定，可以着手调查案情了。

“你到底怎么弄的？怎么发生的？”

戈斯林的脸色比刚才更心虚了。



===Original===

“It was on Saturday evening,” he said. “We were ragging about at home a bit, you know, and my young sister wanted me to send her down a few balls. Somebody had given her a composition bean and a bat, and she’s been awfully keen on the game ever since she got them.”

“I think it’s simply sickening the way girls want to do everything we do,” said Norris disgustedly.

Gosling spoke for the defence.


===Chinese===

“是星期六晚上，”他说。“我们在家里闹着玩来着，你知道，我小妹要我给她投几个球。不知谁送了她一只合成球（composition ball）和一块球拍，自打得了这两样，她对板球狂热得不得了。”

“女孩子家什么都要学我们，我看真是恶心透了，”诺里斯嫌恶地说。

戈斯林为被告方做了辩护。



===Original===

“Well, she’s only thirteen. You can’t blame the kid. Seemed to me a jolly healthy symptom. Laudable ambition and that sort of thing.”

“Well?”

“Well, I sent down one or two. She played ’em like a book. Bit inclined to pull. All girls are. So I put in a long hop on the off, and she let go at it like Jessop. She’s got a rattling stroke in mid-on’s direction. Well, the bean came whizzing back rather wide on the right. I doubled across to bring off a beefy c-and-b, and the bally thing took me right on the tips of the fingers. Those composition balls hurt like blazes, I can tell you. Smashed my second finger simply into hash, and I couldn’t grip a ball now to save my life. Much less bowl. I’m awfully sorry. It’s a shocking nuisance.”


===Chinese===

“哎呀，她才十三岁。总怪不着这小家伙吧。要我说，这倒是个健康得不得了的好兆头。可嘉的雄心壮志，等等等等。”

“然后呢？”

“然后，我就投了一两个。她打得有板有眼。就是有点爱拉击（pull）。女孩子都这样。于是我在外侧投了个长跳球（long hop），她照着它抡了出去，活像杰索普（Jessop）二世。她朝投球侧内场（mid-on）方向有一记相当响亮的击球。结果呢，那颗球嗖地飞回来，偏右，偏得老远。我横跨一步，想完成一记肥美的接投杀（caught and bowled），那该死的东西正正砸在我指尖上。这种合成球砸起来疼得冒火，我告诉你。我的二拇指直接被剁成了肉泥，这会儿让我握个球都握不住，更别提投球了。实在抱歉。烦死人了。”



===Original===

Norris agreed with him. It was more than a nuisance. It was a staggerer. Now that Gethryn no longer figured for the First Eleven, Gosling was the School’s one hope. Baynes was good on his wicket, but the wickets he liked were the sea-of-mud variety, and this summer fine weather had set in early and continued. Lorimer was also useful, but not to be mentioned in the same breath as the great Samuel. The former was good, the latter would be good in a year or so. His proper sphere of action was the tail. If the first pair of bowlers could dismiss five good batsmen, Lorimer’s fast, straight deliveries usually accounted for the rest. But there had to be somebody to pave the way for him. He was essentially a change bowler. It is hardly to be wondered at that Norris very soon began to think wistfully of the Bishop, who was just now doing such great things with the ball, wasting his sweetness on the desert air of the House matches. Would it be consistent with his dignity to invite him back into the team? It was a nice point. With some persons there might be a risk. But Gethryn, as he knew perfectly well, was not the sort of fellow to rub in the undeniable fact that the School team could not get along without him. He had half decided to ask him to play against Charchester, when Gosling suggested the very same thing.


===Chinese===

诺里斯深有问题严重性以上之感。这岂止是烦，这是晴天霹雳。如今盖斯林不再为一队出战，戈斯林便是全校独一根救命稻草。贝恩斯在自己的球道上不错，可他中意的球道是泥海型，而今年夏天好天气来得早、持续得久。洛里默也算得力，可跟伟大的塞缪尔相比不足挂齿。前者是已经好，后者是一年半载之后将会好。他合适的用武之地是收拾尾巴。如果第一对投球手能解决掉五名好击球员，洛里默那快速笔直的投球通常能把剩下的包圆。可总得有人替他铺路。他本质上是一名换手投球手。无怪乎诺里斯很快就唉声叹气地想起了“主教”——此人眼下正把球玩得出神入化，却把满身绝技浪费在宿舍比赛的荒漠长空里。请他归队，是否有损自己的尊严？这是个微妙的问题。换某些人，或有风险。可盖斯林，他知之甚深，绝非那种把“校队离了我就转不动”这一无可否认的事实反复揉到你脸上的人。他已基本拿定主意请他出战查彻斯特，恰好此时戈斯林提出了同一个建议。



===Original===

“Why don’t you have Gethryn in again?” he said. “You’ve stood him out against the O.B.s and the Masters. Surely that’s enough. Especially as he’s miles the best bowler in the School.”

“Bar yourself.”

“Not a bit. He can give me points. You take my tip and put him in again.”

“Think he’d play if I put him down? Because, you know, I’m dashed if I’m going to do any grovelling and that sort of thing.”


===Chinese===

“你干嘛不把盖斯林召回来？”他说。“对校友队（O.B.s）和教师队，你已经晾过他了。怎么也够了。何况他是全校最好的投球手，还没得争。”

“除了你以外。”

“什么呀。他能让我几分。听我一句，把他重新写上吧。”

“你觉得我要是把他填上，他会肯打？因为，你知道，要我低声下气去求人那一套，我死也不干。”



===Original===

“Certain to, I should think. Anyhow, it’s worth trying.”

Pringle, on being consulted, gave the same opinion, and Norris was convinced. The list went up that afternoon, and for the first time since the M.C.C. match Gethryn’s name appeared in its usual place.

“Norris is learning wisdom in his old age,” said Marriott to the Bishop, as they walked over to the House that evening.

Leicester’s were in the middle of their semifinal, and looked like winning it.


===Chinese===

“肯定肯，我看。不管怎样，值得一试。”

征询普林格尔，得到的是同一意见，诺里斯被说服了。名单当天下午贴出，M.C.C.比赛之后头一回，盖斯林的名字回到了它惯常的位置上。

“诺里斯老来渐通世故了，”当晚走回宿舍的路上，马里奥特对“主教”说。

莱斯特舍正打半决赛中段，看样子能赢。



===Original===

“I was just wondering what to do about it,” said Gethryn. “What would you do? Play, do you think?”

“Play! My dear man, what else did you propose to do? You weren’t thinking of refusing?”

“I was.”

“But, man! That’s rank treason. If you’re put down to play for the School you must play. There’s no question about it. If Norris knocked you down with one hand and put you up on the board with the other, you’d have to play all the same. You mustn’t have any feelings where the School is concerned. Nobody’s ever refused to play in a first match. It’s one of the things you can’t do. Norris hasn’t given you much of a time lately, I admit. Still, you must lump that. Excuse sermon. I hope it’s done you good.”


===Chinese===

“我正琢磨这事该怎么办呢，”盖斯林说。“换了你怎么办？你说，打不打？”

“打！亲爱的老兄，不然你还想干什么？你总不是想拒绝吧？”

“正是。”

“可是，老兄！那是十足的叛国。点你为校队出战，你就必须出战。这没什么可商量的。哪怕诺里斯一只手把你打翻在地，另一只手把你钉上名单，你也照样得打。在校队的事情上，不许有个人情绪。从来没人拒绝过打第一流比赛。这属于干不得的那类事。诺里斯最近没给过你好脸，我承认。可你也得忍。原谅我布道。希望你有所收获。”



===Original===

“Very well. I’ll play. It’s rather rot, though.”

“No, it’s all right, really. It’s only that you’ve got into a groove. You’re so used to doing the heavy martyr, that the sudden change has knocked you out rather. Come and have an ice before the shop shuts.”

So Gethryn came once more into the team, and travelled down to Charchester with the others. And at this point a painful alternative faces me. I have to choose between truth and inclination. I should like to say that the Bishop eclipsed himself, and broke all previous records in the Charchester match. By the rules of the dramatic, nothing else is possible. But truth, though it crush me, and truth compels me to admit that his performance was in reality distinctly mediocre. One of his weak points as a bowler was that he was at sea when opposed to a left-hander. Many bowlers have this failing. Some strange power seems to compel them to bowl solely on the leg side, and nothing but long hops and full pitches. It was so in the case of Gethryn. Charchester won the toss, and batted first on a perfect wicket. The first pair of batsmen were the captain, a great bat, who had scored seventy-three not out against Beckford in the previous match, and a left-handed fiend. Baynes’s leg-breaks were useless on a wicket which, from the hardness of it, might have been constructed of asphalt, and the rubbish the Bishop rolled up to the left-handed artiste was painful to witness. At four o’clock﻿—the match had started at half-past eleven﻿—the Charchester captain reached his century, and was almost immediately stumped off Baynes. The Bishop bowled the next man first ball, the one bright spot in his afternoon’s performance. Then came another long stand, against which the Beckford bowling raged in vain. At five o’clock, Charchester by that time having made two hundred and forty-one for two wickets, the left-hander ran into three figures, and the captain promptly declared the innings closed. Beckford’s only chance was to play for a draw, and in this they succeeded. When stumps were drawn at a quarter to seven, the score was a hundred and three, and five wickets were down. The Bishop had the satisfaction of being not out with twenty-eight to his credit, but nothing less than a century would have been sufficient to soothe him after his shocking bowling performance. Pringle, who during the luncheon interval had encountered his young friends the Ashbys, and had been duly taunted by them on the subject of leather-hunting, was top scorer with forty-one. Norris, I regret to say, only made three, running himself out in his second over. As the misfortune could not, by any stretch of imagination, be laid at anybody else’s door but his own, he was decidedly savage. The team returned to Beckford rather footsore, very disgusted, and abnormally silent. Norris sulked by himself at one end of the saloon carriage, and the Bishop sulked by himself at the other end, and even Marriott forbore to treat the situation lightly. It was a mournful homecoming. No cheering wildly as the brake drove to the College from Horton, no shouting of the School song in various keys as they passed through the big gates. Simply silence. And except when putting him on to bowl, or taking him off, or moving him in the field, Norris had not spoken a word to the Bishop the whole afternoon.


===Chinese===

“好吧。我打。不过这事实在没劲。”

“不，其实挺好。你只是钻进了牛角尖。你当惯了重装的殉道者，突变之下有点发懵而已。走，趁小店没关门，吃个冰激凌去。”

于是盖斯林重归球队，跟大伙儿一起去了查彻斯特。写到此处，一个痛苦的两难横在我面前。我必须在真相与偏好之间做选择。我很想说“主教”大放异彩，在查彻斯特一战打破了一切既往纪录。按戏剧的规律，别无他途。可真相——尽管它碾碎我——迫使我承认：他的表现实际上平平无奇，还不止是平平。他作为投球手的弱点之一，是一碰上左撇子便六神无主。许多投球手都有这毛病。某种神秘的力量似乎驱使他们把球专往腿侧投，而且只投长跳球和满抛球。盖斯林正是如此。查彻斯特掷币获胜，在一片完美球道上先攻。头一对击球员一个是他们的队长，一位大牌，上一场对贝克福德曾得七十三分不出局；另一个是左手魔王。球道硬得跟沥青浇筑的一般，贝恩斯的腿转球（leg-break）在它上面毫无作为，而“主教”滚向那位左手艺术家的那些垃圾，看得人于心不忍。到四点钟——比赛十一点半开始——查彻斯特队长拿到百分，几乎紧接着就在贝恩斯投球下被三柱门后截杀。“主教”一球解决了下一个人，这是他整个下午唯一的亮点。随后又是漫长的坚守，贝克福德的投球对着它徒劳地咆哮。五点钟，查彻斯特已得二百四十一分失两门，那位左撇子也跨进了三位数，队长当机立断宣布封局。贝克福德唯一的生路是谋一场逼平，这一点他们做到了。六点三刻撤下三柱门收场时，比分为一百零三分，失五门。“主教”有理由心满意足：自己不出局，名下二十八分。但要把他那骇人的投球表现抚平，少于一百分都不够。普林格尔——午餐间歇时碰上了他那几位阿什比家（the Ashbys）的小朋友，被他们就“满场追皮子（leather-hunting，外场捡球的雅称）”一事好生奚落了一番——以四十一分位列头名。诺里斯，很遗憾，只得了三分，第二轮就把自己跑出局了。由于这场灾祸无论怎么牵强，都只能归罪于他自己，他的火气相当之大。全队回到贝克福德时，脚酸，胃胀，且反常地沉默。诺里斯独自在客车包厢一头生闷气，“主教”独自在另一头生闷气，连马里奥特也没敢拿这局面打趣。这是一场凄凉的归程。没有大马车从霍顿（Horton）驶向学院时的疯狂欢呼，没有穿过大门时各种调门的校歌合唱。只有沉默。而且，除了派他上场投球、把他撤下、或在场上调动他的位置之外，整个下午诺里斯没跟“主教”说过一个字。



===Original===

It was shortly after this disaster that Mr. Mortimer Wells came to stay with the Headmaster. Mr. Mortimer Wells was a brilliant and superior young man, who was at some pains to be a cynic. He was an old pupil of the Head’s in the days before he had succeeded to the rule of Beckford. He had the reputation of being a “ripe” scholar, and to him had been deputed the task of judging the poetical outbursts of the bards of the Upper Fifth, with the object of awarding to the most deserving﻿—or, perhaps, to the least undeserving﻿—the handsome prize bequeathed by his openhanded highness, the Rajah of Seltzerpore.

This gentleman sat with his legs stretched beneath the Headmaster’s generous table. Dinner had come to an end, and a cup of coffee, acting in cooperation with several glasses of port and an excellent cigar, had inspired him to hold forth on the subject of poetry prizes. He held forth.

“The poetry prize system,” said he﻿—it is astonishing what nonsense a man, ordinarily intelligent, will talk after dinner﻿—“is on exactly the same principle as those penny-in-the-slot machines that you see at stations. You insert your penny. You set your prize subject. In the former case you hope for wax vestas, and you get butterscotch. In the latter, you hope for something at least readable, and you get the most complete, terrible, uninspired twaddle that was ever written on paper. The boy mind”﻿—here the ash of his cigar fell off on to his waistcoat﻿—“the merely boy mind is incapable of poetry.”

From which speech the shrewd reader will infer that Mr. Mortimer Wells was something of a prig. And perhaps, altogether shrewd reader, you’re right.


===Chinese===

这场灾难后不久，莫蒂默·威尔斯先生（Mr. Mortimer Wells）来校长（Headmaster）家做客。莫蒂默·威尔斯先生是个聪明而居高临下的青年，颇为刻意地以愤世嫉俗者自居。他是校长执掌贝克福德之前的年月里的旧弟子。素有“成熟”学者之誉，上五年级（Upper Fifth）众诗人爆发出的诗作，评审之责便落在他肩上，以便把那份丰厚的奖金颁发给最配的人——或者，发给最不配里最配的那个也行——此奖乃挥金如土的塞尔泽普尔王公（the Rajah of Seltzerpore）陛下遗赠。

这位先生双腿舒展，坐在校长宽大的餐桌下。晚餐已近尾声，一杯咖啡，与数杯波特酒和一支上好雪茄协同作战，激起了他就诗歌奖金问题发表高论的兴致。他发表了高论。

“诗歌奖金制度，”他说——一个平日智力正常的人，饭后能讲出怎样的胡话，实在惊人——“与你在车站上见到的那些投一便士的机器原理一模一样。你塞进你的便士。你设定你的命题。前者，你指望掉出蜡火柴，掉出来的却是奶油太妃糖。后者，你指望至少是能读的东西，得到的却是有史以来写在纸上的最完备、最恐怖、最没有灵气的梦呓。‘男孩的心智’——此时他的雪茄灰落到马甲上——‘纯粹男孩的心智，是写不了诗的。’”

从这番高论，精明的读者自会推断：莫蒂默·威尔斯先生多少是个自命不凡的家伙。而且也许，精明的读者，你全对。



===Original===

Mr. Lawrie, the master of the Sixth, who had been asked to dinner to meet the great man, disagreed as a matter of principle. He was one of those men who will take up a cause from pure love of argument.

“I think you’re wrong, sir. I’m perfectly convinced you’re wrong.”

Mr. Wells smiled in his superior way, as if to say that it was a pity that Mr. Lawrie was so foolish, but that perhaps he could not help it.

“Ah,” he said, “but you have not had to wade through over thirty of these gems in a single week. I have. I can assure you your views would undergo a change if you could go through what I have. Let me read you a selection. If that does not convert you, nothing will. If you will excuse me for a moment, Beckett, I will leave the groaning board, and fetch the manuscripts.”


===Chinese===

六年级主任劳里先生（Mr. Lawrie）——他受邀赴宴，陪这位大人物——出于原则表示反对。他是那种纯粹出于好辩之癖也要接一个话头的角色。

“我认为您错了，先生。我完全确信您错了。”

威尔斯先生带着他那居高临下的微笑，仿佛在说：可惜呀，劳里先生如此愚蠢，不过这或许也不能怪他。

“啊，”他说，“可您没有试着一星期内蹚过三十多篇这样的杰作。我蹚过。我向您保证，您要是经历了我的经历，看法自会改变。让我给您念一篇精选。如果这都不能把您感化，那就没什么能了。失陪片刻，贝克特（Beckett），我这就暂别这座堆盘叠盏的宴席，去取手稿。”



===Original===

He left the room, and returned with a pile of paper, which he deposited in front of him on the table.

“Now,” he said, selecting the topmost manuscript, “I will take no unfair advantage. I will read you the very pick of the bunch. None of the other﻿—er﻿—poems come within a long way of this. It is a case of Eclipse first and the rest nowhere. The author, the gifted author, is a boy of the name of Lorimer, whom I congratulate on taking the Rajah’s prize. I drain this cup of coffee to him. Are you ready? Now, then.”

He cleared his throat.


===Chinese===

他出了屋子，抱回一摞纸，端放在自己面前的桌上。

“现在，”他说，拣出最上面的一份手稿，“我不占任何不公平的便宜。我给你们念的是这堆里的头名状元。其余那些——呃——诗作，连它的边都挨不上。这就叫‘日蚀（Eclipse）第一，其余无名’。作者，那位天才作者，是个姓洛里默的男孩，我祝贺他摘得王公的奖金。我以这杯咖啡敬他。准备好了吗？那么，开始。”

他清了清嗓子。

## XII “We, the Undersigned—” / 第十二章 “我们，签名者如下—”

===Original===

Norris kept his word with regard to the Bishop’s exclusion from the Eleven. The team which had beaten the O.B.s had not had the benefit of his assistance, Lorimer appearing in his stead. Lorimer was a fast right-hand bowler, deadly in House matches or on a very bad wicket. He was the mainstay of the Second Eleven attack, and in an ordinary year would have been certain of his First Eleven cap. This season, however, with Gosling, Baynes, and the Bishop, the School had been unusually strong, and Lorimer had had to wait.

The nonappearance of his name on the notice-board came as no surprise to Gethryn. He had had the advantage of listening to Norris’s views on the subject. But when Marriott grasped the facts of the case, he went to Norris and raved. Norris, as is right and proper in the captain of a School team when the wisdom of his actions is called into question, treated him with no respect whatever.


===Chinese===

关于把“主教”排除出一队一事，诺里斯说到做到。那支打赢了校友队（O.B.s）的队伍不曾沾他的光，替他出场的是洛里默。洛里默是右手快球投手，在宿舍赛或极烂的球道上致命。他是二队进攻的顶梁柱，搁平常年份，一队帽（First Eleven cap）稳落他头上。可这个赛季，有戈斯林、贝恩斯加“主教”坐镇，校队强得反常，洛里默只得等着。

自己的名字没上布告栏，盖斯林并不意外。他已有幸恭听过诺里斯就该问题发表的高见。可马里奥特一摸清案情，便冲到诺里斯面前咆哮。而诺里斯呢，正如一支校队队长在其决策智慧受到质疑时所应有的那样，对他毫无敬意可言。



===Original===

“It’s no good talking,” he said, when Marriott had finished a brisk opening speech, “I know perfectly well what I’m doing.”

“Then there’s no excuse for you at all,” said Marriott. “If you were mad or delirious I could understand it.”

“Come and have an ice,” said Norris.


===Chinese===

“说这些没用，”马里奥特那段干脆利落的开场白结束时，他说，“我自己在干什么，清楚得很。”

“那你更没得辩解了，”马里奥特说。“你要是疯了或说胡话，我倒还能理解。”

“走，吃个冰去，”诺里斯说。



===Original===

“Ice!” snorted Marriott. “What’s the good of standing there babbling about ices! Do you know we haven’t beaten the O.B.s for four years?”

“We shall beat them this year.”

“Not without Gethryn.”

“We certainly shan’t beat them with Gethryn, because he’s not going to play. A chap who chooses the day of the M.C.C. match to go off for the afternoon, and then refuses to explain, can consider himself jolly well chucked until further notice. Feel ready for that ice yet?”


===Chinese===

“冰！”马里奥特嗤之以鼻。“站在那儿絮叨什么冰有什么用！你可知道咱们已经四年没赢过校友队了？”

“今年就能赢。”

“没有盖斯林赢不了。”

“有盖斯林肯定也赢不了，因为他不会上场。一个专挑M.C.C.比赛的日子出去消磨一下午、事后又拒绝解释的家伙，在另行通知之前，完全可视作光荣除名。现在想吃那个冰了吗？”



===Original===

“Don’t be an ass.”

“Well, if ever you do get any ice, take my tip and tie it carefully round your head in a handkerchief. Then perhaps you’ll be able to see why Gethryn isn’t playing against the O.B.s on Saturday.”

And Marriott went off raging, and did not recover until late in the afternoon, when he made eighty-three in an hour for Leicester’s House in a scratch game.


===Chinese===

“别犯蠢。”

“好吧，哪天真让你吃上了冰，听我一句，用手帕仔细裹住，扎在脑袋上。那样你说不定就能看明白，盖斯林为什么星期六不打校友队了。”

马里奥特怒冲冲地走了，直到下午晚些时候才缓过来——他在一场练习赛里替莱斯特舍一小时内拿下八十三分。



===Original===

There were only three of the eleven Houses whose occupants seriously expected to see the House cricket cup on the mantelpiece of their dining-room at the end of the season. These were the School House, Jephson’s, and Leicester’s. In view of Pringle’s sensational feats throughout the term, the knowing ones thought that the cup would go to the School House, with Leicester’s runners-up. The various members of the First Eleven were pretty evenly distributed throughout the three Houses. Leicester’s had Gethryn, Reece, and Marriott. Jephson’s relied on Norris, Bruce, and Baker. The School House trump card was Pringle, with Lorimer and Baynes to do the bowling, and Hill of the First Eleven and Kynaston and Langdale of the second to back him up in the batting department. Both the other First Eleven men were day boys.

The presence of Gosling in any of the House elevens, however weak on paper, would have lent additional interest to the fight for the cup; for in House matches, where every team has more or less of a tail, one really good fast bowler can make a surprising amount of difference to a side.

There was a great deal of interest in the School about the House cup. The keenest of all games at big schools are generally the House matches. When Beckford met Charchester or any of the four schools which it played at cricket and football, keenness reached its highest pitch. But next to these came the House matches.


===Chinese===

十一间宿舍里，真心指望赛季末能看到宿舍板球杯蹲在自家餐厅壁炉台上的，统共三间：学校宿舍（School House）、杰夫森舍、莱斯特舍。鉴于普林格尔整学期惊天动地的战绩，懂行的都认为杯子归校舍，莱斯特舍屈居第二。一队队员在三间宿舍里分布得相当均匀。莱斯特舍有盖斯林、里斯和马里奥特。杰夫森舍指望诺里斯、布鲁斯和贝克。校舍的王牌是普林格尔，投球靠洛里默和贝恩斯，击球一栏由一队的希尔加二队的基纳斯顿（Kynaston）和兰代尔（Langdale）为他压阵。一队另两名队员都是走读生。

不管哪间宿舍的十一人名单里有戈斯林——哪怕纸面上再弱——都能给杯赛平添一份看点；因为宿舍赛里家家都有长短不等的尾巴，一个真正过硬的快球投手，足以让一支队伍脱胎换骨到惊人的程度。

宿舍杯在校内牵动人心。大学校里火药味最浓的比赛，向来是宿舍赛。贝克福德对上查彻斯特，或板球足球各有交手的那四所学校中的任何一所时，火药味到达顶点。而仅次于这些的，就是宿舍赛。



===Original===

Now that he no longer played for the Eleven, the Bishop was able to give his whole mind to training the House team in the way it should go. Exclusion from the First Eleven meant also that he could no longer, unless possessed of an amount of sangfroid so colossal as almost to amount to genius, put in an appearance at the First Eleven net. Under these circumstances Leicester’s net summoned him. Like Mr. Phil May’s lady when she was ejected (with perfect justice) by a barman, he went somewhere where he would be respected. To the House, then, he devoted himself, and scratch games and before-breakfast field-outs became the order of the day.

House fielding before breakfast is one of the things which cannot be classed under the head of the Lighter Side of Cricket. You get up in the small hours, dragged from a comfortable bed by some sportsman who, you feel, carries enthusiasm to a point where it ceases to be a virtue and becomes a nuisance. You get into flannels, and, still half asleep, stagger off to the field, where a hired ruffian hits you up catches which bite like serpents and sting like adders. From time to time he adds insult to injury by shouting “get to ’em!,” “get to ’em!”﻿—a remark which finds but one parallel in the language, the “keep moving” of the football captain. Altogether there are many more pleasant occupations than early morning field-outs, and it requires a considerable amount of keenness to carry the victim through them without hopelessly souring his nature and causing him to foster uncharitable thoughts towards his House captain.


===Chinese===

既然不再为一队出战，“主教”便能一门心思按正道调教宿舍队了。被一队除名还意味着：除非他冷静到近乎天才的境界，否则没法再在一队练习网露面。此情此景之下，莱斯特舍的练习网向他发出了召唤。正像菲尔·梅（Phil May）笔下那位被酒吧侍者（以完全正当的手段）轰出门去的女士，他找了个能受尊重的地方。于是他把身心献给了宿舍，练习赛和早餐前的防守训练成了每日的必修课。

早餐前的宿舍防守训练，属于没法归入“板球轻快面”名下的事物。你在凌晨惨淡的钟点里被人从舒适的被窝里拖起来，动手的是某位运动狂，你觉得此人的热忱已经越过了美德的边界、进入了扰民的领域。你套上法兰绒裤，半睡半醒地挪到场上，一个雇来的壮汉朝天上给你抡高飞球，接起来如蛇咬、如蝰蜇。他还时不时往伤口上撒盐，大喝“上啊！”“上啊！”——这话在英语里只有一个难兄难弟，就是橄榄球队长的“跑起来别停！”。总而言之，比清早防守训练惬意的营生多得是，要把受害者撑过这些课目而不彻底酸化他的性情、不叫他对舍长滋生不厚道的念头，需要相当剂量的热忱。



===Original===

J. Monk of Leicester’s found this increased activity decidedly uncongenial. He had no real patriotism in him. He played cricket well, but he played entirely for himself.

If, for instance, he happened to make fifty in a match﻿—and it happened fairly frequently﻿—he vastly preferred that the rest of the side should make ten between them than that there should be any more half-centuries on the score sheet, even at the expense of losing the match. It was not likely, therefore, that he would take kindly to this mortification of the flesh, the sole object of which was to make everybody as conspicuous as everybody else. Besides, in the matter of fielding he considered that he had nothing to learn, which, as Euclid would say, was absurd. Fielding is one of the things which is never perfect.

Monk, moreover, had another reason for disliking the field-outs. Gethryn, as captain of the House team, was naturally master of the ceremonies, and Monk objected to Gethryn. For this dislike he had solid reasons. About a fortnight after the commencement of term, the Bishop, going downstairs from his study one afternoon, was aware of what appeared to be a species of free fight going on in the doorway of the senior day-room. The senior day-room was where the rowdy element of the House collected, the individuals who were too old to be fags, and too low down in the School to own studies.


===Chinese===

莱斯特舍的J. 蒙克（J. Monk）发现这番加码的活跃实在不合胃口。他体内并无真正的集体荣誉感。他板球打得好，但完完全全是为自己打。

譬如说，他若在一场比赛里得了五十分——这种事发生得相当频繁——他宁愿全队其余人合起来凑十分，也不愿记分纸上再出现半个五十分，哪怕以输掉比赛为代价。因此，指望他心悦诚服地接受这种苦修肉体——其唯一宗旨是让每个人都跟别人一样显眼——是不大可能的。何况，防守一途，他自认再无可学，而正如欧几里得会说的，此论荒谬。防守属于永远无法臻于完美的事物。

此外，蒙克厌恶防守训练还有另一层原因。盖斯林身为舍队队长，自然是操办一切的那位司仪，而蒙克对盖斯林本就有意见。这份嫌恶有其坚实的根据。开学约莫两周后的一天下午，“主教”从书房下楼，察觉高年级活动室的门洞里似乎正进行着某种群殴。高年级活动室是宿舍里吵闹分子的集散地，那帮人年纪太大，当不了跑腿小子，在学校的位次又太低，分不到书房。



===Original===

Under ordinary circumstances the Bishop would probably have passed on without investigating the matter. A head of a house hates above all things to get a name for not minding his own business in unimportant matters. Such a reputation tells against him when he has to put his foot down over big things. To have invaded the senior day-room and stopped a conventional senior day-room “rag” would have been interfering with the most cherished rights of the citizens, the freedom which is the birthright of every Englishman, so to speak.

But as he passed the door which had just shut with a bang behind the free fighters, he heard Monk’s voice inside, and immediately afterwards the voice of Danvers, and he stopped. In the first place, he reasoned within himself, if Monk and Danvers were doing anything, it was probably something wrong, and ought to be stopped. Gethryn always had the feeling that it was his duty to go and see what Monk and Danvers were doing, and tell them they mustn’t. He had a profound belief in their irreclaimable villainy. In the second place, having studies of their own, they had no business to be in the senior day-room at all. It was contrary to the etiquette of the House for a study man to enter the senior day-room, and as a rule the senior day-room resented it. As to all appearances they were not resenting it now, the obvious conclusion was that something was going on which ought to cease.

The Bishop opened the door. Etiquette did not compel the head of the House to knock, the rule being that you knocked only at the doors of those senior to you in the House. He was consequently enabled to witness a tableau which, if warning had been received of his coming, would possibly have broken up before he entered. In the centre of the group was Wilson, leaning over the study table, not so much as if he liked so leaning as because he was held in that position by Danvers. In the background stood Monk, armed with a walking-stick. Round the walls were various ornaments of the senior day-room in attitudes of expectant attention, being evidently content to play the part of “friends and retainers,” leaving the leading parts in the hands of Monk and his colleague.


===Chinese===

换了平常，“主教”多半径直走过，不予查究。一舍之长最怕的，莫过于落个在小事上多管闲事的名声。这样的名声，等他要在大事上踩下脚定时会拖他后腿。闯进高年级活动室、叫停一场高年级活动室约定俗成的“闹腾”，无异于干涉公民最珍视的权利，干涉可以说每个英国人与生俱来的自由。

可他走过那扇刚在自由搏击选手们身后砰然关上的门时，听见了屋里蒙克的声音，紧接着是丹弗斯的声音，于是他停下了。第一，他心中推理道，蒙克和丹弗斯若在干什么，那多半不是好事，应当制止。盖斯林始终觉得，自己有义务去看看蒙克和丹弗斯在干什么，并告知他们不许干。他对这两人不可救药的恶性怀有坚定不移的信念。第二，他们各自有书房，压根不该出现在高年级活动室。书房人士踏入高年级活动室，有违宿舍的礼法，且高年级活动室照例对此深恶痛绝。眼下他们既然毫无憎恶之相，显而易见的结论便是：正在进行的某事，应当立刻停止。

“主教”推开门。礼法并不要求舍长敲门，规矩是只敲宿舍里辈分高过你的人的门。于是他得以目睹这样一幅画面——倘使他们得到他驾到的预警，这画面多半会在他进门之前散场。人群中央是威尔逊，俯在长桌上，与其说他喜欢这么俯着，不如说他是被丹弗斯按在了那个姿势上。背景处站着蒙克，手持一根手杖。沿墙一溜摆着高年级活动室的各色摆设人物，姿态里满是翘首以待，显然乐于出演“朋友与仆从”的角色，把主角让给蒙克及其同事。



===Original===

“Hullo,” said the Bishop, “what’s going on?”

“It’s all right, old chap,” said Monk, grinning genially, “we’re only having an execution.”

“What’s the row?” said the Bishop. “What’s Wilson been doing?”

“Nothing,” broke in that youth, who had wriggled free from Danvers’s clutches. “I haven’t done a thing, Gethryn. These beasts lugged me out of the junior day-room without saying what for or anything.”


===Chinese===

“喂，”“主教”说，“这是唱的哪出？”

“没事没事，老伙计，”蒙克亲切地咧嘴一笑，“我们只是行刑。”

“怎么回事？”“主教”说。“威尔逊干什么了？”

“什么也没干，”那少年挣脱了丹弗斯的钳制，抢着说道。“我什么都没干，盖斯林。这帮畜生把我从低年级活动室里拽出来，也不说为了什么。”



===Original===

The Bishop began to look dangerous. This had all the outward aspect of a case of bullying. Under Reynolds’s leadership Leicester’s had gone in rather extensively for bullying, and the Bishop had waited hungrily for a chance of catching somebody actively engaged in the sport, so that he might drop heavily on that person and make life unpleasant for him.

“Well?” he said, turning to Monk, “let’s have it. What was it all about, and what have you got to do with it?”

Monk began to shuffle.

“Oh, it was nothing much,” he said.


===Chinese===

“主教”的面色开始显得危险。这事从各方面看都像一桩霸凌。雷诺兹治下的莱斯特舍曾把霸凌经营得颇具规模，“主教”一直如饥似渴地等着机会，逮一个现行，好重重落到那人头上，让他日子不好过。

“说吧，”他转向蒙克，“道来。到底怎么回事，跟你又有什么相干？”

蒙克开始支吾。

“哦，也没什么大不了的事，”他说。



===Original===

“Then what are you doing with the stick?” pursued the Bishop relentlessly.

“Young Wilson cheeked Perkins,” said Monk.

Murmurs of approval from the senior day-room. Perkins was one of the ornaments referred to above.

“How?” asked Gethryn.


===Chinese===

“那你拿着手杖干什么？”“主教”穷追不舍。

“小威尔逊冒犯了珀金斯（Perkins），”蒙克说。

高年级活动室里响起一片赞许的低语。珀金斯就是前文所述摆设人物之一。

“怎么冒犯的？”盖斯林问。



===Original===

Wilson dashed into the conversation again.

“Perkins told me to go and get him some grub from the shop. I was doing some work, so I couldn’t. Besides, I’m not his fag. If Perkins wants to go for me, why doesn’t he do it himself, and not get about a hundred fellows to help him?”

“Exactly,” said the Bishop. “A very sensible suggestion. Perkins, fall upon Wilson and slay him. I’ll see fair play. Go ahead.”

“Er﻿—no,” said Perkins uneasily. He was a small, weedy-looking youth, not built for fighting except by proxy, and he remembered the episode of Wilson and Skinner.


===Chinese===

威尔逊再次抢进对话。

“珀金斯叫我去小卖部给他弄点吃的。我当时正做题，去不了。再说了，我又不是他的跑腿。珀金斯要是想收拾我，干嘛不自己动手，非要纠集一百来号帮手？”

“正是，”“主教”说。“一条非常明智的建议。珀金斯，扑向威尔逊，消灭他。我保证公平竞赛。上吧。”

“呃——不了，”珀金斯不安地说。他是个瘦小枯干的少年，体格生来不适合打架，除非打代理人战争，何况他还记得威尔逊和斯金纳（Skinner）那段公案。



===Original===

“Then the thing’s finished,” said Gethryn. “Wilson walks over. We needn’t detain you, Wilson.”

Wilson departed with all the honours of war, and the Bishop turned to Monk.

“Now perhaps you’ll tell me,” he said, “what the deuce you and Danvers are doing here?”

“Well, hang it all, old chap﻿—”

The Bishop begged that Monk would not call him “old chap.”


===Chinese===

“那么此事了结，”盖斯林说。“威尔逊完胜全场。就不留你了，威尔逊。”

威尔逊带着战争的全副荣光撤离，“主教”转向蒙克。

“现在，你或许肯告诉我，”他说，“你和丹弗斯在这屋里究竟搞什么名堂？”

“咳，我说，老伙计——”

“主教”敬告蒙克，请勿称呼他“老伙计”。



===Original===

“I’ll call you ‘sir,’ if you like,” said Monk.

A gleam of hope appeared in the Bishop’s eye. Monk was going to give him the opportunity he had long sighed for. In cold blood he could attack no one, not even Monk, but if he was going to be rude, that altered matters.

“What business have you in the day-room?” he said. “You’ve got studies of your own.”

“If it comes to that,” said Monk, “so have you. We’ve got as much business here as you. What the deuce are you doing here?”

Taken by itself, taken neat, as it were, this repartee might have been insufficient to act as a *casus belli*, but by a merciful dispensation of Providence the senior day-room elected to laugh at the remark, and to laugh loudly. Monk also laughed. Not, however, for long. The next moment the Bishop had darted in, knocked his feet from under him, and dragged him to the door. Captain Kettle himself could not have done it more neatly.


===Chinese===

“您愿意的话，我管您叫‘长官’好了，”蒙克说。

“主教”眼中闪起一线希望。蒙克即将送给他渴求已久的机会。冷静状态下他无从出手，连对蒙克也无从出手，可对方若是无礼，性质就变了。

“你们在活动室里有什么公干？”他说。“你们自己有书房。”

“要那么说，”蒙克道，“你也有。我们在这儿跟你一样名正言顺。你在这儿搞什么名堂？”

单看这一句——如此说来是纯饮、不加冰的——它或许还不足充当开战理由（*casus belli*），但蒙上天仁慈的安排，高年级活动室众人认定这话可笑，而且笑得响亮。蒙克自己也笑了。只是没笑多久。下一瞬间，“主教”已欺身而入，挑翻他的双脚，把他拖到门口。凯特尔船长（Captain Kettle）亲临，也不过如此利落。



===Original===

“Now,” said the Bishop, “we can discuss the point.”

Monk got up, looking greener than usual, and began to dust his clothes.

“Don’t talk rot,” he said, “I can’t fight a prefect.”

This, of course, the Bishop had known all along. What he had intended to do if Monk had kept up his end he had not decided when he embarked upon the engagement. The head of a House cannot fight by-battles with his inferiors without the loss of a good deal of his painfully acquired dignity. But Gethryn knew Monk, and he had felt justified in risking it. He improved the shining hour with an excursus on the subject of bullying, dispensed a few general threats, and left the room.


===Chinese===

“好了，”“主教”说，“现在咱们可以讨论讨论这个问题了。”

蒙克爬起来，脸色比平日更绿，开始掸衣服。

“别讲浑话，”他说，“我不能跟级长打架。”

这一点，当然是“主教”从头到尾都清楚的。倘若蒙克真敢接招，他打算怎么办——交上手之际他也没拿定。一舍之长与辈分低者打遭遇战，难免折损辛苦积攒的威严。但盖斯林了解蒙克，他觉得这险冒得值。他趁热打铁，就霸凌问题发表了一篇专题论述，抛下几句泛泛的威胁，离室而去。



===Original===

Monk had﻿—perhaps not unnaturally﻿—not forgotten the incident, and now that public opinion ran strongly against Gethryn on account of his M.C.C. match manoeuvres, he acted. A mass meeting of the Mob was called in his study, and it was unanimously voted that field-outs in the morning were undesirable, and that it would be judicious if the team were to strike. Now, as the Mob included in their numbers eight of the House Eleven, their opinions on the subject carried weight.

“Look here,” said Waterford, struck with a brilliant idea, “I tell you what we’ll do. Let’s sign a round-robin refusing to play in the House matches unless Gethryn resigns the captaincy and the field-outs stop.”

“We may as well sign in alphabetical order,” said Monk prudently. “It’ll make it safer.”

The idea took the Mob’s fancy. The round-robin was drawn up and signed.


===Chinese===

蒙克——或许也不算不合常情——没忘掉这段过节。如今舆论因M.C.C.比赛那番操作而对盖斯林大为不利，他出手了。“乌合之众”在他的书房里召开了群众大会，一致表决：早晨的防守训练要不得；舍队若能罢训，方为明智。而“乌合之众”的编制里有八名舍队队员，他们就此问题的意见颇有分量。

“听着，”沃特福德灵光一闪，“我说咱们这么办。签一封联署书（round-robin），声明除非盖斯林交出队长职务、防守训练停办，否则拒绝参加宿舍赛。”

“咱们不妨按姓氏字母顺序签，”蒙克谨慎地说。“保险一些。”

这主意很对“乌合之众”的胃口。联名信起草完毕，签字完成。



===Original===

“Now, if we could only get Reece,” suggested Danvers. “It’s no good asking Marriott, but Reece might sign.”

“Let’s have a shot at any rate,” said Monk.

And a deputation, consisting of Danvers, Waterford, and Monk, duly waited upon Reece in his study, and broached the project to him.


===Chinese===

“现在，要是能把里斯拉进来就好了，”丹弗斯提议。“马里奥特就别指望了，里斯倒可能签。”

“不管怎样，试一试，”蒙克说。

于是，一支由丹弗斯、沃特福德和蒙克组成的代表团，如期到里斯书房觐见，向他抛出了这个计划。
